# inside and not what a stranger would say.
#
# TWO TRIGGERS AND NEITHER IS THE OTHER'S OPPOSITE. This is the important part.
# A man is not scored on one axis from sinful to virtuous - the counts are two
# independent tallies over the same trait list, and a man with three sins and
# three virtues answers yes to both, while a man with a mild temperament and no
# strong traits at all answers no to both. Writing one trigger and negating it
# for the second moment would have been wrong and would have quietly made the
# two templates mutually exclusive when they are not.
#
# THE THRESHOLD IS TWO, NOT ONE, ON BOTH SIDES. One sinful trait is most adult
# characters in the game under most faiths, and a gate that fires for nearly
# everybody is not a gate. Two is a man his septon has a settled opinion about.
#
# BOTH ARE ROOT-RELATIVE and both are used bare, on the commander in one
# template and on the knight in the other - they read the scoped character and
# nothing else, so there is no walk and no guard to get wrong.
bc_his_own_faith_counts_against_him_trigger = {
	num_sinful_traits >= 2
}

bc_his_own_faith_counts_for_him_trigger = {
	num_virtuous_traits >= 2
}


# --- what he was raised to be ------------------------------------------------
#
# `has_education_*` APPEARS ZERO TIMES IN THIS MOD. Ninety-eight commander
# gates and sixty-six knight gates read what a man can do - prowess, martial,
# learning, the trait list, the illness flag, the count of bad genetic traits -
# and not one of them has ever read what somebody spent his childhood trying to
# make him into. Education is the single largest statement the engine makes
# about a character's intended shape and the chronicle has never opened it.
#
# BOTH WRAP AGOT'S OWN TRIGGERS RATHER THAN LISTING TRAITS, for exactly the
# reason bc_sworn_to_the_wall_trigger wraps agot_is_member_of_nights_watch:
# AGOT overrides 00_scripted_triggers.txt and adds a FIFTH tier to every
# education line (00_scripted_triggers.txt:65 and :85 are five-arm ORs, not the
# vanilla four). A hand-written `OR` over education_learning_1 through _4 here
# would silently miss every maester-taught lord in the game with the top rank.
#
# TWO TRIGGERS AND THEY ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE BY CONSTRUCTION, which is the
# opposite situation from batch eighty's two counts. A character has exactly
# one education trait, so no man ever answers yes to both of these, and the two
# templates can never both be live for the same person - though they roll in
# different pools anyway, so it would not have mattered if they could.
#
# NEITHER IS A NEGATIVE GATE. "Not raised for war" would have been the obvious
# way to write the first one and would have been wrong: it fires for most of
# the peerage, and a gate that fires for nearly everybody is not a gate. Naming
# the education he did get is both narrower and more specific about the man.
bc_he_was_raised_for_books_trigger = {
	has_education_learning_trigger = yes
}

bc_he_was_raised_for_the_dark_trigger = {
	has_education_intrigue_trigger = yes
}


# --- how many banners are standing on this side ------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER ASKED HOW MANY LORDS ARE ON ITS OWN SIDE. Every side-level
# question it has - bc_side_is_winning_trigger, bc_side_is_outnumbered_trigger,
# bc_host_is_vast_trigger, bc_field_is_bloody_trigger - treats the side as one
# undifferentiated quantity of men with one man at the top of it. A host in
# this setting is a stack of banners, and the man the battle is named after is
# not the only man on the field giving orders.
#
# `any_side_commander` IS THE ENGINE'S OWN LIST AND ITS DOCUMENTATION IS THE
# WHOLE REASON THESE EXIST: "the commanders of every army on the side, not just
# the one leading the battle". That is a count of independent hosts, which is
# to say a count of lords who each brought men and each expect to be consulted.
# Nothing in this mod had ever read it.
#
# BOTH ARE WRITTEN ON `scope:combat_side` AND ARE THEREFORE ROOT-INDEPENDENT,
# the same shape as bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger and bc_host_is_vast_trigger.
# They ask about the side and not about the man asking, so a commander and a
# knight standing on the same side get the same answer, which is correct - the
# number of banners is a fact about the afternoon rather than about either of
# them. `scope:combat_side` needs no `exists` guard; it is the scope the phase
# event is handed.
#
# THREE AND ONE, WITH THE GAP LEFT DELIBERATELY EMPTY. Two banners is the
# ordinary case - a lord and one of his vassals - and neither moment is about
# the ordinary case. Three is a council; one is a man with nobody to send to.
# The two gates are disjoint by construction rather than by side row, which is
# the opposite arrangement from batch seventy-nine.
bc_the_host_is_many_banners_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_commander = { count >= 3 }
	}
}

bc_the_host_is_one_banner_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_commander = { count = 1 }
	}
}


# --- the sword itself --------------------------------------------------------
#
# ARTIFACTS APPEAR NOWHERE IN THIS MOD. A hundred commander gates and
# sixty-eight knight gates read men, ground, weather, numbers, wars, faiths and
# births, and not one of them has ever asked what is in anybody's hand. In this
# setting there are a few hundred swords that are worth more than the men
# carrying them and each one has a name, and the chronicle has fought two
# hundred moments without noticing a single one of them.
#
# `agot_is_valyrian_steel_artifact` IS AGOT'S OWN TEST AND IT IS READ ON AN
# ARTIFACT, NOT ON A CHARACTER (00_agot_artifact_triggers.txt:44 - it is
# `has_variable = valyrian_steel`, a variable AGOT maintains on the item). Both
# wrappers below therefore spell an artifact iteration around it, the same way
# bc_he_is_free_folk_trigger spells `culture = { ... }` around a culture test.
#
# `any_equipped_character_artifact`, NOT `any_character_artifact`. The engine
# offers both and the difference is the whole point: a named sword in a vault
# at home is not on this field. The equipped list is what a man actually has
# with him.
#
# TWO TRIGGERS, ONE ROOT-RELATIVE AND ONE A GUARDED WALK, and they are the same
# fact from the two ends of it. The first is used bare and reads whichever man
# the template is about; the second reaches across to the man opposite and
# takes the standing guard on `enemy_side.side_commander`, which is an optional
# link. Neither contains the other and both can be true at once, which is a
# perfectly ordinary afternoon in this setting.
#
# THE SECOND USES `enemy_side` AND NOT `var:bc_battle_foe`, for the reason the
# rest of the file uses it: the variable is written by bc_battle_open_effect
# inside a moment's effect and does not exist during a battle's first is_valid.
bc_he_carries_valyrian_steel_trigger = {
	any_equipped_character_artifact = {
		agot_is_valyrian_steel_artifact = yes
	}
}

bc_the_foe_carries_valyrian_steel_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			any_equipped_character_artifact = {
				agot_is_valyrian_steel_artifact = yes
			}
		}
	}
}


# --- what has already been taken off him -------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS TWO KINDS OF BODY AND NEITHER OF THEM IS THIS ONE.
# bc_he_is_unwell_trigger reads `has_trait_with_flag = illness` - a fever, a
# thing that arrived and may leave. bc_his_body_was_dealt_to_him_trigger reads
# `num_of_bad_genetic_traits` - a thing that was there before he was. Between
# those two sits the largest category of damaged men in this setting by a very
# long way: the ones who walked onto a field whole and did not walk off it that
# way. Nothing in this chronicle had ever asked.
#
# BOTH READ THE TRAIT DIRECTLY AND NOT AGOT'S APPEARANCE WRAPPERS, and that is
# a deliberate departure from the rest of this file. `agot_has_any_eye_patch_
# trigger` (00_agot_appearance_triggers.txt:404) is `has_trait = one_eyed` AND
# one of a dozen cosmetic flags naming which patch the portrait wears;
# `agot_has_visible_disfigurement_trigger` (:358) is a set of inactive
# portrait traits. Both are questions about how a man is DRAWN. A one-eyed
# lord whose portrait was never assigned a patch flag answers no to AGOT's
# wrapper and is still a one-eyed lord, and it is the eye and not the patch
# that these two moments are about.
#
# NEITHER TRAIT CARRIES THE illness FLAG AND NEITHER IS GENETIC - both are
# `category = health` and nothing else (00_traits.txt:6472 and :6551) - so
# these gates are disjoint from both of the mod's existing body triggers by
# construction rather than by intent.
#
# TWO TRIGGERS AND NEITHER CONTAINS THE OTHER. They are separate traits: a man
# can lose an eye cleanly and keep his face, or keep both eyes and lose most of
# the rest of it, or have both, and the engine tracks all three.
bc_he_lost_an_eye_trigger = {
	has_trait = one_eyed
}

bc_his_face_was_taken_trigger = {
	has_trait = disfigured
}


# --- whose name this is ------------------------------------------------------
#
# THIS MOD HAS ASKED ABOUT HOUSES EXACTLY ONCE AND ONLY ACROSS THE LINE.
# bc_foe_is_house_trigger compares his house to the enemy commander's, which is
# a question about the man opposite. Nothing in eighty-four batches has ever
# asked where he stands INSIDE the name he carries, which in this setting is
# the question that organises most of a man's life. A Frey is not a fact about
# a man; being the Frey, or being one of the ninety who are not, is.
#
# `is_house_head` DOES NOT APPEAR IN THE 1.19.0.6 SCRIPT DUMP even though
# vanilla script uses it (00_available_for_events_triggers.txt:884), so this
# file will not spend the mod's zero-error baseline on it. Both triggers below
# use the target form instead - `this = house.house_head` - which is the idiom
# vanilla itself reaches for at 00_activity_triggers.txt:643 and which the
# dump does document, house_head being a listed event target out of
# dynasty_house. Same answer, and it is an answer the validator can check.
#
# BOTH HOUSE LINKS ARE LEAD-LINED for the reason recorded on
# bc_foe_is_house_trigger: a lowborn character has no house at all, CK3 does
# not short-circuit, and the second trigger walks two links deep. `exists`
# sits as a sibling; every READ is nested in a trigger_if whose limit repeats
# the exists.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND NOT BY LUCK. The second contains an
# explicit NOT on the first, so a house head can never answer yes to it, and
# they never both fire on the same man.
bc_the_name_is_his_trigger = {
	exists = house
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = house }
		exists = house.house_head
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = house.house_head }
			this = house.house_head
		}
	}
}

# He carries the name and the man who IS the name is standing on this field.
#
# `any_side_commander` ON HIS OWN SIDE, not enemy_side: this is about serving
# under him, not facing him. The iteration is the same one batch eighty-two
# used to count banners, asked a different question - not how many lords are
# here, but whether one particular lord is.
#
# `root`, NOT `prev`, inside the entered scope, for the reason recorded on
# bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger.
bc_he_serves_his_own_name_trigger = {
	exists = house
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = house }
		exists = house.house_head
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = house.house_head }
			NOT = { this = house.house_head }
			scope:combat_side = {
				any_side_commander = {
					this = root.house.house_head
				}
			}
		}
	}
}


# --- what his own world has already convicted him of -------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS WHAT MEN THINK OF HIM AND HAS NEVER READ WHAT HE DID. `dread`
# appears twenty-two times in this file and its neighbours, and dread is a
# feeling in other people that decays if he stops feeding it. Nothing here has
# ever asked what is actually written against his name. In a setting where the
# guest right is the single most load-bearing custom in the whole world, that
# is a strange gap.
#
# `tyranny` IS NOT `dread` AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE ENTIRE POINT. Dread is how
# frightened of him his vassals are; tyranny is the sum of what he has taken
# without a right to it - imprisonments without cause, revocations, executions
# that no court would have handed down. A beloved lord can be dreaded by
# accident of reputation, and a man can be at ease in every hall in his realm
# with a tyranny score that would cost him half his vassals if it were ever
# read aloud. The trigger is documented on character with the full comparison
# set. TWENTY-FIVE, not one: a lord who has revoked a single county in a
# lifetime is every lord, and a gate that fires for nearly everybody is not a
# gate.
bc_his_own_people_have_a_ledger_trigger = {
	tyranny >= 25
}

# The oldest crime this setting has.
#
# THE OR IS SPELLED OUT AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. `kinslayer` is a trait GROUP
# with three members (00_traits.txt:12151, :12210, :12276) and both vanilla and
# AGOT write `has_trait = kinslayer` against the group name in places
# (00_dynast_interactions.txt:333, 00_agot_order_interactions.txt:521). It
# works. It is also a name that is not a trait, and this mod does not spend its
# zero-error baseline on keywords that resolve by convention; the accolade
# files in both games spell the three out (04_ep2_eminent_attributes.txt:570)
# and so does this.
#
# NO LEVEL TEST. The engine grades this crime and the grading is about who was
# killed, not how many; a man who killed one brother and a man who killed a
# father and two sons are both standing in a rank today having done the thing
# nobody in this world comes back from, and the moment is about the floor and
# not the depth.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_his_own_faith_counts_against_him_trigger, which counts sins
# against a doctrine and changes its mind when he changes faith. This one is
# not a religious verdict at all - the Seven, the old gods and the drowned god
# agree about it, and so does every hedge knight who has never thought about a
# god in his life.
bc_he_killed_his_own_blood_trigger = {
	OR = {
		has_trait = kinslayer_1
		has_trait = kinslayer_2
		has_trait = kinslayer_3
	}
}


# --- the crown that is not on him yet ----------------------------------------
#
# AGOT SHIPS A WHOLE CORONATION SYSTEM AND THIS MOD HAS NEVER LOOKED AT IT.
# `agot_is_uncoronated_trigger` (00_agot_coronation_triggers.txt:25) and its
# opposite at :8 are the state of a king who has been proclaimed and not yet
# crowned - the gap between the shouting and the septon, which in this setting
# is measured in years and wars rather than in weeks. Eighty-six batches have
# read who a man is, what he believes, who he is related to and what he has
# taken, and not one has read whether the thing he is calling himself has been
# made official by anybody.
#
# BOTH GATES CARRY `agot_ruler_requires_coronation` AND THAT IS NOT DECORATION.
# Read the AGOT trigger: when the coronation system is off it falls through to
# `trigger_else = { has_realm_law = uncrowned }`, and asking a landed knight or
# a count about a kingdom-tier realm law is a question with no meaningful
# answer. The requirement trigger (:1) is kingdom tier or higher, independent,
# landed and feudal, so pairing them means the gate only ever fires on the men
# the system is actually about.
bc_the_crown_is_not_on_him_yet_trigger = {
	agot_ruler_requires_coronation = yes
	agot_is_uncoronated_trigger = yes
}

# He is in a rank, and the man this side belongs to has not been crowned.
#
# THE SAME QUESTION ASKED FROM THE OTHER END OF THE ARMY. `side_commander` is
# an optional link and is guarded the way every optional link in this file is
# guarded - `exists` as a sibling, the read nested in a trigger_if whose limit
# repeats it.
#
# `side_commander` AND NOT `enemy_side.side_commander`: this is about the man
# he is standing FOR. The mod has eight triggers that reach across the line and
# this is the second that reaches up his own side, after
# bc_he_serves_his_own_name_trigger.
bc_he_stands_for_an_uncrowned_man_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			agot_ruler_requires_coronation = yes
			agot_is_uncoronated_trigger = yes
		}
	}
}


# --- the marriage --------------------------------------------------------
#
# `is_married`, `any_spouse` AND `num_of_spouses` ALL RETURNED ZERO OVER THE
# WHOLE MOD. Eighty-seven batches have read a man's blood, his house, his
# heirs, his education, his faith, his body and his crimes, and not one of them
# has read the single arrangement that most of those things exist to produce.
# In a setting where a wedding is a treaty and a treaty is a wedding, the
# chronicle has fought two hundred moments without once noticing that the man
# in front of it is married.
#
# THE FIRST GATE IS NOT `is_married = yes`. That is nearly every landed man
# over twenty in the game and a gate that fires for nearly everybody is not a
# gate. What it asks is whether the marriage and the battle are the same
# object: is the man commanding the other side close family to the woman he
# is married to.
#
# `is_close_family_of` RATHER THAN A HOUSE COMPARISON, and that is the sharper
# question by a long way. A house test would catch every third cousin of a
# great house and there are hundreds of those; close family is her father, her
# mother, her brother, her sister, her child. The mod already uses this trigger
# in twelve places, all of them about the man himself - this is the first time
# it is asked about somebody who is not on the field at all.
#
# THE ENEMY COMMANDER IS AN OPTIONAL LINK and is guarded the way every optional
# link in this file is guarded. `enemy_side` rather than `var:bc_battle_foe`,
# because the variable is written inside a moment's effect and does not exist
# during a battle's first is_valid.
bc_he_married_into_the_other_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		any_spouse = {
			is_close_family_of = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

# There is a child coming and it does not know about today.
#
# THE `OR` IS NOT REDUNDANT. This mod's knight pool is drawn from whoever the
# game hands it, and AGOT hands it women often enough to matter - Mormonts,
# wildlings, the odd Dornishwoman with a spear. `any_spouse = { is_pregnant }`
# catches the man waiting on news and misses the woman carrying it; the second
# half catches her. Both are the same moment from inside two different bodies
# and the toast is written to be true of either.
#
# NO `is_married` COMPANION. A pregnancy implies the arrangement that produced
# it as far as this gate is concerned, and the spouse iteration cannot return
# anything if there is no spouse, so the extra row would only be a second way
# of asking a question already answered.
bc_a_child_of_his_is_on_the_way_trigger = {
	OR = {
		is_pregnant = yes
		any_spouse = { is_pregnant = yes }
	}
}


# --- the paper that put them here --------------------------------------------
#
# `is_allied_to` AND `is_allied_in_war` BOTH RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD.
# Eighty-eight batches have read blood, house, faith, tongue, education, body,
# crime, crown and marriage - every way one man can be bound to another except
# the one that is written down and signed. Half the hosts in this setting are
# two or three realms standing next to each other because of a document, and
# the chronicle has never once asked whether the man forty yards away is here
# for the same reason he is.
#
# `is_allied_to` RATHER THAN `is_allied_in_war`. The narrower trigger asks
# whether two men are on the same side of a specific war, which every commander
# on a combat side already is by definition - it would be a gate that fires for
# everybody. The broader one asks whether there is a standing arrangement
# between them, which is the question worth asking: not are you here with me,
# but is there a reason you would be here with me tomorrow.
#
# THE `NOT` IS LOAD-BEARING. Without it, a side with exactly one commander on
# it would test the man against himself, and this mod does not gamble on what
# the engine thinks a man's alliance with himself is.
#
# `root`, NOT `prev`, inside the entered scope, for the reason recorded on
# bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger.
bc_an_ally_stands_on_this_side_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_commander = {
			NOT = { this = root }
			is_allied_to = root
		}
	}
}

# He is in a rank because of a sentence in a document he has never seen.
#
# THE SAME ARRANGEMENT READ FROM THE BOTTOM. His own lord is not the man this
# host belongs to, and his own lord and that man have paper between them, which
# is the entire explanation for why he is standing in this county at all.
#
# BOTH LINKS ARE GUARDED and the guard has to hold twice, because `liege` is
# optional - an independent lord has none - and `side_commander` is optional
# for the reason recorded throughout this file.
#
# `liege` APPEARS TWICE AND MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS, which is worth writing
# down before somebody tidies it. The first is a comparison - is my lord that
# man - and the second enters his scope to ask a question about him. CK3 reads
# `= <target>` and `= { ... }` differently and both are correct here.
bc_he_is_here_on_paper_trigger = {
	exists = liege
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = liege }
		exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
			NOT = { liege = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
			liege = {
				is_allied_to = scope:combat_side.side_commander
			}
		}
	}
}


# --- owned and held are two different questions ------------------------------
#
# `county_controller` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. bc_the_ground_is_his_
# own_trigger walks location -> county -> holder and asks who this county
# BELONGS to, which is a question about a document in a room somewhere. The
# engine also tracks who is standing on it with soldiers, which during a war is
# very often somebody else, and which is the only one of the two questions that
# has any effect on where a man can get water this afternoon. Eighty-nine
# batches read the deed and never once read the occupation.
#
# THE FIRST GATE IS DISJOINT FROM bc_the_ground_is_his_own_trigger BY
# CONSTRUCTION rather than by luck. That trigger requires the HOLDER to be him
# or his liege-or-above; this one requires that the holder is NOT, and that the
# CONTROLLER is. They cannot both be true of the same man on the same field.
#
# THE REALM TEST IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, for the reason recorded on the older
# trigger: a county held by his own vassal is his in every sense a battle cares
# about, and a county controlled by his own vassal's garrison is under his
# hand. `NOR` on the holder side is the exact negation of the older trigger's
# `OR`, which is what makes the disjointness structural.
#
# THREE OPTIONAL LINKS, THREE GUARDS. `location` is the standing one every
# field trigger carries; the holder guard exists because AGOT has real unheld
# land beyond the Wall and in the ruins; the controller guard exists because
# unheld land has nobody standing on it either.
bc_the_ground_is_held_not_owned_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county.holder
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county.holder }
			scope:combat_side.combat.location.county.holder = {
				NOR = {
					this = root
					target_is_liege_or_above = root
				}
			}
		}
		exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county_controller
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county_controller }
			scope:combat_side.combat.location.county_controller = {
				OR = {
					this = root
					target_is_liege_or_above = root
				}
			}
		}
	}
}

# This county belongs to the men on the other side.
#
# THE ONLY GROUND TRIGGER IN THE MOD THAT DOES NOT READ `root`, which is why it
# is the only one that can be used from the knight pool. The older ownership
# trigger asks whether the county is the COMMANDER's and would answer about a
# knight if it were ever called from a knight template, meaning something much
# narrower; this one compares the county's holder against the man on the far
# side and gives the same answer no matter which of his own men is asking.
#
# `enemy_side` rather than `var:bc_battle_foe`, because the variable is written
# inside a moment's effect and does not exist during a battle's first is_valid.
bc_the_ground_belongs_to_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
			exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county.holder
			trigger_if = {
				limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county.holder }
				scope:combat_side.combat.location.county.holder = {
					OR = {
						this = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
						target_is_liege_or_above = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
					}
				}
			}
		}
	}
}


# --- what was chosen for him, and what he chose ------------------------------
#
# `has_lifestyle` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. Batch eighty-one read
# education, which is a thing that was decided about a boy of eight by adults
# who had their own reasons and which is fixed by sixteen. A lifestyle is the
# opposite: it is what a grown man with his own household spends his own hours
# on, year after year, and the engine tracks it separately for exactly that
# reason. Nothing here had ever asked what he chose.
#
# NEITHER GATE READS A LIFESTYLE ON ITS OWN, and that is the whole design.
# `has_lifestyle = martial_lifestyle` on a commander would fire for a very
# large share of the men who command anything, and a gate that fires for nearly
# everybody is not a gate. What is worth reading is the DISAGREEMENT between
# the two: the man who was pointed at a thing and walked somewhere else, and
# the man who was pointed away from a thing and walked back to it.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND ON BOTH ROWS. One requires a martial
# education and no martial lifestyle; the other requires no martial education
# and a martial lifestyle. Each row of one is the exact negation of the
# corresponding row of the other, so no man can answer yes to both.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE BATCH EIGHTY-ONE GATES. Those read a learning
# education and an intrigue education with nothing else attached. The first of
# these excludes both by requiring a martial education; the second includes
# both and also stewardship and diplomacy, and then narrows all four by a row
# they never had.
#
# `has_education_martial_trigger` IS VANILLA'S (00_scripted_triggers.txt:45),
# the same wrapper batch eighty-one used for the learning and intrigue
# branches, and it is level-agnostic - a martial education of any grade counts,
# because the question is what the boy was pointed at and not how well it took.
bc_he_was_pointed_at_war_and_left_trigger = {
	has_education_martial_trigger = yes
	NOT = { has_lifestyle = martial_lifestyle }
}

bc_he_was_pointed_elsewhere_and_came_back_trigger = {
	NOT = { has_education_martial_trigger = yes }
	has_lifestyle = martial_lifestyle
}


# --- the household, as opposed to the levy ------------------------------------
#
# `has_court_position` AND EVERY COURT-POSITION ITERATOR RETURNED ZERO OVER THE
# WHOLE MOD. Ninety-one batches read blood, oath, land, faith, tongue, crime,
# upbringing and wages-by-the-company, and never once read the plainest
# arrangement a hall contains: a man who is on somebody's payroll and lives in
# his rooms. That is neither vassalage nor kinship nor a mercenary contract -
# it is employment, it is renewed rather than inherited, and it puts a specific
# and recognisable kind of man in a battle line.
#
# `any_court_position_employer` RATHER THAN `has_court_position`. The latter
# needs a position key, and a hand-written list of keys would go stale the
# moment AGOT adds a position - AGOT ships its own set on top of vanilla's. The
# iterator asks the question the moments actually care about, which is not
# WHICH office but WHOSE, and it needs no key at all. Vanilla precedent for
# this exact form is events/dlc/ep2/wedding_events/ep2_wedding_events.txt:18540
# (`any_court_position_employer = { this = root }`); AGOT uses the same
# iterator at common/script_values/00_agot_nightswatch_values.txt:26.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger. That trigger reads
# `is_close_family_of`, which is blood and marriage; a hired huntsman is not
# close family of the man who hires him, and a brother in the knight list is
# not on anybody's payroll. The two gates overlap only on the rare man who is
# both, and neither can be reached from the other by dropping a row.
#
# NO `exists` GUARD ON THE FIRST AND NONE IS NEEDED, for the reason recorded on
# bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger: `any_side_knight` is an ITERATOR, not a link,
# and a side with no knights iterates nothing and answers false.
#
# `root`, NOT `prev` OR `this`, INSIDE THE TWO NESTED ITERATIONS. Inside
# `any_side_knight` the current scope is the knight and inside
# `any_court_position_employer` it is the employer; root is still the commander
# the phase event is rolling for. Same rule and same reason as the note on
# bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger.
#
# THE COMMANDER MAY BE IN HIS OWN KNIGHT LIST and it does not matter here
# either: a man cannot hold a court position under himself, so an unguarded
# iteration cannot match on him.
bc_a_man_off_his_own_payroll_is_here_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			any_court_position_employer = { this = root }
		}
	}
}

# The knight's end of the same arrangement. The link IS optional here - a side
# can be leaderless - so this one pays for it with the standard guard.
bc_the_man_who_pays_him_commands_here_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		any_court_position_employer = {
			this = scope:combat_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}


# --- the one he buried -------------------------------------------------------
#
# `any_former_spouse` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. Batch eighty-eight read
# the marriage a man is currently inside - a wife on the far side, a child on
# the way - and stopped there. The engine also keeps the marriages that ENDED,
# and in Westeros the overwhelming majority of them ended the one way, which
# means this list is mostly a list of the dead.
#
# `is_alive = no` IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IS NOT OPTIONAL. A former spouse can
# also be a living annulment or a woman sent back to her father, and neither of
# those is the fact these two moments are built on. The row keeps the iterator
# to the burials.
#
# `is_married` IS DOING REAL WORK HERE, WHICH IT COULD NOT DO ALONE. Batch
# eighty-eight's header records why a bare `is_married = yes` is not a gate: it
# is nearly every landed man in the game. Sitting beside a burial it stops
# being a census line and becomes the only question that matters about it -
# whether he filled the place again or left it empty - and the two gates split
# the widowed on exactly that row.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One requires `is_married = no` and the
# other `is_married = yes`, over the identical burial row, so no man can answer
# yes to both.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE BATCH EIGHTY-EIGHT GATES. That batch's first gate
# requires a living spouse related to the enemy commander, which the first of
# these excludes outright by requiring no spouse at all, and which the second
# neither implies nor is implied by. Its second gate is a pregnancy, which is a
# fact about a household that is growing rather than one that lost somebody.
bc_he_buried_her_and_left_the_place_empty_trigger = {
	is_married = no
	any_former_spouse = { is_alive = no }
}

bc_he_buried_her_and_filled_the_place_trigger = {
	is_married = yes
	any_former_spouse = { is_alive = no }
}


# --- the paper that is still good --------------------------------------------
#
# `has_truce` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. Ninety-three batches read what
# two men are to each other by blood, faith, tongue, oath, land and wages, and
# never once read the one thing they may have actually signed. A truce is the
# only bond in the game that is a DOCUMENT: it has a term, it was agreed to by
# both of them in a specific year over a specific defeat, and it is still in
# force this afternoon.
#
# IT IS NOT A CONTRADICTION FOR THIS TO FIRE. A truce stops a man DECLARING a
# war on the other; it does nothing about a war a third man declared and called
# them both into on opposite sides. That is the whole moment: neither of them
# chose today, both of them are keeping the letter of the thing that is in a
# chest at home, and they are on a field together anyway.
#
# ONE TRIGGER, BOTH POOLS. It reads the scoped character against the far side's
# commander and never touches `root`, which is exactly the property that makes
# bc_the_ground_belongs_to_the_far_side_trigger usable from the knight file. A
# commander asks it about himself; a knight asks it about himself. There is no
# second trigger here because there would be nothing different in it.
#
# THE STANDARD GUARD. `enemy_side.side_commander` is an optional link - a side
# can be leaderless - so the read sits inside a trigger_if whose limit repeats
# the exists, per the mod-wide rule.
#
# `enemy_side`, NOT `var:bc_battle_foe`. The variable is written by
# bc_battle_open_effect inside a moment's effect and does not exist during a
# battle's first is_valid. Same reason as every other far-side gate.
#
# THE DIRECTION IS THE SCOPED MAN'S. The API is explicit that truces are one
# way - the question is whether the SCOPED character cannot attack the target -
# so this asks whether the paper binds him, not whether it binds the other man.
# That is the correct direction for both moments: each is about a man keeping
# something he agreed to.
bc_the_truce_still_holds_across_the_field_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_truce = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}


# --- what the noise on the far side means -------------------------------------
#
# EVERY LANGUAGE KEYWORD RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. Ninety-four batches
# read culture six ways - the same culture, the westerosi/essosi split, the
# wildling flag, the sea between them - and never once read the layer
# underneath culture, which is the one that decides whether a man can
# UNDERSTAND the field he is standing on. AGOT ships twenty-six languages of
# its own in common/culture/pillars/00_agot_language.txt.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE CULTURE GATES AND THE TWO AXES GENUINELY CROSS.
# Two Westerosi cultures that share nothing else share language_agot_westeros,
# so bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger can be false while the tongue is shared; and
# language_agot_first_men and language_agot_old_ghis exist, so two men can be
# on the same side of bc_foe_is_from_across_the_sea_trigger and still not have
# a word in common. That crossing is the whole reason this layer is worth
# reading separately.
#
# `knows_court_language_of` RATHER THAN `knows_language` OR
# `knows_language_of_culture`. The first needs a language key and a
# hand-written list would rot the first time AGOT added one. The second reads a
# culture's language, which is a fact about a people rather than about the two
# men. `knows_court_language_of` asks the question a battle actually poses -
# whether THIS man can follow the tongue THAT hall gives its orders in - and it
# takes a character, so it needs no key at all. Vanilla precedent is
# common/character_interactions/00_alliance.txt:659 and :745, both with a
# scope argument; AGOT overrides that file and keeps both lines.
#
# COURT LANGUAGE IS THE RIGHT ONE FOR A BATTLE. A landless knight's court
# language is the language of the hall he belongs to, which is exactly the
# language the orders around him are shouted in. A trigger about his personal
# education would be a different and much less useful question.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One requires that he does not know the
# far side's court language and the other that he does, so no man can answer
# yes to both. The second then adds the culture row, which makes it disjoint
# from bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger as well.
#
# THE STANDARD GUARD ON BOTH, and `enemy_side` rather than `var:bc_battle_foe`,
# for the reasons recorded on every other far-side gate in this file.
#
# THE SECOND READS `root.culture` FROM INSIDE AN ENTERED SCOPE, which is the
# same form and the same reason as bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger: inside
# `scope:...side_commander = { }` the current scope is the far commander, and
# root is still the man the phase event is rolling for. That is the man whose
# culture the question is about in BOTH pools - the commander in one file, the
# knight in the other - so the trigger is correct from either.
bc_the_far_side_is_only_noise_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		NOT = {
			knows_court_language_of = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

bc_he_can_hear_what_they_are_saying_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		knows_court_language_of = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		NOT = {
			scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = { culture = root.culture }
		}
	}
}


# --- the fostering -----------------------------------------------------------
#
# `has_relation_guardian` AND `has_relation_ward` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE
# MOD. Ninety-five batches read blood, house, oath, wages, tongue, land and
# paper, and never once read the arrangement Westeros actually runs on. A
# fostering is not kinship and it is not vassalage: it is a boy sent to another
# man's hall for ten years to be made into something, and the engine keeps both
# ends of it as a named relation for exactly as long as both men live.
#
# THE DIRECTION IS EVERYTHING AND IS THE WHOLE REASON THERE ARE TWO. Vanilla
# stores the pair as inverses - `has_relation_ward = X` means X was raised by
# the scoped man, `has_relation_guardian = X` means the scoped man was raised
# by X - so the two gates below are the same fact read from opposite ends, and
# the moments built on them have nothing in common except a hall thirty years
# ago.
#
# EACH CARRIES THE NEGATION OF THE OTHER, so they are disjoint by construction
# rather than by argument. Ordinary play cannot make two men each other's ward,
# but the engine would answer both if some future content ever set both
# relations, and a gate that can be true beside its own opposite is not a gate.
#
# THE STANDARD GUARD ON BOTH. `enemy_side.side_commander` is an optional link,
# and `enemy_side` rather than `var:bc_battle_foe` for the reason recorded on
# every other far-side gate: the variable does not exist during a battle's
# first is_valid.
#
# NEITHER READS `root`, so either is usable from either pool. The two templates
# this batch ships put them in opposite files on purpose.
bc_the_boy_he_raised_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_ward = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		NOT = {
			has_relation_guardian = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

bc_the_man_who_raised_him_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_guardian = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		NOT = {
			has_relation_ward = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}


# --- which way this war travelled -------------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS READ THE WAR SCOPE THREE TIMES AND NEVER ASKED WHICH WAY IT WENT.
# bc_war_is_long_trigger asks how old the quarrel is, bc_war_is_a_rebellion_trigger
# asks what kind of quarrel it is, and bc_the_war_is_his_trigger asks whether
# this man is the one who will sign whatever gets signed. Not one of the three
# asks the first thing anybody asks about a war, which is who came to whom.
# `is_attacker_in_war` and `is_defender_in_war` have both returned zero over
# ninety-six batches.
#
# THESE ARE CHARACTER TRIGGERS WITH A WAR TARGET, NOT WAR TRIGGERS. The
# 1.19.0.6 dump has them as "is the scope character in the target war as an
# attacker" and the defender equivalent, supported scope character, supported
# target war. So the shape is the opposite of bc_the_war_is_his_trigger, which
# walks to the war and compares a character back against it: these are read on
# the man and handed the war. AGOT precedent for the pair is
# common/character_interactions/00_alliance.txt:1007 and :3811.
#
# THE WALK IS STILL bc_war_is_long_trigger'S, EXACTLY. A combat can exist
# without a war behind it - the engine's own link is optional - so the `exists`
# sits as a sibling and the read is nested in a trigger_if that repeats it.
# Being the target rather than the scope does not make an absent link safe to
# hand to a trigger, and this mod does not gamble on how the engine feels about
# being given nothing.
#
# THEY ARE DISJOINT WITHOUT AN EXPLICIT NOT, WHICH IS UNUSUAL HERE AND WORTH
# SAYING OUT LOUD. The last three batches each carried the negation of the
# paired gate, because in every one of those cases the engine would cheerfully
# answer yes twice. A war is not that shape. The attacker list and the defender
# list are the two halves of one object, and being written into one is the
# thing that keeps a man out of the other, so no NOT is added here and none is
# missing.
#
# THE THIRD ANSWER IS NO TO BOTH, AND IT IS THE COMMON ONE. A man who is not a
# named party - a courtier, a household knight, a hired sword, a son riding
# with his father - is in neither list while standing in the exact middle of
# the battle. That silence is correct rather than a hole, and it is also what
# keeps these two off the "fires for nearly everybody" pile: in the knight pool
# especially, the gate picks out the men whose own name is on the quarrel, not
# the far larger number who arrived with somebody who has one.
#
# THEY ARE NOT THE side_primary_participant FAMILY, WHICH IS THE NEAR MISS
# WORTH NAMING. bc_fighting_his_lords_war_trigger, bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger
# and the two hook gates all compare a man against the WAR LEADER OF HIS OWN
# SIDE, which is a question about who is answerable for the quarrel rather than
# which way it travelled. A vassal called into his liege's invasion answers no
# to bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger and yes to bc_he_is_the_one_who_crossed_trigger;
# a lord defending his own seat answers yes to the first and no to the second.
# Neither gate here is a subset of any of those and neither reuses an argument.
#
# ROOT IS THE MAN THE TEMPLATE IS ABOUT in either pool, so either gate is
# usable from either, for the reason recorded on bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger.
bc_he_is_the_one_who_crossed_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war }
		is_attacker_in_war = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	}
}

bc_the_war_came_to_him_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war }
		is_defender_in_war = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	}
}


# --- what he is carrying that nobody here can see ---------------------------
#
# NINETY-SEVEN BATCHES AND THE MOD HAS NEVER OPENED A SECRET. It reads what a
# man has taken openly (tyranny), what somebody has on him (has_hook), what
# people think of him (opinion), what he is related to and married into and
# raised by. Every one of those is a fact somebody else could tell you. The
# engine also keeps a list of the facts nobody can, and `any_secret` and
# `any_known_secret` have both returned zero over the whole mod.
#
# TWO ITERATIONS, NOT A LINK, SO NO GUARD. `any_secret` is a list on the
# character and `any_secret_knower` is a list on the secret; the mod's variable
# guard covers optional LINKS - side_commander, combat.location, house, liege -
# and iterators are not links. bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger and
# bc_he_has_another_war_trigger are unguarded for the same reason.
#
# THE SECOND GATE CARRIES THE EXACT NEGATION OF THE FIRST, plus the row that
# says he has something to be silent about at all. So they are disjoint by
# construction, and a man with two secrets - one out and one not - answers yes
# to the first and no to the second, which is the correct answer: once anybody
# knows anything, the afternoon in the second template is not the one he is
# having.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE HOOK AXIS. bc_his_lord_holds_a_hook_trigger and
# bc_he_holds_his_lords_hook_trigger are about leverage between two men who are
# both attached to this battle, and a hook is a thing being USED. These two
# never look at anybody on the field. The knower may be a septon three kingdoms
# away who has never once thought about doing anything with it, and that is the
# whole difference: a hook is a transaction and a secret is a condition.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_his_own_people_have_a_ledger_trigger'S TERRITORY EITHER.
# Tyranny is the sum of what a man did in the open and was not stopped from
# doing - it is on the record and the record is the point. A secret is the
# other pile.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. Most characters in the game carry no
# secret at all; the ones who do are the ones something happened to.
#
# ROOT-AGNOSTIC. Nothing here reads a property only a commander has and nothing
# walks to the side, so either pool may call either gate.
bc_somebody_out_there_knows_trigger = {
	any_secret = {
		any_secret_knower = { count >= 1 }
	}
}

bc_nobody_alive_knows_it_trigger = {
	any_secret = { count >= 1 }
	NOT = {
		any_secret = {
			any_secret_knower = { count >= 1 }
		}
	}
}


# --- weight, as opposed to rank ---------------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS READ RANK EIGHT TIMES AND WEIGHT NEVER. `highest_held_title_tier`
# and `is_landed` carry the whole station batch, and both of them describe what
# a man HOLDS. Neither says anything about what he is worth to the man above
# him, and in a realm those are different numbers: a duke can be one of six men
# a king cannot govern without, or he can be a duke. `is_powerful_vassal` and
# `is_powerful_vassal_of` had both returned zero over ninety-eight batches.
#
# THE ENGINE'S OWN DEFINITION IS DELIBERATELY NOT RESTATED HERE. `is_powerful_vassal`
# is documented on character as "Is the character a powerful vassal?" and the
# threshold behind it belongs to the engine and to whatever a rule set does to
# it. Writing a realm-fraction of our own would be this mod inventing a
# constitution.
#
# THE FIRST GATE IS AN ITERATION AND NEEDS NO GUARD, for the reason recorded on
# bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger: `any_side_knight` is a list, not a link. It
# uses `root` inside the entered scope for the same reason that trigger does -
# once a scope is entered, `this` is the knight.
#
# THE SECOND GATE CARRIES AN EXPLICIT NOT AND THAT NOT IS THE POINT OF IT. A
# powerful vassal standing in his own liege's line is an ordinary and rather
# dull fact - it is what powerful vassals are for. The moment is the other one:
# a great man of somebody else's realm in a line commanded by a man who has no
# hold on him whatsoever. Without the NOT the second gate would be the first
# gate's subject read from the wrong end, and the two would be the same batch
# written twice.
#
# THE GUARD IS THE STANDING ONE. `side_commander` is optional, the `exists` sits
# as a sibling, and the read - including the read inside the NOT - is nested in
# a trigger_if that repeats it. A NOT around an unguarded link read is still an
# unguarded link read.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE HOOK AXIS. bc_his_lord_holds_a_hook_trigger and
# bc_he_holds_his_lords_hook_trigger are a specific piece of leverage between
# two named men, held by one and usable once. This is structural: nobody did
# anything to anybody, no transaction exists, and the weight would be exactly
# the same if the two men had never met.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. Powerful vassals are a handful per realm
# by construction, and the second gate then removes the common case.
bc_one_of_his_great_men_is_in_the_ranks_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			is_powerful_vassal_of = root
		}
	}
}

bc_he_answers_to_somebody_else_entirely_trigger = {
	is_powerful_vassal = yes
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		NOT = { is_powerful_vassal_of = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
	}
}


# --- the third relation ------------------------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS TWO OF THE THREE AND HAS NEVER READ THE THIRD. `has_relation_friend`
# gates bc_knight_for_a_friend, `has_relation_rival` gates bc_knight_knows_that_man
# and the whole rivalry seam, and in ninety-nine batches `has_relation_lover` has
# never appeared once. The mod reads marriage, kin, house, fostering, wardship,
# employment and wages - every bond in this setting that somebody wrote down -
# and the one the engine keeps that nobody writes down was still missing.
#
# BOTH RELATION READS ALSO POINT SOMEWHERE THE MOD HAS NEVER POINTED ONE. Every
# `has_relation_*` in the file is aimed at the side commander or the enemy's
# side commander. The second gate below aims one INTO THE RANKS, at whoever
# happens to be standing in the same line, which is where a bond like this
# would actually be found.
#
# THE FIRST GATE TAKES THE STANDING GUARD. `enemy_side.side_commander` is an
# optional chained link. The second gate is an iteration and takes none, for
# the reason recorded on bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger, and uses `root` inside
# the entered scope for the same reason that one does.
#
# THE SECOND GATE CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION, rather than
# repeating its body. Both could otherwise be true of one man on one afternoon
# - the engine allows more than one such relation - and the two templates below
# are about opposite arrangements of the same field. Written this way, the man
# whose bond is across the line is never also the man whose bond is beside him,
# and the first gate wins that tie because it is the larger fact.
#
# NEITHER GATE IS `is_married`. bc_he_married_into_the_other_side_trigger is a
# treaty between two families and can exist between people who have met four
# times; this is the engine's other list entirely, it is nobody's arrangement,
# and it appears and disappears without a single document changing hands.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. The relation is uncommon to begin with
# and both gates then require the other person to be standing on this
# particular field on this particular afternoon.
bc_the_one_he_loves_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_lover = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_the_one_he_loves_is_in_this_line_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			has_relation_lover = root
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_the_one_he_loves_commands_the_far_side_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the quarrel that is waiting at home -------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER ONCE ASKED WHAT IS HAPPENING BEHIND THE MAN. A hundred
# batches of gates read this field, this war, this line, this body, this bond
# and this ledger. They read who he is standing beside and who he is looking
# at across the grass. `is_a_faction_member` and `has_targeting_faction` are
# both at zero occurrences across the whole mod, and between them they are the
# only two questions the engine will answer about a quarrel that is not on
# this field at all and does not care what happens here.
#
# THE TWO ARE OPPOSITE ENDS OF ONE ROPE. The first man has a faction pointed
# at him - somebody at home has written his name at the top of a page and gone
# round collecting signatures under it. The second man has put his own name on
# somebody else's page. Neither fact is visible from ten feet away on a
# battlefield, and both of them are the reason a particular afternoon has to
# end a particular way for a particular man.
#
# THE FIRST GATE CARRIES THE SECOND AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION, rather than the
# two being left to overlap. A man can perfectly well be both at once -
# signing against his liege while his own vassals sign against him is not a
# rare arrangement in this setting - and the two templates below are about
# opposite pressures, so the pair is written so that the man being conspired
# against is never also the man conspiring. `is_a_faction_member` wins that tie
# because a signature he put there with his own hand is the nearer fact.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are plain reads on `root`, which is the character
# the template is already rooted on, and there is no optional link in either.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_his_lord_holds_a_hook_trigger, WHICH IS THE NEAR MISS WORTH
# NAMING. A hook is one man's private leverage over another and it is used or
# it is not; a faction is a list of names with a stated demand attached, and
# it exists whether or not anybody ever acts on it.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. Factions are a minority state in any
# given realm at any given moment, and the first gate additionally requires
# that somebody has gone to the trouble of aiming one at this specific man.
bc_there_is_a_faction_pointed_at_him_trigger = {
	has_targeting_faction = yes
	NOT = { is_a_faction_member = yes }
}

bc_he_is_sworn_against_somebody_trigger = {
	is_a_faction_member = yes
}


# --- who is allowed to put whom in a cell ------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS EVERY REASON TWO MEN COULD HAVE TO WANT EACH OTHER DEAD AND HAS
# NEVER READ THE ONE THAT SAYS ONE OF THEM IS ALLOWED TO KEEP THE OTHER. Rivalry,
# kin, house, faith, hooks, tyranny, opinion, kinslaying - the file is full of
# private grievance and public reputation, and `has_imprisonment_reason` was at
# zero occurrences. It is the only trigger in the engine that answers a question
# about LAW between these two particular men, and the answer changes what a
# battle is for: a field you can win is not the same field as one where the man
# opposite is a thing you are entitled to carry home and hold.
#
# THE TWO GATES POINT THE SAME LAW IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS. The first asks
# whether root may lawfully take the man commanding the far side. The second
# asks whether that man may lawfully take root. The engine keeps these as two
# separate facts and the mod has never asked either one.
#
# BOTH TAKE THE STANDING GUARD. `enemy_side.side_commander` is an optional
# chained link, so each gate carries its own `exists` sibling and repeats it in
# the `trigger_if` limit before reading through it. The second gate reads the
# far commander's own reasons from inside his scope and therefore uses `root`,
# not `prev`, for the reason recorded on bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION. Both can be true at once
# and often are - the reasons men accumulate against each other tend to be
# mutual - and the two templates below are about opposite afternoons, one where
# the law is an opportunity and one where it is a mouth. Written this way, the
# man who may take is never also the man who may be taken, and taking wins the
# tie because it is the half that decides what the whole field is doing.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_foe_is_formidable_trigger OR THE HOOK GATES, WHICH ARE THE NEAR
# MISSES. A hook is leverage that gets spent in a conversation; formidability is
# an estimate of what the far side can do to you this afternoon. This is neither.
# It is a standing entitlement that exists on paper before anybody drew a sword
# and will still exist tomorrow whichever way the field goes.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. An imprisonment reason has to be earned,
# by one specific man against one specific other, and both gates then require
# that man to be commanding the far side of this particular field today.
bc_the_law_would_let_him_take_that_man_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_imprisonment_reason = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_the_far_side_could_take_him_lawfully_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			has_imprisonment_reason = root
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_the_law_would_let_him_take_that_man_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the one who was preferred -----------------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS BLOOD AND HAS NEVER READ PREFERENCE. It knows who is kin, whose
# house is whose, who fostered whom, who is a bastard, who stands to inherit and
# who has a child on the way. Every one of those is a fact a herald could look
# up. `num_of_relation_favorite_child` and `is_favorite_child` were both at zero
# occurrences, and between them they are the only place the engine records the
# thing families actually run on, which is that out of several people entitled
# to the same amount of love, one of them gets more of it.
#
# THE TWO GATES ARE THE SAME FACT FROM THE TWO ENDS. The first asks whether root
# is the one doing the preferring. The second asks whether root is the one being
# preferred. A man can be both - most fathers were somebody's son - which is why
# the second carries the first as an explicit negation and the two are disjoint
# by construction. Doing the preferring wins that tie, because a man who is
# owed something and also owes something is, on a battlefield, mostly the second
# thing.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. `num_of_relation_favorite_child` and `is_favorite_child`
# are both plain reads on root, which is the character the template is already
# rooted on, and there is no optional link in either.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_he_stands_to_inherit_trigger OR bc_a_child_of_his_is_on_the_way_trigger,
# WHICH ARE THE NEAR MISSES WORTH NAMING. Inheritance is an order of names on a
# roll and does not care who anybody likes; a child on the way is a fact about
# next spring. This is neither an order nor a date. It is a standing arrangement
# of somebody's affection that everybody in the household knows about and nobody
# has ever written anywhere.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. The relation has to be deliberately set,
# it is set for at most one person at a time by anybody who has it, and most
# characters in the world are on neither end of one.
bc_there_is_one_of_them_he_prefers_trigger = {
	num_of_relation_favorite_child >= 1
}

bc_he_is_the_one_who_was_preferred_trigger = {
	is_favorite_child = yes
	NOT = { bc_there_is_one_of_them_he_prefers_trigger = yes }
}


# --- whose oath is the one that broke ----------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD ASKS SEVEN QUESTIONS ABOUT THE WAR AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS ABOUT THE
# OATH. It knows whose war this is, which way it travelled, how long it has run,
# whether the man has another one, whether a truce holds across the field and
# who the primary participants are. All seven are questions about a conflict.
# `is_at_war_with_liege` was at zero occurrences, and it is the only trigger in
# the engine that answers the different question underneath all of them, which
# is whether the man standing here is in the field against the person he swore
# to, and is therefore in a position no amount of winning ever entirely undoes.
#
# THE TWO GATES SEPARATE THE MAN WHO BROKE IT FROM THE MAN WHO WAS BROUGHT.
# The first is in open rebellion himself. The second is not - his own oath is
# intact and he has kept every part of it - and the lord he swore it to is the
# one in rebellion, which is how he comes to be standing on this field with
# absolutely nothing of his own at stake in the quarrel. Both are on the same
# side of the same battle and the two afternoons have nothing in common.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION AND IS THEREFORE
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. A man can be in rebellion against his liege while
# his liege is in rebellion against his - the middle of a three-deep chain
# coming apart is exactly where that happens - and the first gate wins the tie,
# because a man who has broken his own oath is not usefully described by
# somebody else's.
#
# THE SECOND TAKES THE STANDING GUARD ON `liege`, which is an optional link:
# `exists` as a sibling, repeated in the `trigger_if` limit before anything
# reads through it. The leading `NOT` is a plain read on root and needs no
# guard, which is why it can sit outside.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_fighting_his_lords_war_trigger OR bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger,
# WHICH ARE THE NEAR MISSES. Those two ask who OWNS the war, one of them off
# `is_war_leader` inside the war scope, and both are perfectly satisfied by a
# lord fighting a neighbour on his liege's behalf. These two ask who the war is
# AGAINST, and
# only ever return yes when the answer is the man at the top of your own chain.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. Open rebellion is rare and brief in any
# given realm, and the second additionally requires the man to have a liege who
# is in one while he is not.
bc_he_has_broken_with_his_own_lord_trigger = {
	is_at_war_with_liege = yes
}

bc_the_man_above_him_broke_first_trigger = {
	NOT = { bc_he_has_broken_with_his_own_lord_trigger = yes }
	exists = liege
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = liege }
		liege = { is_at_war_with_liege = yes }
	}
}


# --- who made whom -----------------------------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS THE TRAIT AND HAS NEVER READ THE RELATION. `has_trait = squire`
# gates bc_knight_the_squires_best_day and bc_knight_what_he_agreed_to, and both
# of them know that a man is somebody's squire without knowing whose. AGOT keeps
# the answer in its own scripted relation, `agot_squire` / `agot_knight`, set in
# 00_agot_knighthood_effects.txt and 00_agot_character_history_effects.txt when
# a boy is placed with a knight, and it was at zero occurrences in this mod. It
# is the only record in either game of the one relationship in this setting that
# is entirely a matter of craft: not blood, not law, not affection, just a man
# who knew how to do this and a man he taught it to.
#
# THE DIRECTION IS FIXED BY AGOT AND IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE. The squire is the
# character who takes `set_relation_agot_knight = $KNIGHT$`, so on any root,
# `has_relation_agot_knight = X` reads "X is the man who made me", and the
# reverse read is done by pointing that same trigger at root from inside
# somebody else's scope. Both gates below use it in one of those two ways and
# neither uses `has_relation_agot_squire`.
#
# THE FIRST GATE TAKES THE STANDING GUARD on `enemy_side.side_commander`, which
# is an optional chained link. The second is an iteration over
# `any_side_knight`, which is not a link and needs none, and it uses `root`
# rather than `prev` inside the entered scope for the reason recorded on
# bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION and is therefore disjoint
# by construction. One man can perfectly well have made somebody standing in his
# own line while the man who made HIM commands the far side - that is a
# three-generation trade working exactly as it is supposed to - and the first
# wins that tie because being on the receiving end of it is the larger fact.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_the_man_who_raised_him_commands_the_far_side_trigger, WHICH IS
# THE NEAR MISS WORTH NAMING, and the two do overlap in the world: AGOT sets
# `set_relation_guardian` alongside this one when the boy is under sixteen. They
# are still different questions. A guardian is responsible for a child, answers
# for him, feeds him and is finished when he comes of age. This is a man who
# taught another man a specific physical trade and can watch him practise it
# from the far side of a field and know which parts of it are his.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. The relation exists only where AGOT wrote
# a knighting or a squireship, and both gates then require the other end of it
# to be standing on this particular field this particular afternoon.
bc_the_man_who_made_him_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_agot_knight = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_a_man_he_made_is_in_this_line_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			has_relation_agot_knight = root
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_the_man_who_made_him_commands_the_far_side_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the cloak and the body it is over ---------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD KNOWS WHO WEARS THE WHITE AND HAS NEVER ASKED WHOSE BODY IT IS OVER.
# bc_is_kingsguard_trigger is the `kingsguard` trait or one of seven council
# positions, and gates bc_knight_the_white_cloak; it establishes that a man took
# the vow and nothing else. AGOT keeps the rest of it in a scripted relation, `bodyguard` /
# `bodyguard_target`, set in agot_start_bodyguarding in
# 00_agot_kingsguard_effects.txt when a sworn sword is actually assigned to a
# particular person. It was at zero occurrences here.
#
# THE DIRECTION IS FIXED BY AGOT AND IS AGAIN NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE. The person
# being guarded is the one who takes `set_relation_bodyguard = scope:councillor`,
# so it is the SWORN SWORD who ends up holding `bodyguard_target`, and on any
# root `has_relation_bodyguard_target = X` reads "X is the person I stand over".
# Both gates below use that trigger and neither uses `has_relation_bodyguard`.
#
# THE TWO GATES ARE THE TWO ENDS OF ONE ARRANGEMENT, and the arrangement is not
# a rank, an oath or an office. It is a distance. The first asks whether
# somebody in this line is keeping that distance to root. The second asks
# whether root is keeping it to the man commanding this side.
#
# THE FIRST IS AN ITERATION over `any_side_knight`, which is not a link and
# needs no guard, and uses `root` inside the entered scope for the reason
# recorded on bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger. The second takes the standing
# guard on `side_commander`, which is an optional link.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION and is therefore disjoint
# by construction. Being stood over is the larger fact and wins the tie.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_is_kingsguard_trigger AND NEITHER IS bc_knight_pulls_him_out'S
# GATE, WHICH ARE THE TWO NEAR MISSES WORTH NAMING. The first of those says a
# man holds an office; this says which person that office currently resolves to,
# and the relation exists in AGOT for arrangements that are not the Kingsguard
# at all. The second is a rescue that happens - a man reaching a commander who
# is losing - and both of these are about a standing condition that is true all
# afternoon whether or not anything ever comes of it.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. AGOT assigns this relation deliberately
# and one at a time, and both gates then require the other end of it to be
# standing on this particular field.
bc_a_man_in_this_line_is_sworn_to_his_body_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			has_relation_bodyguard_target = root
		}
	}
}

bc_the_one_he_is_sworn_to_commands_this_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_bodyguard_target = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
	NOT = { bc_a_man_in_this_line_is_sworn_to_his_body_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the marriage that is standing on both sides -----------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS READ MARRIAGE THREE TIMES AND HAS NEVER READ THE IN-LAWS.
# bc_he_married_into_the_other_side_trigger asks whether one of root's spouses
# is close family of the man commanding the far side, and there is a pregnancy
# gate near it; both go through `any_spouse`. AGOT keeps the rest of it in a
# scripted relation, `agot_parent_in_law` / `agot_child_in_law`, set in
# on_lmf_in_law_setup in agot_lmf_misc_on_actions.txt and again at game start.
# `has_relation_agot_child_in_law` was at zero occurrences here.
#
# THE DIRECTION IS FIXED BY AGOT AND IS, FOR ONCE, THE OBVIOUS ONE, BUT CHECK
# IT ANYWAY. In on_lmf_in_law_setup it is `every_parent` of the newly married
# character that takes `set_relation_agot_child_in_law = scope:spouse`, so the
# PARENT holds `agot_child_in_law` and the person who married in holds
# `agot_parent_in_law`. On any root, `has_relation_agot_child_in_law = X` reads
# "X married one of my children". Both gates below are written from the older
# end of that arrangement and neither uses `has_relation_agot_parent_in_law`.
#
# THE FIRST DELIBERATELY READS child_in_law AND NOT parent_in_law, AND THAT IS
# A DISTINCTNESS DECISION RATHER THAN A TASTE ONE. `is_close_family_of` covers
# parents, so a gate saying "the far commander is my spouse's father" would be
# a strict SUBSET of bc_he_married_into_the_other_side_trigger and would not be
# allowed to ship. Read the other way round it is not: the man who married
# root's child is not close family of root's spouse, and the shipped gate never
# sees him.
#
# THE SECOND IS THE SAME ARRANGEMENT SEEN FROM THE YOUNGER END, iterated over
# root's own line rather than aimed across the field. It is not a subset of
# bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger either, and for the same reason in reverse: a
# spouse's father is not close family, so that gate has never counted him.
#
# THE FIRST TAKES THE STANDING GUARD on `enemy_side.side_commander`, which is an
# optional link. The second is an iteration over `any_side_knight`, which is not
# a link and needs no guard, and uses `root` inside the entered scope for the
# reason recorded on bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION and is therefore disjoint
# by construction. Both can be true on one field - a man can have married a
# daughter to the far side and be standing beside his own wife's father - and
# when they are, the man across the field is the larger fact and wins the tie.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. AGOT only sets this relation on living
# parents at the moment a marriage is made, both gates then require the other
# end of it to be armed and on this particular field, and the first additionally
# requires him to be running the far side rather than merely standing in it.
bc_the_one_who_married_his_child_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_agot_child_in_law = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_the_father_of_his_spouse_is_in_this_line_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			has_relation_agot_child_in_law = root
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_the_one_who_married_his_child_commands_the_far_side_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the thing he does not have any more -------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS MENTIONED DRAGONRIDERS EXACTLY TWICE AND BOTH TIMES IN A COMMENT.
# The note at the top of bc_knight_phase_events.txt records that AGOT's own
# combat phase events include a dragonrider entry at base = 100000 gated behind
# `is_current_dragonrider_warfare`, and that it therefore does not crowd this
# mod's pool. That is the whole of the mod's engagement with the subject. No
# gate anywhere reads a dragon trait.
#
# BOTH GATES BELOW ARE THE PHOTOGRAPHIC NEGATIVE OF THAT AGOT EVENT. AGOT's
# fires for a man who has a dragon on this field. These fire for a man who used
# to have one and is standing in the mud like everybody else. `dragonrider` is
# never read here; the two traits that are read are the two AGOT swaps it for.
#
# THE TWO TRAITS ARE BOTH REQUIRED AND NEITHER IS SUFFICIENT ON ITS OWN. In
# agot_dragon.0018 a rider whose dragon has just died has `dragonrider` removed
# and takes `dragonwidowed` if scope:dragonwidowed exists and
# `dragonless_dragonrider` otherwise, and agot_untame_dragon in
# 00_agot_dragon_effects.txt hands out `dragonless_dragonrider` again for a
# beast that is still alive and simply no longer his. The mod does not care
# which of the three ways it went. It cares that the man could once do a thing
# he cannot do today, so the `OR` covers both traits and nothing distinguishes
# them downstream.
#
# NEITHER GATE READS `agot_dragon`, THE RELATION, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. That
# relation is self-corresponding - `corresponding = agot_dragon` - so it is held
# by the dragon as well as by the rider, and a gate built on it would be true of
# the beast. The traits are only ever on people.
#
# THE FIRST IS A PLAIN TRAIT TEST ON ROOT and takes no guard, because a trait is
# not a link. The second is an iteration over `any_side_knight`, which is not a
# link either, and uses `root` inside the entered scope for the reason recorded
# on bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION and is therefore disjoint
# by construction, which also settles the one case that would otherwise be
# ambiguous: `any_side_knight` will hand back root himself if root is knighted
# on this side, so without the negation a man would satisfy the second gate by
# being the first gate. He does not. Having been the rider is the larger fact
# and wins the tie.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. There is no era of this map in which more
# than a handful of living people have ever been on a dragon, and both gates
# then require one of that handful to be armed and standing on this field with
# the thing already taken off him.
bc_he_used_to_arrive_from_above_trigger = {
	OR = {
		has_trait = dragonless_dragonrider
		has_trait = dragonwidowed
	}
}

bc_one_in_this_line_used_to_arrive_from_above_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			OR = {
				has_trait = dragonless_dragonrider
				has_trait = dragonwidowed
			}
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_he_used_to_arrive_from_above_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the men who are standing here as terms ----------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER READ THE HOSTAGE SYSTEM. `is_hostage`, `is_hostage_of` and
# `is_hostage_from` were all at zero occurrences here, and AGOT uses the system -
# it appears in its activities, its interactions and its casus belli files - so
# these are not gates on an empty table.
#
# THE THREE TRIGGERS ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE AND THE DUMP IS EXPLICIT ABOUT IT.
# `is_hostage_of = X` asks whether X is the WARDEN, the man currently holding
# him. `is_hostage_from = X` asks whether X is the ORIGINAL LIEGE, the man who
# handed him over. `is_hostage` alone asks neither. The two gates below use one
# each of the first two and neither uses the third, because a bare `is_hostage`
# gate would be a superset of both and could not be told apart from them.
#
# THE FIRST IS DELIBERATELY THE ORIGINAL LIEGE AND NOT THE WARDEN. A hostage
# lives in his warden's court, so the army he is standing in is almost always
# the warden's; a gate asking whether the warden commands the far side would
# describe a situation the game very rarely produces. Asking whether the man who
# GAVE HIM UP commands the far side describes the situation it produces all the
# time, which is the two of them going to war over precisely the thing he was
# handed across to prevent.
#
# THE SECOND IS THE SAME ARRANGEMENT FROM THE OTHER END, and it is the warden's
# end, so it uses `is_hostage_of`. It asks whether one of the men armed and
# standing in root's own line is a boy root is holding to somebody else's good
# behaviour.
#
# THE FIRST TAKES THE STANDING GUARD on `enemy_side.side_commander`, which is an
# optional link. The second is an iteration over `any_side_knight`, which is not
# a link and needs no guard, and uses `root` inside the entered scope for the
# reason recorded on bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION and is therefore disjoint
# by construction. Both can be true of one man on one field - being held is no
# bar to holding somebody - and when they are, being the terms yourself is the
# larger fact and wins the tie.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. Hostages are handed over one at a time and
# by name, both gates then require that particular man to be armed and on this
# particular field, and the first additionally requires the other party to the
# arrangement to be running the army opposite him.
bc_the_man_who_gave_him_up_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		is_hostage_from = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_one_he_was_given_stands_in_this_line_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		any_side_knight = {
			is_hostage_of = root
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_the_man_who_gave_him_up_commands_the_far_side_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the reputations that are not his ----------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS MENTIONED ACCOLADES ONCE, IN A COMMENT, TO BORROW THEIR SPELLING.
# The note on bc_his_own_people_have_a_ledger_trigger's neighbour cites
# 04_ep2_eminent_attributes.txt for writing out the three kinslayer traits
# longhand. That is the entirety of it. `is_accolade_successor` and
# `any_acclaimed_knight` were both at zero occurrences, and the accolade files
# are present under this load order, so neither gate is aimed at an empty table.
#
# THESE TWO ARE NOT A PAIR IN THE USUAL SENSE AND ARE NOT WRITTEN AS ONE. Every
# recent batch has been one arrangement seen from both ends, with the second
# carrying the first as an explicit negation. These are two different facts
# about fame that happen to come out of the same system, they are aimed at
# different pools - the first at knights, the second at commanders - and those
# pools roll separately, so there is nothing for a negation to protect. Writing
# one in anyway would have cost the best case each gate has: a man who is next
# in line for a name is exactly the sort of man a commander with no famous
# knights of his own would want, and there is no reason both should not be true
# on the same field.
#
# THE FIRST IS A STATE, NOT AN ACHIEVEMENT, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE MOMENT. The
# engine's successor is not a man who has an accolade. He is a man the engine
# has written down as the one who gets it when somebody else is finished with
# it. There is no glory on him today and there may never be.
#
# THE SECOND IS A COMPARISON ACROSS THE FIELD and is the only gate in this file
