# that asks the same question of both commanders and keeps the answer only when
# it comes back lopsided. `any_acclaimed_knight` is an ITERATOR and needs no
# guard; the link it is run on, `enemy_side.side_commander`, takes the standing
# guard.
#
# THE SECOND IS NOT bc_side_is_outnumbered_trigger, WHICH IS THE NEAR MISS
# WORTH NAMING. That one compares what the two armies are made of on the day.
# This one compares what the two households have been able to keep, which is a
# slower and much more personal number, and it is allowed to be true of an army
# that is winning every other comparison on the field.
#
# NEITHER FIRES FOR NEARLY EVERYBODY. Accolades are created one at a time by
# lieges who can afford them, successors are named one at a time inside those,
# and the second gate additionally requires the lopsidedness to run the wrong
# way for the man reading it.
bc_he_is_the_named_successor_to_a_name_trigger = {
	is_accolade_successor = yes
}

bc_the_far_side_keeps_famous_men_and_he_does_not_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			any_acclaimed_knight = { count >= 1 }
		}
	}
	NOT = {
		any_acclaimed_knight = { count >= 1 }
	}
}


# --- how many people are left with that name ---------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS HOUSES AND HAS NEVER COUNTED ONE. `bc_foe_is_house_trigger` and
# its neighbours ask whether two men share a house; nothing anywhere asks how
# many people are in it. `any_dynasty_member` and `dynasty_prestige_level` were
# both at zero occurrences.
#
# THE COUNT IS TWO AND THE THRESHOLD IS BORROWED, NOT INVENTED. Vanilla's own
# 00_relation_triggers.txt uses `any_dynasty_member = { count <= 2 }` twice for
# the same idea, and two is the number that makes the gate mean what its name
# says: him, and at most one other person anywhere in the world. Three would
# have caught every minor house on the map and a gate that fires for nearly
# everybody is not a gate.
#
# `is_alive = yes` IS INSIDE THE COUNT ON PURPOSE. The iterator is believed to
# list living members only, and if that is right the filter costs nothing. If it
# is ever wrong, the filter is the difference between "nearly the last of his
# house" and "a house that has not existed for two hundred years", which is not
# a difference this mod can afford to leave to belief.
#
# `dynasty` IS AN OPTIONAL LINK AND TAKES THE STANDING GUARD. Lowborn characters
# have none, and this setting is full of them - hedge knights, sellswords, men
# raised out of nowhere by a war. Both gates guard it, and the second guards it
# twice, once on the far commander and once on that man's dynasty.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE FIRST AS AN EXPLICIT NEGATION and is therefore disjoint
# by construction. Two nearly-extinct houses can absolutely meet on one field -
# that is most of what the later part of a long war looks like - and when they
# do, being the last one yourself is the larger fact and wins the tie. The
# negation is safe because the first gate carries its own guard, which is the
# precedent recorded on bc_the_one_he_loves_is_in_this_line_trigger.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_foe_is_house_trigger AND NEITHER IS
# bc_his_own_people_have_a_ledger_trigger. The first of those asks whether two
# men share a name; this asks how much of a name is left. The second counts what
# a man has done to his own vassals, which is a thing he chose; this counts
# something that has been done to him by thirty years of other people's wars.
bc_he_is_nearly_the_last_of_his_house_trigger = {
	exists = dynasty
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = dynasty }
		dynasty = {
			any_dynasty_member = {
				count <= 2
				is_alive = yes
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_the_man_opposite_is_nearly_the_last_of_his_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			exists = dynasty
			trigger_if = {
				limit = { exists = dynasty }
				dynasty = {
					any_dynasty_member = {
						count <= 2
						is_alive = yes
					}
				}
			}
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_he_is_nearly_the_last_of_his_house_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the shape of the rest of his wars ---------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD ASKS WHO LEADS THIS WAR AND HAS NEVER ASKED WHAT ELSE HE IS IN.
# bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger reads `is_war_leader = root` off the war behind
# this battle. That is one war. `is_at_war_as_attacker` and
# `is_at_war_as_defender` were both at zero occurrences, and they are not about
# this war at all - they are the shape of a man's whole ledger of wars on the
# day he happens to be standing here.
#
# THE DUMP'S CAVEAT IS THE WHOLE REASON THESE ARE RARE ENOUGH TO BE GATES. Both
# triggers are documented "Does not consider your lieges' wars". A knight riding
# in his lord's host, with no war of his own, answers no to both of them. So
# does every landless man on the field. What is left is the much smaller set of
# people who are personally, in their own name, at war - and then the gates ask
# what shape that is.
#
# THE FIRST IS DELIBERATELY AN EXCLUSIVE READING and is not just
# `is_at_war_as_attacker`. A bare attacker gate would be true of half the map in
# any decade with a war in it, and it would also be true of every man the second
# gate is about, which would make the second a strict subset. Spelling the
# negation in gives the first gate a meaning a bare one does not have: every war
# with his name on it today is one he chose to open.
#
# THE SECOND IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME SPLIT and needs no negation of the
# first, because the first already excludes it by its own second row. They are
# disjoint by construction in both directions, which is unusual here and worth
# saying out loud so that nobody later "tidies" the first gate's `NOT` away and
# silently makes the pair overlap.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are plain character triggers on root and neither
# reads a link.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger AND THAT IS THE NEAR MISS WORTH
# NAMING. That one is about the war this battle belongs to and answers a
# question about today. These answer a question about the year, and both of them
# can be true of a man who is fighting somebody else's battle this afternoon.
bc_every_war_with_his_name_on_it_he_opened_trigger = {
	is_at_war_as_attacker = yes
	NOT = { is_at_war_as_defender = yes }
}

bc_he_is_attacking_somewhere_and_defending_somewhere_trigger = {
	is_at_war_as_attacker = yes
	is_at_war_as_defender = yes
}


# --- what his years are actually going into ----------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS WHAT A MAN WAS TAUGHT AND HAS NEVER READ WHAT HE IS SPENDING
# HIS YEARS ON NOW. `has_focus` was at zero occurrences across the whole mod.
# An education trait is a fact about a boy; a lifestyle focus is a fact about
# what the man has chosen to put his attention into this decade, and it can be
# changed, and the two are not the same sentence.
#
# THE NEAR MISSES ARE bc_he_was_raised_for_books_trigger AND
# bc_he_was_raised_for_the_dark_trigger, and the distinction is one this file
# has already drawn once: those two wrap vanilla's education wrappers and ask
# where a man STARTED. These ask where his years are GOING. Neither pair is a
# subset of the other in either direction - a lord educated in arms can spend
# his forties on books, and a maester-taught boy can spend his forties on
# nothing of the kind.
#
# THE OTHER NEAR MISS IS `has_lifestyle`, which bc_he_was_pointed_at_war_and_
# left_trigger and its partner already read as `martial_lifestyle`. Both gates
# below are disjoint from that one by construction, because a learning focus
# and an intrigue focus each put a man in a lifestyle that is not the martial
# one. Nothing here can overlap with either shipped gate.
#
# DISJOINT WITHOUT A NEGATION, WHICH IS THE EXCEPTION AND NOT THE RULE HERE. A
# character holds exactly one focus at a time and `set_focus` replaces rather
# than adds, so these two cannot both answer yes and a `NOT` would be dead
# script that implies a reader should worry about something the engine already
# guarantees. This is written down so that the missing negation reads as a
# decision rather than an oversight.
#
# THE ADVENTURER AND PIRATE VARIANTS ARE DELIBERATELY LEFT OUT. Vanilla adds
# `learning_adventurer_focus` and `intrigue_adventurer_focus`, and AGOT adds
# `learning_scholarship_landless_pirate_focus` (common/focuses/
# 00_lifestyle_focuses.txt:1061). Those belong to landless governments and
# describe a different life entirely, and folding them in would widen both
# gates in exchange for a page that would then be wrong about half the men it
# fired for.
#
# THE KEYS ARE LISTED RATHER THAN GROUPED because there is no group key for a
# lifestyle's focuses to test, and all six are confirmed present in AGOT's own
# override at 00_lifestyle_focuses.txt - intrigue at :467, :537 and :599,
# learning at :967, :1013 and :1100.
#
# RARITY. Three focuses out of the nineteen a character can be running, and
# then the man has to be standing in a battle while running one of them.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are plain character triggers on root and neither
# reads a link.
bc_the_years_are_going_into_books_trigger = {
	OR = {
		has_focus = learning_medicine_focus
		has_focus = learning_scholarship_focus
		has_focus = learning_theology_focus
	}
}

bc_the_years_are_going_into_the_dark_trigger = {
	OR = {
		has_focus = intrigue_skulduggery_focus
		has_focus = intrigue_temptation_focus
		has_focus = intrigue_intimidation_focus
	}
}


# --- who there is above him -------------------------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS READ RANK, TENURE AND WEIGHT AND HAS NEVER READ INDEPENDENCE.
# `highest_held_title_tier` and `is_landed` carry the station batch,
# `is_powerful_vassal` carries the weight batch, and all three of them describe
# a man's position INSIDE a realm. `is_independent_ruler` was at zero
# occurrences and asks the one question none of them asks: whether there is
# anybody above him at all.
#
# THE NEAR MISS IS bc_facing_a_crown_trigger, which reads
# `highest_held_title_tier >= tier_kingdom` on the far commander. Rank and
# independence are not the same fact in either direction - a vassal king holds
# tier_kingdom and answers to an emperor, and a merchant prince or a pirate
# lord answers to nobody at all while holding nothing so grand. Neither gate
# below is a subset of that one and it is not a subset of either.
#
# THE OTHER NEAR MISS IS bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger, which asks who opened
# the war this battle belongs to. A vassal can be the war leader of his own
# rebellion while having a liege in the world; independence is about the map
# and not about today.
#
# `is_landless_ruler` WAS CONSIDERED AS THE SECOND GATE AND REJECTED. It is
# documented as "holds any title, but no on-map land", which makes it a strict
# subset of the shipped `is_landed = no` on bc_commander_phase_events.txt:1928,
# and a gate that is contained by a gate the mod already fires on is not a new
# question. The pairing below is by direction instead.
#
# THE FIRST IS A COMMANDER-POOL GATE IN PRACTICE. A knight in somebody's line
# is a courtier or a vassal and answers no to it almost always; there is no
# harm in the knight pool asking, and no expectation that it will ever say yes.
# The second is the reverse and works from either pool, because its subject is
# the man on the other side and its only condition on root is a negative one.
#
# THEY ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The second carries the exact negation of
# the first, so a field where both commanders answer to nobody produces the
# first and never the second, which is the correct precedence: being the man
# with nobody above him is the larger fact about an afternoon than noticing it
# about somebody else.
#
# RARITY. Most wars in this setting are fought between men who both have a
# liege somewhere, and most of the men standing in the line have two or three
# above them. Independence at the head of a battle is a minority case and the
# second gate narrows it further by requiring that root is not in it.
#
# THE FIRST TAKES NO GUARD; the second takes the standing one, because an enemy
# side can be leaderless.
bc_there_is_nobody_above_him_trigger = {
	is_independent_ruler = yes
}

bc_the_man_opposite_answers_to_nobody_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			is_independent_ruler = yes
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_there_is_nobody_above_him_trigger = yes }
}


# --- what he paid to learn about going backwards -----------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER READ A PERK. `has_perk` was at zero occurrences over a
# hundred and fourteen batches. An education trait is what was done to a boy, a
# focus is where a man's attention is this decade, and a perk is the third
# thing and the only one of the three that is a PURCHASE: a node he spent years
# of accumulated experience on, deliberately, in preference to the node beside
# it. That is a much narrower statement about a man than either of the others.
#
# THE TWO CHOSEN ARE THE OPPOSITE ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION, which is what makes
# them a pair rather than two unrelated nodes. `organized_retreat_perk`
# (common/lifestyle_perks/00_martial_1_strategy_tree_perks.txt:96) is a man who
# decided that knowing how to go backwards was worth paying for.
# `never_back_down_perk` (00_martial_3_chivalry_tree_perks.txt:229) is a man
# who paid for the reverse conviction. Both are confirmed present in AGOT's own
# copies of those files, which is where the line numbers above come from.
#
# THE SECOND CARRIES THE NEGATION because the trees are not exclusive and a man
# with enough years in him can hold both nodes. When he does, the first wins:
# having bought the knowledge of how to withdraw is the fact that describes
# what he will actually do this afternoon, and the conviction is the thing that
# gets overruled by it.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE LIFESTYLE OR FOCUS GATES ALREADY SHIPPED. A perk
# is permanent and a focus is not: bc_he_was_pointed_elsewhere_and_came_back_
# trigger reads `has_lifestyle = martial_lifestyle`, which is a statement about
# where the man's attention is now, and a lord who bought a martial node at
# thirty and spends his fifties on books answers yes here and no there. The
# batch 113 focus gates are the same case in the other direction.
#
# NEITHER IS bc_he_was_pointed_at_war_and_left_trigger EITHER. That gate is
# about a boy pointed at arms who did not stay pointed at them. These are about
# a specific sum of experience spent on a specific opinion, by a man who may
# have been pointed anywhere at all.
#
# RARITY. Two nodes out of the twenty-four in the three martial trees, and each
# of them sits deep enough that a man has to have spent a real part of his life
# in that tree to be holding it.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are plain character triggers on root and neither
# reads a link.
bc_he_paid_to_know_how_to_go_backwards_trigger = {
	has_perk = organized_retreat_perk
}

bc_he_paid_for_the_opposite_conviction_trigger = {
	has_perk = never_back_down_perk
	NOT = { bc_he_paid_to_know_how_to_go_backwards_trigger = yes }
}


# --- how his people fight, as opposed to who they are ------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS ASKED WHO A MAN'S PEOPLE ARE THREE TIMES AND HAS NEVER ASKED HOW
# THEY FIGHT. bc_he_is_free_folk_trigger and the westerosi wrapper name a
# people; bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger asks whether two men share one. All
# three are questions of identity. `has_cultural_tradition` was at zero
# occurrences and asks a question of method, and the two are orthogonal - a
# Westerosi culture can hold either of the traditions below and so can a
# culture from anywhere else.
#
# THE TRIGGER IS CULTURE-SCOPED, NOT CHARACTER-SCOPED. The 1.19.0.6 dump gives
# its form as `<culture> = { has_cultural_tradition = scope:tradition }`, so
# both gates spell `culture = { ... }` around it, exactly as
# bc_he_is_free_folk_trigger already does for agot_is_wildling_culture. A bare
# `has_cultural_tradition` on a character would not be a narrower gate, it
# would be a broken one.
#
# `culture` TAKES NO GUARD. Every character has one, and this file already
# reads it unguarded in three places.
#
# THE TWO CHOSEN ARE VANILLA'S OWN MATCHED PAIR, tradition_stand_and_fight and
# tradition_hit_and_run, at common/culture/traditions/00_combat_traditions.txt
# :2516 and :2391 in AGOT's copy of the file. They are the two opposite answers
# to the question of what a people does when it meets an army, which is what
# makes them a pair here rather than two traditions picked for flavour.
#
# THEY ARE NOT ENGINE-EXCLUSIVE, which is why the second carries a negation.
# AGOT's copy sets `is_shown = { always = yes }` on both and has commented out
# the exclusivity clauses vanilla shipped, so a culture that acquired both
# would answer yes twice. When it does, the standing tradition wins: this mod's
# every moment happens inside a set-piece battle, which is the situation
# tradition_stand_and_fight is about, and the raiding tradition describes what
# those same people do on all the days they are not in one.
#
# RARITY. A culture holds a handful of traditions out of the several dozen it
# could, and these are two specific ones out of the combat category alone.
bc_his_people_meet_it_where_it_stands_trigger = {
	culture = { has_cultural_tradition = tradition_stand_and_fight }
}

bc_his_people_do_not_usually_be_here_for_this_trigger = {
	culture = { has_cultural_tradition = tradition_hit_and_run }
	NOT = { bc_his_people_meet_it_where_it_stands_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the names that already mean an argument ---------------------------------
#
# THE MOD READS HOUSES AS A RELATION AND AS A COUNT AND HAS NEVER READ WHICH
# HOUSE. bc_foe_is_house_trigger asks whether two men share a name; batch 111
# asks how many people are left holding one. Neither of them has ever asked
# what a particular name already means before anybody on this field was born.
#
# `agot_is_historical_feud_trigger` (00_agot_dynasty_triggers.txt:33) IS
# AGOT'S OWN LIST and is used rather than a hand-written OR of dynasty keys for
# the usual reason: it is five pairs of houses whose quarrels predate the
# bookmark - Peake and Manderly, Jast and Falwell, Whitehill and Forrester,
# Yronwood and Fowler, Blackwood and Bracken - and if AGOT ever adds a sixth
# pair these two gates follow it without being touched.
#
# THE GUARD IS NOT OPTIONAL AND IT IS NOT AGOT'S. Read the trigger: it is a
# bare `dynasty = dynasty:dynn_Peake` and the rest, with no `?=` anywhere,
# where the trigger directly beneath it in the same file spells `dynasty ?=`
# for the same kind of test. This setting is full of hedge knights and
# sellswords with no dynasty at all, so both gates below put the standing
# `exists = dynasty` guard in front of the call and nest the call in a
# trigger_if that repeats it.
#
# NEITHER GATE CLAIMS THE OTHER HALF OF THE FEUD IS ON THE FIELD. The AGOT
# trigger answers a question about one man's name and nothing about who is
# opposite him; Blackwood facing Forrester answers yes twice and is not a feud
# at all. Both toasts are written to what the gate actually says, which is that
# a man is carrying a name whose meaning was settled centuries before this
# hedge, and neither of them may assert that the counterpart is here.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The second carries the exact negation of the first,
# so a field with one of these names at each end produces the first only. That
# is the right precedence: being the man with the name is a larger fact about
# his afternoon than noticing the name on somebody else.
#
# RARITY. Ten houses on a map with several hundred, and then the man has to be
# leading or standing in a battle.
bc_his_name_is_half_of_an_old_argument_trigger = {
	exists = dynasty
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = dynasty }
		agot_is_historical_feud_trigger = yes
	}
}

bc_the_name_opposite_is_half_of_an_old_argument_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			exists = dynasty
			trigger_if = {
				limit = { exists = dynasty }
				agot_is_historical_feud_trigger = yes
			}
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_his_name_is_half_of_an_old_argument_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the men on his payroll who are not here ----------------------------------
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER READ A COURT. It reads what a man holds, who he is related
# to, what he was taught, what he believes, what he has vowed and what he is
# owed, and `employs_court_position` was at zero occurrences across the whole
# mod. It asks something none of those ask: what work he has decided is worth
# paying somebody else to do all year.
#
# BOTH POSITIONS ARE AGOT'S OWN, not vanilla's, and both keys were read out of
# AGOT's file rather than guessed - `master_at_arms_court_position`
# (00_agot_court_positions.txt:2965) and `maester_court_position` (:31).
# The engine trigger takes the position type key directly; AGOT itself spells it
# `employs_court_position = harbormaster_court_position` at
# activities/activity_types/agot_sailing.txt:177, and vanilla does the same in
# hunt.txt and chariot_race.txt.
#
# NO GUARD. `employs_court_position` is a character-scoped test in the 1.19.0.6
# dump - "is the scoped character employing a target court position type?" - and
# not a link read. There is nothing here to be null.
#
# NO NEGATION, AND THAT IS A DECISION. These two are not opposite halves of one
# question; they are two different facts, and a lord can perfectly well be
# paying for both. What keeps them apart is the engine: the first is a commander
# template and the second is a knight template, the side commander is not in the
# knight list, and the two pools roll separately. A `NOT` between them would
# guard against a collision the game cannot produce. Batch 113 made the same
# call for the same reason.
#
# RARITY IS DOUBLE ON BOTH. Each requires a landed ruler who has actually filled
# that seat, and each position carries a cultural innovation on top - AGOT hides
# the master-at-arms behind `innovation_agot_sword_bound` and the maester behind
# `innovation_maesters` - and then the man has to be leading or fighting in a
# battle. The second is the rarer of the two by a distance, because it asks for
# a man who keeps a maester and is still out here in a melee himself.
bc_he_keeps_a_man_paid_to_be_better_at_this_trigger = {
	employs_court_position = master_at_arms_court_position
}

bc_there_is_a_chain_and_a_tower_behind_him_trigger = {
	employs_court_position = maester_court_position
}


# --- the arrangement that has not happened yet --------------------------------
#
# `is_betrothed` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. The mod reads marriages at
# length - a living wife, a pregnant one, a buried one, a wife's father in the
# far line, a marriage into the other side - and it has never once read the
# state that comes before all of that, which is a contract signed by other
# people about a wedding that has not happened. `is_betrothed` is documented in
# the 1.19.0.6 dump as "Is the scope character betrothed?", character scope,
# yes/no.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE MARRIAGE GATES. A betrothal is not a marriage: the
# batch eighty-eight pair needs a living spouse, the widower pair needs a dead
# former one, and none of the four says anything about a promise outstanding. A
# betrothed widower answers yes to one of the widower gates and yes to this one,
# which is two true facts about the same man and not a containment either way.
#
# THE SECOND TAKES THE STANDING GUARD because it walks
# scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander, which is an optional link: the
# `exists` sits as a sibling and the read is nested in a trigger_if that repeats
# it. `is_betrothed` itself needs nothing - it is a plain character test.
#
# THE SECOND IS ALSO KNOWABLE, WHICH MATTERS FOR WHAT IS WRITTEN ON IT. A
# betrothal in this setting is not a private arrangement; it is negotiated
# between houses, announced, and talked about for a year before the day. A man
# in the line may perfectly well know who the lord opposite is promised to
# without ever having been within a hundred yards of him.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, the first winning the tie, on the usual reasoning:
# the promise a man is carrying himself is a larger fact about his afternoon
# than the promise somebody else is carrying.
#
# RARITY. Most adults with anything to command are already married, and most
# betrothals in the game belong to children who are not standing in any battle.
# What is left is a narrow band - the unmarried grown man with a contract
# outstanding - and then he has to be leading or fighting here.
bc_there_is_a_wedding_waiting_on_him_trigger = {
	is_betrothed = yes
}

bc_a_wedding_is_waiting_on_the_man_opposite_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			is_betrothed = yes
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_there_is_a_wedding_waiting_on_him_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the slow thing that is running while he is here --------------------------
#
# `any_scheme` AND `any_targeting_scheme` BOTH RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD.
# The mod reads a man's hatreds and his friendships and his secrets and his
# hooks, and it has never read the one thing that is actually in motion: a plot
# with a clock on it, running in some other part of the world, while he spends
# an afternoon in a hedge.
#
# BOTH ARE ITERATORS AND ITERATORS ARE NOT LINKS, so neither takes a guard: an
# empty list is a clean no. The 1.19.0.6 dump gives `any_scheme` as "Iterate
# through all schemes owned by the character" and `any_targeting_scheme` as
# "Iterate through all schemes targeting the character", both character scope,
# both yielding scheme scope.
#
# `scheme_type = murder` IS READ IN SCHEME SCOPE and is vanilla's own idiom -
# 00_scheme_interactions.txt:1582 and :2038 spell exactly this inside
# `any_targeting_scheme`. The key is `murder`, which is the first line of
# common/schemes/scheme_types/murder_scheme.txt in vanilla AND in AGOT's
# override of the same file, so the type survives AGOT.
#
# MURDER AND NOT ANY SCHEME AT ALL. `any_scheme = { count >= 1 }` would fire for
# a large fraction of the AI, which is not a gate. Restricting it to the one
# scheme type that intends a death makes both halves narrow and makes both of
# them mean something specific on a battlefield.
#
# NO NEGATION BETWEEN THEM, AND THAT IS A DECISION. These are two different
# facts and a man may perfectly well be both a plotter and a target. What keeps
# the templates apart is the engine: the first is a commander template and the
# second is a knight template, the side commander is not in the knight list, and
# the pools roll separately. Batches 113 and 118 made the same call.
#
# THE SECOND IS A FACT ITS SUBJECT DOES NOT HAVE. A scheme targeting a man is
# not something he is told about, and the toast written on this gate is
# accordingly forbidden from letting him find out, suspect, or name anybody. The
# mod has narrated around a man before - a conversation he was not at, a room he
# will never see - and this is the same move with more at stake.
#
# RARITY. A murder plot is a rare thing to have running at any given moment even
# among the AI, it has to be running on this exact day, and then the man has to
# be leading or fighting in a battle.
bc_he_has_a_murder_running_somewhere_trigger = {
	any_scheme = {
		scheme_type = murder
	}
}

bc_somebody_is_trying_to_have_him_killed_trigger = {
	any_targeting_scheme = {
		scheme_type = murder
	}
}


# --- the other thing that is killing people this year -------------------------
#
# EVERY EPIDEMIC KEYWORD RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. The mod reads wars,
# feuds, factions, plots, oaths and weather, and it has never once read the
# other mechanism in this game that empties a hall. `any_character_epidemic` is
# documented in the 1.19.0.6 dump as "Gets epidemics affecting the scoped
# character", character scope, yielding epidemic scope. AGOT uses the system -
# common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_epidemics_triggers.txt is nine of its own
# triggers built on top of it - so this is not a vanilla feature the submod is
# pretending exists in Westeros.
#
# ITERATORS ARE NOT LINKS. The first gate takes no guard; an empty list is a
# clean no. Vanilla spells the same test as `any_character_epidemic = { }` at
# events/dlc/ep3/ep3_emperor_yearly_8.txt:119, and this file's own house style
# is the explicit `count >= 1`, which is what the shipped
# bc_he_stands_to_inherit_trigger uses on `any_heir_title`.
#
# THE SECOND WALKS AN OPTIONAL LINK and takes the standing guard around
# scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander: `exists` as a sibling, the read
# nested in a trigger_if that repeats it. The iterator inside the entered scope
# still needs nothing.
#
# THE SECOND IS VISIBLE, WHICH IS WHAT IT IS ALLOWED TO BE WRITTEN ON. Nobody in
# a line knows the name of a sickness or where it started. What a man can see
# from fifty paces is a host that moves wrong - slow, short-ranked, sitting down
# when it should not be - and that is the whole of what the toast on that gate
# may claim to have noticed.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, the first winning the tie. During a real outbreak
# both hosts will often answer yes, and the right precedence is the same as
# everywhere else in this file: the thing in his own camp is a larger fact about
# his afternoon than the thing in somebody else's.
#
# RARITY. Epidemics are episodic rather than ambient - most years in most realms
# have none at all - and then it has to be running on the day, and then the man
# has to be leading or fighting in a battle.
bc_there_is_sickness_in_his_own_camp_trigger = {
	any_character_epidemic = { count >= 1 }
}

bc_the_host_opposite_is_sick_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			any_character_epidemic = { count >= 1 }
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_there_is_sickness_in_his_own_camp_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the other host that has his name on it -----------------------------------
#
# `any_army` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. A hundred and twenty batches have
# read the men in front of a commander, the men beside him, the ground under
# him and the war he is inside, and not one of them has ever asked the simplest
# strategic question there is: whether this field is the whole of what he
# currently has in the world, or one of two places his name is being spent
# today. The 1.19.0.6 dump gives `any_army` as "Iterate through all armies",
# character scope, yielding army scope.
#
# `count >= 2` AND NOT `count >= 1`. The host standing on this field is itself
# one of the entries, so `count >= 1` would be true of every raiser in every
# battle and would not be a gate at all. Two is the smallest number that means
# what the moment is about.
#
# ITERATORS ARE NOT LINKS, so the first gate takes no guard. The second walks
# scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander and takes the standing one, with
# the `exists` as a sibling and the read nested in a trigger_if that repeats it.
#
# THIS IS NOT AN ODDS GATE. bc_their_host_gap_value and the side-size rows read
# how many men are on this field. This reads whether there is a second field at
# all, which is a fact about a campaign rather than a fact about a battle, and
# the two are independent in both directions: a man may be outnumbered here and
# have nothing anywhere else, or hold the larger host here and still have half
# his strength sitting in front of somebody's gate a week's ride away.
#
# THE SECOND IS RUMOUR AND MUST BE WRITTEN AS RUMOUR. Where the far lord's other
# host is, and what it is doing, is not knowable from a rank. That there is one
# is exactly the sort of thing a camp knows, because armies are raised in public
# and men on the roads talk.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, the first winning the tie. Both sides in a large war
# will often answer yes, and having half of one's own strength elsewhere is a
# larger fact about a man's afternoon than knowing that the enemy does.
#
# RARITY. Most raisers hold one host at a time; a second raised army standing
# somewhere else on the same day is a thing that happens in the middle of a big
# war and not otherwise, and then the man has to be in a battle.
bc_he_has_a_second_host_somewhere_trigger = {
	any_army = { count >= 2 }
}

bc_the_man_opposite_has_a_second_host_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			any_army = { count >= 2 }
		}
	}
	NOT = { bc_he_has_a_second_host_somewhere_trigger = yes }
}


# --- the man who is holding it while he is out here ---------------------------
#
# THE DIARCHY SYSTEM RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. `is_diarch` is zero,
# `has_active_diarchy` is zero, and the only two occurrences of the string
# `diarch` anywhere in this submod are filename citations inside comments in
# `bc_scripted_effects.txt` and `bc_script_values.txt`. A hundred and twenty-two
# batches have read who a man's enemies are, who his family are, who is owed
# money by him and who wants him dead, and none of them has ever read the one
# arrangement that answers a different question entirely: not who is against
# him, but WHOSE AUTHORITY IS ACTUALLY BEING SPENT ON THIS FIELD.
#
# THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP gives `has_active_diarchy` as "Does the scoped character has
# an active diarchy", yes/no, character scope, and `is_diarch` as "Is the scoped
# character currently an active diarch of anyone?", yes/no, character scope.
# AGOT uses both live and unguarded: `has_active_diarchy = yes` at
# `common/casus_belli_types/00_event_war.txt:3605` and at
# `common/character_interactions/00_marriage_interactions.txt:904`, and
# `is_diarch = yes` at `00_event_war.txt:3674` and throughout
# `common/character_interactions/00_diarch_interactions.txt`. This is a system
# the submod's host game is running, not a vanilla feature being wished onto
# Westeros - a regency over a child, or a man deliberately seated beside his
# lord, is about as Westerosi as an arrangement gets.
#
# THE TWO GATES READ TWO DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT MEN, which is why
# neither carries a negation of the other. The first asks whether the man
# commanding here left somebody behind him holding his seal. The second asks
# whether the man commanding the far host holds his own command on somebody
# else's account. They are not two halves of one fact and they are not on the
# same axis in the sense the distinctness rule cares about: a man may be both,
# either or neither, and the two produce entirely different afternoons. The
# separation that matters is structural and the engine provides it - the side
# commander is not in the knight list, and the two pools roll separately.
#
# NEITHER OF THESE IS A LOYALTY GATE. `diarch_loyalty` and `diarchy_swing` exist
# and are deliberately not read: how well the arrangement is going is a question
# about a household, and a man standing in a battle has no access to today's
# answer. That the arrangement EXISTS is a fact of a different kind. It was
# announced, it was witnessed, and everybody who matters has known about it
# since it was made.
#
# THE SECOND IS RUMOUR AND IS WRITTEN AS RUMOUR. Who holds a great man's seat is
# not a secret and never has been; it is the sort of thing heralds say out loud.
# What is not knowable from a rank, and is therefore not written, is how that
# man is doing at it.
#
# RARITY. A diarchy needs a ruler who is a child, or incapable, or who chose to
# seat somebody next to himself, and then the relevant man has to be standing in
# a battle on the day. Most lords in most years have no diarch and are nobody's.
bc_somebody_else_is_holding_his_seat_trigger = {
	has_active_diarchy = yes
}

bc_the_man_opposite_is_holding_it_for_somebody_else_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			is_diarch = yes
		}
	}
}


# --- what is already written in him -------------------------------------------
#
# THE MEMORY SYSTEM RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. `any_memory` is zero,
# `any_character_memory` is zero, `has_memory_category` is zero and
# `has_memory_participant` is zero. A hundred and twenty-three batches have read
# what a man owns, who he is related to, what he believes, who he owes and what
# the weather is doing to him, and not one of them has ever read THE THING THE
# GAME ITSELF KEEPS ABOUT HIS PAST. Memories are the engine's own record of what
# has already happened to a character, and this is a mod about a man in the
# middle of something that will become one.
#
# THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP gives `any_memory` as "Iterate through all memories of a
# character", character scope, yielding character_memory, with an optional type
# filter; `has_memory_category` as "Does the character memory have this memory
# category?"; and `has_memory_participant` as "Does the character memory have
# this target character as a participant?", character_memory scope, character
# target. Vanilla spells the participant test as a scope target at
# `common/scripted_rules/00_rules.txt:386-392`, inside exactly this shape - an
# `any_memory` on one man testing a saved scope as participant.
#
# THE `battle` CATEGORY IS REAL AND IS NOT UNIVERSAL. It is declared on
# `witnessed_death_battle_good`, `witnessed_death_battle_good_killer` and
# `witnessed_death_battle_bad` in `common/character_memory_types/
# character_memories_1.txt:1980-2040`, and on three more in
# `ce1_memories.txt:2-60`, always alongside `war` and either `martial` or
# `death`. It is not a counter of battles fought. It is the much narrower thing:
# whether one of these has ever left something behind in him.
#
# THE TWO GATES READ TWO DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT MEN, so neither
# carries a negation of the other, on the same reasoning as the diarchy pair.
# The first asks whether the man commanding here has a battle already written
# into him. The second asks whether the man standing in this rank shares a
# recorded afternoon with the man commanding the far host. A man may have both,
# either or neither, and the structural separation is the engine's: the side
# commander is not in the knight list and the two pools roll separately.
#
# ITERATORS ARE NOT LINKS, so the first gate takes no guard and an empty list is
# a clean no. The second reads `scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander` as
# a TARGET, which is a read, so it takes the standing guard - the `exists` as a
# sibling and the iterator nested in a trigger_if that repeats it.
#
# THE SECOND IS NOT RUMOUR AND DOES NOT NEED TO BE. Everything in it happened to
# the man doing the remembering. He was there. That is what a memory is, and it
# is the reason this gate can license something the far-side gates never can:
# not a thing the camp says, but a thing he personally knows.
#
# RARITY. Battle memories are written by particular event chains rather than by
# fighting, so most men who have been in a battle do not have one; and a shared
# memory with the specific man commanding the other host today is rarer still.
bc_he_has_one_of_these_already_written_in_him_trigger = {
	any_memory = {
		has_memory_category = battle
	}
}

bc_he_shares_an_afternoon_with_the_man_opposite_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		any_memory = {
			has_memory_participant = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}


# --- what the house bought before he was born ---------------------------------
#
# `has_dynasty_perk` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. A hundred and twenty-four
# batches have read a man's name, his house, his feuds, his marriages and the
# quarrel his grandfather started, and not one of them has ever read the other
# thing a dynasty is: A LIST OF PURCHASES. Legacies are renown spent by people
# who are dead on advantages that arrive, unasked and unearned, in the hands of
# whoever happens to be standing in a field today.
#
# THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP gives `has_dynasty_perk` as "Does the dynasty have this
# dynasty perk?", and its supported scope is DYNASTY, not character. It must
# therefore be read inside `dynasty = { }`, which is an optional link, which is
# why both gates carry the standing guard - the `exists = dynasty` as a sibling
# and the read nested in a trigger_if that repeats it. AGOT writes exactly this
# shape at `common/accolade_types/04_ep2_common_attributes.txt:36-42`: an
# `exists = dynasty` and then `dynasty = { has_dynasty_perk = warfare_legacy_3 }`.
#
# THE TWO PERKS ARE REAL AND THEIR MODIFIERS ARE THE POINT.
# `common/dynasty_perks/00_dynasty_perks.txt:74` gives `warfare_legacy_1`, House
# of Warriors, `prowess = 2` and `knight_effectiveness_mult = 0.15`; line 94
# gives `warfare_legacy_2`, Generational Belligerence, `pursue_efficiency = 0.15`
# and `retreat_losses = -0.15`. Those are not decorations. The first makes the
# man in the rank measurably better at the thing he is doing this minute, and
# the second makes a bad afternoon cost fewer men to leave. Both descs are
# written off the modifier and not off the flavour name.
#
# THE TWO GATES READ TWO DIFFERENT PURCHASES ON TWO DIFFERENT MEN, so neither
# carries a negation, on the same reasoning as the diarchy and memory pairs. The
# perks sit on the same track and the second implies the first, but that
# implication is about ONE dynasty, and these two gates are never read on the
# same character - the side commander is not in the knight list and the pools
# roll separately.
#
# NEITHER IS A COMPETENCE GATE. `prowess` and `martial` are read all over this
# mod and they say what a man is. This says what was bought for him, which is a
# different fact with a different flavour of unfairness attached, and the two
# are independent: a magnificent soldier may be the first of his line to matter,
# and a poor one may be standing inside four generations of expenditure.
#
# RARITY. A legacy rung costs renown that most houses spend elsewhere, and AGOT
# adds ten regional tracks on top of vanilla's seven, so the warfare track is one
# option among many rather than the obvious one. Then the man has to be in a
# battle.
bc_his_house_paid_for_the_arm_he_is_using_trigger = {
	exists = dynasty
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = dynasty }
		dynasty = {
			has_dynasty_perk = warfare_legacy_1
		}
	}
}

bc_his_house_paid_for_getting_home_trigger = {
	exists = dynasty
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = dynasty }
		dynasty = {
			has_dynasty_perk = warfare_legacy_2
		}
	}
}


# --- the people in his hall who are not his -----------------------------------
#
# THE GUEST SYSTEM RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. `any_foreign_court_guest`
# is zero, `is_foreign_court_guest` is zero, and so are the pool-guest forms. A
# hundred and twenty-five batches have read who a man is related to, who he
# employs, who he owes, who wants him dead and what his house bought before he
# was born, and not one of them has read the simplest social fact about a
# household: THAT SOME OF THE PEOPLE IN IT BELONG TO SOMEBODY ELSE.
#
# THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP is explicit about the distinction and it is the distinction
# that makes this worth reading. `is_foreign_court_guest` - "Is the character a
# guest from another court? In contrast to is_pool_guest the character has a
# liege", yes/no, character scope. `any_foreign_court_guest` - "Iterate through
# all guests visiting from another court (in contrast to pool_guest they have a
# liege)", character scope, character target. A POOL GUEST IS NOBODY'S. A
# FOREIGN COURT GUEST IS SOMEBODY'S, and the somebody is not the man whose bread
# he is eating. That is the whole moment in both halves.
#
# BOTH ARE LIVE AND UNGUARDED IN AGOT. `is_foreign_court_guest = yes` at
# `common/character_interactions/00_courtier_and_guest_interactions.txt:646` and
# `00_agot_lmf_interactions.txt:2914`; `any_foreign_court_guest` at
# `00_diarch_interactions.txt:1425` and `00_prison_interactions.txt:108`, both
# of which vanilla also carries at the matching lines.
#
# NO GUARDS ON EITHER. The first is an iterator, and iterators are not links -
# an empty hall is a clean no. The second is a plain yes/no read on the scoped
# character with no link traversed at all.
#
# THE TWO GATES READ TWO DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT MEN, so neither
# carries a negation, on the same reasoning as the diarchy, memory and legacy
# pairs. The first asks whether the man commanding here has strangers under his
# roof. The second asks whether the man standing in this rank IS one, in
# somebody else's. The engine keeps them apart structurally: the side commander
# is not in the knight list and the pools roll separately.
#
# THIS IS NOT A COURT SIZE GATE AND NOT A HOSPITALITY GATE. It does not count
# courtiers, it does not read wealth, grandeur or a royal court, and it says
# nothing about how many people are in a hall. It reads one thing only, which is
# whether any of them will be going home to report.
#
# RARITY. Guests arrive through a mechanic that most halls in most years are not
# hosting, and the second half needs the guest to have been picked as a knight
# on top of that.
bc_there_are_strangers_eating_in_his_hall_trigger = {
	any_foreign_court_guest = { count >= 1 }
}

bc_he_is_a_guest_in_the_hall_he_is_fighting_for_trigger = {
	is_foreign_court_guest = yes
}


# --- how much of this is bought and how much is owed --------------------------
#
# THE MEN-AT-ARMS SYSTEM RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD. `any_maa_regiment`
# zero, `maa_regiments_count` zero, `maa_regiments_max_count` zero,
# `number_of_maa` zero. A hundred and twenty-six batches have read the size of a
# host, the ground under it, the weather on it and the war around it, and not
# one of them has ever read WHAT KIND OF ARMY IT IS. That is arguably the oldest
# question there is about a medieval field and the mod has never asked it.
#
# THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP gives `maa_regiments_count` as "Current number of personal
# MaA regiments", character scope, with the full set of comparators. It is live
# and unguarded in AGOT at `common/character_interactions/00_alliance.txt:3525`
# and `03_fp2_interactions.txt:3352`, at lines vanilla matches, and AGOT also
# reads it in the dotted cross-value form at `06_ep3_laamp_interactions.txt:3142`.
#
# THE MOD USES THE PLAIN THRESHOLD FORM AND NOT THE CROSS-VALUE FORM. Comparing
# `maa_regiments_count` against `maa_regiments_max_count` would be a prettier
# gate - "he has bought everything his rank allows" - but doing it correctly
# needs a dotted scope on both sides, and inside a scripted trigger there is no
# guarantee about what `root` is. Two literal thresholds say almost the same
# thing and cannot be wrong.
#
# `>= 4` AND `= 0`, AND BOTH NUMBERS ARE CHOSEN RATHER THAN CONVENIENT. The
# personal regiment cap runs with title tier, so four standing companies means a
# man of duke's rank or better who has actually spent the money, which is not
# most raisers. Zero means a host with no professional core at all - levy,
# household and whatever the knights brought - which is exactly what a poor
# lord, a landless captain or an over-extended heir turns up with.
#
# THE FIRST TAKES NO GUARD, being a plain value read on the scoped character.
# The second walks `scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander` and takes the
# standing one, with the `exists` as a sibling and the read nested in a
# trigger_if that repeats it.
#
# THIS IS NOT AN ODDS GATE AND NOT A WEALTH GATE. `bc_their_host_gap_value` and
# the side-size rows count men; `gold` and the debt gates read a purse. This
# reads composition - the ratio of contract to obligation in a body of men - and
# it is independent of both: a large host may be entirely levy, a small one
# entirely professional, and a man in debt may be in debt precisely because he
# bought companies.
#
# THE SECOND IS NOT RUMOUR AND DOES NOT NEED A CAMP TO REPORT IT. What a levy
# looks like from a hundred paces is the single most legible fact on any
# medieval field. Nothing matches, nobody keeps step, and the front rank is
# holding whatever was in the barn.
#
# RARITY. Four regiments is money on top of rank. Zero is real but not universal
# among the men who end up commanding a side. Neither fires for most commanders,
# and they are read on different men in different pools, so neither carries a
# negation of the other.
bc_the_expensive_part_of_this_army_is_his_trigger = {
	maa_regiments_count >= 4
}

bc_there_is_nothing_professional_opposite_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			maa_regiments_count = 0
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 128 - THE MONEY THAT LEFT BEFORE THE WAR DID, AND THE OTHER TRADE.
#
# A hundred and twenty-seven batches have read what a man commands, what he
# owns, who he owes, what is under his feet and what the weather is doing to
# it. None of them has ever read what he has PAID SOMEBODY TO MAKE. The census
# is clean: `inspiration` returns zero over the whole mod, in every form -
# `any_sponsored_inspiration`, `has_completed_inspiration`, `any_inspiration`,
# `can_sponsor_inspiration`.
#
# THE DUMP. `any_sponsored_inspiration` is "Iterate through all sponsored
# inspirations", character scope, target inspiration, standard `count`
# comparators. `has_completed_inspiration` is "Checks if the scoped character
# has ever completed an inspiration", character scope, yes/no. AGOT runs both
# live: the iterator with `count >= 3` and `count >= 2` in the fund-an-artisan
# interaction at `common/character_interactions/02_ep1_interactions.txt:188`
# and `:197`, and again at `00_prison_interactions.txt:8525`; the boolean at
# `00_agot_lmf_interactions.txt:3332` and `:3410`, and in the court-position
# requirements at `common/court_positions/types/00_agot_court_positions.txt:3264`.
# AGOT ships its own `common/inspirations/00_inspirations.txt`, so the system
# is not a dead vanilla surface here - it is furnished and running.
#
# NEITHER GATE TAKES A GUARD, FOR TWO DIFFERENT REASONS. The first is an
# iterator, and an iterator over an empty list is a clean no; iterators are not
# links. The second is a plain boolean read on the character already in scope,
# with no dotted step in it at all. Nothing in this block reaches through an
# optional link, so nothing in this block needs `exists`.
#
# TWO KEYWORDS, TWO FACTS, TWO MEN. The first is money going out of a purse for
# a thing that is not finished. The second is a pair of hands that once
# finished one. They are not two thresholds on one number and they are not the
# same question asked from either end - a man may be paying for four workshops
# and have never made anything, and a man may have built the finest thing in a
# kingdom and be too poor to fund a nail. So neither carries a negation of the
# other, and the engine keeps them apart anyway: the first is read on the side
# commander, the second on a knight in the line, and the two pools roll
# separately.
#
# THIS IS NOT A WEALTH GATE AND NOT A STATION GATE. `gold`, the debt rows and
# the men-at-arms row of batch 127 all read the size or the shape of what he
# can spend. This reads what he has already spent it ON, and specifically on
# something with no military value whatever, commissioned in peace, still
# unfinished. That is the whole point of it: it is the one line of his ledger
# that this afternoon cannot reach.
#
# RARITY. Sponsorship costs real gold and AGOT's own AI actively throttles it -
# the interaction above subtracts fifty from the will-do at three concurrent
# works for an emperor and two for a king, which is the game telling us in its
# own hand that even the richest men in Westeros are running two or three of
# these at most, and most men are running none. A completed inspiration is
# rarer still: it requires the whole arc to have run to its end on that
# specific character. Neither fires for most men on most fields.
bc_he_is_paying_for_something_being_made_at_home_trigger = {
	any_sponsored_inspiration = {
		count >= 1
	}
}

bc_he_has_finished_a_thing_with_his_own_hands_trigger = {
	has_completed_inspiration = yes
}

# BATCH 129 - THE DOORS AT HOME, AND THE ONE THAT IS ALREADY SHUT.
#
# The mod reads captivity from exactly one direction and always has. Batch 118
# read hostages, `is_hostage` occurring ten times; the imprisonment-reason pair
# above reads whether the LAW would let one of these two men keep the other.
# Nothing anywhere reads whether either of them is ALREADY keeping somebody.
# The census is clean: `any_prisoner` zero, `num_of_prisoners` zero,
# `is_imprisoned` zero, `any_close_family_member` zero.
#
# THE DUMP. `any_prisoner` is "Iterate through all prisoners", character scope,
# target character. `any_close_family_member` is "Iterate through all the close
# family [father, mother, siblings, children, grandparents]", character scope.
# `is_imprisoned` is "is the character imprisoned?", character scope, yes/no.
# AGOT runs all three, and runs the second and third in exactly the nested form
# used here: `common/casus_belli_types/00_agot_rv_rescue_wars.txt:36` has
# `any_close_family_member = { OR = { is_imprisoned = yes ... } }` and `:111`
# has `any_prisoner`. `00_religious_war.txt:2106` iterates prisoners with an
# inner check as well. This is AGOT's own rescue-war arithmetic, borrowed.
#
# NEITHER GATE TAKES A GUARD. Both are iterators, and an iterator over an empty
# list is a clean no. Iterators are not links. There is no dotted step anywhere
# in this block.
#
# TWO KEYWORDS, TWO FACTS, TWO MEN, AND THE TWO FACTS ARE OPPOSITE ENDS OF THE
# SAME DOOR. The first is a man who is holding somebody. The second is a man
# somebody is holding. Neither implies the other and neither is a threshold on
# the other's number - a gaoler's own family may be entirely at liberty, and a
# man with a brother in a cell may keep nobody at all - so neither carries a
# negation of the other, and the engine keeps them apart regardless: the first
# is read on the side commander, the second on a knight in the line, and the
# two pools roll separately.
#
# WHY TWO AND NOT ONE. A single prisoner is an accident - a skirmish that went
# well, a guest who said the wrong thing, a debt that ran out of patience. Two
# or more at once is a different kind of fact about a household: it means
# somebody is running a gaol, with a man to carry the bread and a door somebody
# has to check, and it means the arithmetic of ransom is a live and familiar
# part of his year rather than a thing that happened to him once.
#
# RARITY. Most lords in Westeros hold nobody at all on most days; prisoners
# arrive from fields and sieges and leave again as soon as a price is agreed,
# so the state is transient by design. Close family is a bounded list of at most
# a dozen people, and at any given moment nearly all of them are at liberty.
# Neither of these fires for most men on most fields.
bc_he_is_keeping_men_in_his_own_cells_trigger = {
	any_prisoner = {
		count >= 2
	}
}

bc_somebody_of_his_blood_is_in_a_cell_tonight_trigger = {
	any_close_family_member = {
		is_imprisoned = yes
	}
}

# BATCH 130 - THE TWO RELATIONS AGOT HIDES ON PURPOSE.
#
# The marriage axis opened at batch 88 and has been worked several times since -
# the pregnancy row, the two burial rows, the married-into-the-other-side row -
# and every one of them reads the marriage as an ARRANGEMENT: who it joined,
# what it produced, whether it ended. None of them has ever read what it is
# like. AGOT keeps two scripted relations for exactly that and the mod had never
# touched either: `unfruitful_marriage` zero, `kicked_from_marriage_bed` zero,
# `primary_spouse` zero.
#
# BOTH ARE AGOT'S OWN AND BOTH ARE `hidden = yes`. They sit at
# `common/scripted_relations/00_agot_scripted_relations.txt:15` and `:52`, each
# reciprocal with itself, and the first carries `fertility = -5`, which is the
# mod telling us in its own hand what it means by it. Being hidden, neither ever
# appears in the character interface - they are facts the game keeps and never
# shows, which is precisely the register this file wants. AGOT sets the first in
# `common/on_action/marriage_concubinage.txt:189` and reads both live:
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_lmf_triggers.txt:425` and `:427`, and
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_spouse_events.txt:5132` reads the second
# against `primary_spouse`, which is exactly the form used here.
#
# BOTH GATES TAKE THE GUARD. `primary_spouse` is an optional link and reading it
# as a TARGET is a read, so each gate carries `exists = primary_spouse` as a
# sibling and repeats it in a `trigger_if` limit before the relation is asked.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF ANY SHIPPED MARRIAGE GATE. Batch 88's header already
# records why a bare `is_married = yes` is not a gate - it is nearly every
# landed man in the game - and no such gate was ever shipped. The rows that
# exist require a pregnancy, or a dead former spouse, or a marriage across the
# battle line, and none of those is implied by either relation here.
#
# TWO FACTS, TWO MEN, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME UNHAPPINESS. The first is a
# marriage that is still a marriage and has produced nothing. The second is a
# marriage that has stopped being one in the only room where that is measured.
# A man may have either without the other, so neither carries a negation of the
# other, and the pools roll separately regardless.
#
# RARITY. Both relations are set by specific AGOT events on specific
# characters - one from a long marriage that has stayed empty, the other from a
# particular kind of night - and neither is a demographic category. Most married
# men in Westeros have neither.
bc_the_marriage_has_produced_nothing_trigger = {
	exists = primary_spouse
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = primary_spouse }
		has_relation_unfruitful_marriage = primary_spouse
	}
}

bc_he_does_not_sleep_in_that_room_any_more_trigger = {
	exists = primary_spouse
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = primary_spouse }
		has_relation_kicked_from_marriage_bed = primary_spouse
	}
}

# BATCH 131 - HOW MUCH APPETITE EACH OF THEM ACTUALLY HAS.
#
# The whole AI-personality surface returned zero over the mod: `ai_boldness`,
# `ai_energy`, `ai_zeal`, `ai_greed`, `ai_compassion`, `ai_honor`,
# `ai_rationality`, `ai_vengefulness`, `ai_sociability`. A hundred and thirty
# batches have read traits, skills, stress, dread, opinion and tyranny, and not
# one of them has read the number the engine itself uses to decide whether a man
# will risk something.
#
# THE DUMP IS WRONG ABOUT THE SECOND ONE AND THE LIVE FILES ARE RIGHT.
# `dread_modified_ai_boldness` is documented in 1.19.0.6 as
# `dread_modified_ai_boldness = { character = root value >= 5 }`. That parameter
# name does not appear in a single live file. Vanilla writes `dreaded_character`
# sixty-seven times - `common/scripted_effects/00_interaction_effects.txt:733`
# has `dreaded_character = scope:actor` with `value <= -50` under the comment
# "Too scared to do anything" - and AGOT writes it too, at
# `common/character_interactions/00_agot_spy_network_interactions.txt:421` with
# `value < -20` and again at `:426` with `value < -45`. The live form is used
# here. This is the second time the dump has been the less reliable of the two
# and it is worth the note: the shipped files are the authority.
#
# THE SCALE IS SIGNED, NOT ZERO-TO-A-HUNDRED. Vanilla's own
# `common/script_values/00_ai_values.txt:25` sets `high_positive_ai_value = 50`,
# and the dread-modified reads above go to `-20`, `-45`, `-50`. So the range is
# roughly minus a hundred to a hundred with nothing in the middle, and the
# thresholds below are chosen against vanilla's own constants rather than
# invented.
#
# BOTH GATES ARE ROOT-INDEPENDENT ON PURPOSE. Batch 127's header records why:
# inside a scripted trigger there is no guarantee what `root` is. Every
# character here is named through `scope:combat_side`, which the knight
# templates already rely on to reach their own commander, so both gates read
# the same men whichever pool called them.
#
# BOTH TAKE THE GUARD, AND THE FIRST TAKES TWO. `side_commander` and
# `enemy_side.side_commander` are optional links, and naming one as the target
# of `dreaded_character` is a read, so every `exists` is repeated inside the
# `trigger_if` limit before anything is asked.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE DREAD ROWS. The shipped dread reads are all `dread` on the
# scoped man himself - how frightening he is in general, to everybody. This
# asks what that frightfulness has actually DONE to one specific person's
# willingness to gamble today, which is a different number and can be small
# while his dread is enormous, or large while it is nothing.
#
# TWO FACTS, TWO MEN. The first is the far commander's appetite once his fear of
# the man opposite has been subtracted from it. The second is the plain,
# unmodified appetite of the knight's OWN commander - not fear at all, just what
# kind of man is about to give him an order. Neither implies the other and
# neither is a threshold on the other's number, so neither carries a negation.
#
# RARITY. A signed scale with the mass in the middle means both tails are thin:
# most men sit between the two thresholds, and the first needs a real dread
# differential between two specific people on top of that.
bc_the_man_opposite_has_no_appetite_for_this_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			dread_modified_ai_boldness = {
				dreaded_character = scope:combat_side.side_commander
				value <= -20
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_the_man_leading_him_is_a_gambler_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			ai_boldness >= high_positive_ai_value
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 132 - THE TWO STORIES A MAN CAN BE CARRYING ONTO A FIELD.
#
# `any_owned_story` returned zero over the whole mod. AGOT ships fifty-nine
# story-cycle files and the mod had never asked whether either man in front of
# it is in the middle of one.
#
# THE DUMP. `any_owned_story` is "Iterate through all owned stories for a
# character", character scope, target story, and it takes an optional type
# filter. `story_type` is "Is the story in scope of this type?", story scope.
# AGOT reads exactly this form: `any_owned_story = { story_type = story_wear_face
# ... }` at `common/character_interactions/00_agot_religious_interactions.txt:449`
# and again in `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_general_on_actions.txt:35`,
# and the loan cycle is filtered the same way at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_banking_effects.txt:2130`.
#
# NEITHER GATE TAKES A GUARD. Both are iterators and an iterator over an empty
# list is a clean no.
#
# ONE KEYWORD, TWO STORIES, TWO MEN, AND THE STORIES HAVE NOTHING TO DO WITH
# EACH OTHER. This is the terrain-band pattern rather than the two-keyword
# pattern: the same iterator asked about two entirely unrelated cycles. Neither
# is a threshold on the other and neither implies the other, so neither carries
# a negation.
#
# THE FIRST IS NOT THE DEBT ROWS. Every shipped money gate reads `gold`, which
# is a purse - what is in his hands at this moment. A live loan is the opposite
# state: a man who has just borrowed is CARRYING coin, and may read as wealthy
# on every gate this mod already ships, while owing more than he has. The two
# can be true together or separately and neither implies the other.
#
# THE SECOND IS NOT THE NICKNAME ROW OR THE SQUIRE ROW. Those ask what the line
# knows about a man who is who he says he is. This asks about a man who is not,
# and AGOT keeps that in a story rather than a trait precisely because it is a
# process with a beginning and an end.
#
# RARITY. Loans are taken by lords who have run out of coin and are settled or
# defaulted within a few years, so the state is real but transient and far from
# universal. The second is rare by construction - it requires a very specific
# and very expensive sequence to have happened to a very specific kind of
# person - and it is meant to be. A line in which nobody is wearing somebody
# else's face is the ordinary case, and the point of the row is the afternoon
# where that is not true.
bc_he_borrowed_to_pay_for_this_trigger = {
	any_owned_story = {
		story_type = story_agot_loan
	}
}

bc_the_face_in_the_line_is_not_his_own_trigger = {
	any_owned_story = {
		story_type = story_wear_face
	}
}

# BATCH 133 - WHO IS LEADING THE COLUMN AND WHO IS ONLY IN IT.
#
# `is_travelling`, `is_travel_leader`, `any_travel_plan` and
# `is_travel_entourage_character` all returned zero across the whole mod. That
# is a large silence: travel is one of the few states in this game that a
# character can be in for months at a stretch, and the mod has three dozen gates
# about what a man owns, whom he is related to and what he believes, and not one
# about whether he is at this moment a long way from where he sleeps.
#
# The pair splits the same road two ways, which is the point. A travelling party
# has exactly one leader and any number of people riding in his train, and the
# two are not the same experience of the same journey. The man at the head of it
# chose the route, set the pace, and is answerable for everyone behind him. The
# men behind him are baggage with opinions.
#
# THEY ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The engine is explicit that the travel
# leader is himself part of the entourage - `is_travel_entourage_character` is
# documented as "is the character travelling and part of the entourage (travel
# leader is part of the entourage)". So the second gate would be a strict
# superset of the first if it were left alone, and it is not left alone: it
# carries `is_travel_leader = no` as a sibling, which cuts the leader back out.
# A man passes exactly one of these two or neither. He cannot pass both.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are plain booleans on the character in scope, not links, and a
# man who is not travelling at all is simply a clean no on each.
#
# THESE ARE NOT THE HOME AND CAPITAL ROWS. Those ask where a man's holdings are
# and whether he is fighting near them, which is a fact about geography that is
# true of him whether he is standing in his own hall or two kingdoms away. This
# asks whether he is in motion - whether there is a column somewhere with his
# tents on it that expects to be somewhere else by the end of the week. A man
# can be fighting on his own doorstep and still be mid-journey, and a man can be
# very far from home and perfectly stationary.
#
# RARITY. Travel is a fraction of any character's life; most men on most days
# are where they live. Of those who are travelling, exactly one per party leads
# it, and the entourage row further excludes him. Neither gate is anywhere near
# universal, and both describe a genuinely unusual thing to be doing on the
# morning a battle starts.
bc_he_is_the_one_the_column_follows_trigger = {
	is_travel_leader = yes
}

bc_he_is_riding_in_another_mans_train_trigger = {
	is_travel_entourage_character = yes
	is_travel_leader = no
}

# BATCH 134 - WHAT THE CROWN ON THIS SIDE IS ACTUALLY WORTH.
#
# `legitimacy`, `legitimacy_level`, `has_legitimacy` and `has_legitimacy_flag`
# all returned zero across the whole mod. AGOT uses the system - two hundred and
# eighty-three of its files mention it, and it reads `legitimacy_level` against
# 0, 1 and 2 in its own activity files - and this mod has never once asked the
# single question the system exists to answer: whether the men around a crowned
# man think the crown is his by right.
#
# `has_legitimacy = yes` IS NOT DECORATION ON THE FIRST GATE, IT IS THE GATE.
# `legitimacy_level` returns 0 for a character who does not use legitimacy at
# all, which is nearly everybody in a battle line. Written alone, `<= 1` would
# be true of every landless knight, every household guard and every hedge
# swordsman on the field - a gate that fires for nearly everybody is not a gate.
# The boolean cuts the population down to men the game actually scores, and the
# threshold then does the work it was written to do. Do not delete it as
# redundant. It is the reason the row is rare.
#
# THE TWO GATES READ TWO DIFFERENT MEN, AND THE NEGATION IS DELIBERATELY
# OMITTED. The first is the man wearing the thin crown. The second is a man
# standing in the line who has noticed that the person he is following wears
# one. Commander and knight roll from separate pools, so neither can suppress
# the other, and on the afternoon when both are true they are true about
# different people and describe genuinely different afternoons.
#
# THE SECOND IS ROOT-INDEPENDENT ON PURPOSE, for the reason batch 127 recorded:
# `root` is not guaranteed inside a scripted trigger, so the man being asked
# about is named through `scope:combat_side` rather than assumed. The knight
# templates already reach their own commander that way in every effect they run.
# `side_commander` is an optional link and takes the guard.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE DREAD ROWS OR THE OPINION ROWS. Dread is how frightening a man
# is. Opinion is how a specific person feels about him. Legitimacy is neither -
# it is whether the office he holds is believed in, which can be catastrophic
# while he is personally both feared and liked, and can be immaculate on a man
# nobody can stand. The three move independently and the mod already reads two
# of them.
#
# RARITY. The first requires a legitimacy-using ruler at the bottom two levels
# of the scale, which is a crown in visible trouble rather than an ordinary one.
# The second requires that man to be leading the side a knight is standing in.
# Neither is close to universal.
bc_the_crown_he_wears_is_thin_trigger = {
	has_legitimacy = yes
	legitimacy_level <= 1
}

bc_the_man_he_follows_wears_a_thin_crown_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			has_legitimacy = yes
			legitimacy_level <= 1
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 135 - WHICH DIRECTION THE LAST PEACE RAN.
#
# `any_truce_holder` and `any_truce_target` both returned zero across the whole
# mod. That is a real omission, because a truce is the only object in this game
# that is a durable, dated record of a war somebody LOST - it exists precisely
# because two men stopped, and it remembers which of them had to. A hundred and
# thirty-four batches have read wars, grudges, dread, opinion and kin, and not
# one of them has read the small legal residue of the last time this went badly
# for somebody.
#
# THE PAIR IS THE SAME OBJECT READ FROM ITS TWO ENDS, AND THAT IS THE POINT. A
# truce has a winner and a loser. `any_truce_target` iterates the men this
# character holds one over - the people who are not allowed to come for him,
# because he beat them recently enough that the game is still counting. Its
# mirror `any_truce_holder` iterates the men who hold one over HIM, which is the
# same fact told the other way round: he is the one who stopped.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE OTHER AND NEITHER CARRIES A NEGATION. They point in
# opposite directions, and a man who has fought on both sides of his career can
# be in both lists at once without either statement becoming less true. The two
# gates also read two different men - a commander and a knight, from pools that
# roll separately - so on the afternoon they are both true they are true about
# different people, which is the same reasoning batches 110, 113, 118, 120 and
# everything from 123 onward record.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are iterators, and an iterator over an empty list is a clean
# no. Nothing here is an optional link.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE WAR ROWS. Those ask about the war he is standing in - how long
# it has run, who declared it, what it is for. This asks about a DIFFERENT war,
# one that is already over, and about the paperwork it left behind. A man can be
# in the first year of a fresh war and still be carrying three truces from the
# last one, and a man can have fought for a decade without ever generating a
# single one, because truces come from settlements and not from fighting.
#
# RARITY. A truce requires a concluded war with a surrender in it and expires on
# a timer, so the state is real, dated and transient. Most men in most lines have
# neither list populated.
bc_men_who_may_not_come_for_him_yet_trigger = {
	any_truce_target = {
		count >= 1
	}
}

bc_somebody_still_holds_his_surrender_trigger = {
	any_truce_holder = {
		count >= 1
	}
}

# BATCH 136 - WHICH OF THE TWO SIDES DID NOT COME ALONE.
#
# `any_war_ally` returned zero across the whole mod. A hundred and thirty-five
# batches have read this war's length, its cause, its declarer and its stakes,
# and not one of them has asked the simplest question a man can ask standing in
# a line and looking left: is everybody here for the same reason, or has
# somebody else's banner turned up with its own arithmetic attached.
#
# THE TWO GATES READ THE TWO SIDES, NOT THE TWO MEN. This is the first pair in
# the mod where the distinction between them is which ARMY is being asked about
# rather than which character. The first asks whether the side the scoped man is
# standing in has allies in it; the second asks whether the side opposite does.
# Both are read through `scope:combat_side`, which makes them role-agnostic -
# nothing here is a property only a commander has, so either file may call
# either gate, and a knight reaches them the same way he already reaches his own
# side's primary participant in the hook rows above.
#
# THEY ARE NOT MIRRORS AND NEITHER IMPLIES THE OTHER. Wars are frequently
# lopsided: one side is a coalition and the other is one furious man. Both can
# be coalitions, and both can be nobody, and a side's ally count says nothing
# whatever about the other side's. No negation is needed and none is written.
#
# BOTH TAKE THE GUARD. `side_primary_participant` is an optional link and the
# second walks two of them, so its `exists` rows come in a pair, exactly as
# batch 131's dread-modified gate does.
#
# `any_war_ally` IS AN ITERATOR INSIDE AN ENTERED SCOPE, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME
# AS AN ITERATOR AT THE TOP LEVEL. The iterator itself needs no guard, but the
# scope it runs inside is a link and that link does.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE HOST-SIZE ROWS OR THE OUTNUMBERED ROWS. Those count men.
# Allies are not men - a side with four allies can be smaller than a side with
# none, and the fact that matters here is not the size of the line but that some
# part of it belongs to somebody who can go home whenever he decides the price
# has been paid.
#
# RARITY. Most wars in this game are two people. Alliances are called in
# deliberately and cost something to call, so a side that has one is a side
# where somebody has already spent a favour to be standing here.
bc_this_side_did_not_come_alone_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_primary_participant
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_primary_participant }
		scope:combat_side.side_primary_participant = {
			any_war_ally = {
				count >= 1
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_the_other_side_did_not_come_alone_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_primary_participant
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_primary_participant
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_primary_participant = {
			any_war_ally = {
				count >= 1
			}
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 137 - WHAT EACH OF THEM IS OWED ON PAPER.
#
# `any_claim`, `has_claim_on` and `is_claimant` all returned zero across the
# whole mod. That is a surprising gap in a setting built almost entirely out of
# claims. A hundred and thirty-six batches have read what a man holds, what he
# owes, whom he hates and where he sleeps, and not one has read the thing this
# entire war is legally about: a piece of parchment saying something ought to
# have been his.
#
# THE TWO READS SPLIT ON `pressed`, WHICH IS THE ONLY DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS.
# A pressed claim is one somebody is currently fighting over - it is in a war
# aim, it has men dying for it today. An unpressed claim is the same parchment
# with nobody acting on it: a thing that is true, and filed, and doing nothing.
# The engine keeps both under one iterator and separates them with a parameter,
# and vanilla uses both values - `pressed = yes` at
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_courtier_guest_management_triggers.txt:643` and
# `pressed = no` at `common/script_values/destiny_score_interest_value.txt:473`.
# AGOT ships the same lines.
#
# NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF THE OTHER AND NEITHER NEEDS A NEGATION. They are two
# disjoint filters over one list, but a man can be in both results at once,
# holding one claim that men are dying for today and three more that nobody has
# mentioned in twenty years. Both statements stay true and describe different
# afternoons.
#
# THE THRESHOLDS ARE NOT THE SAME NUMBER AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. One pressed
# claim is already unusual - it requires an active war whose aim is that title.
# Unpressed claims accumulate: they arrive by inheritance, marriage and grant
# and they never expire on their own, so one of them is nearly ambient among
# highborn men. Three is the number that makes the second gate mean what its
# name says, which is a man carrying a folder rather than a man carrying a
# grievance. A gate that fires for nearly everybody is not a gate.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are iterators over the scoped character's own list, and an
# empty list is a clean no. Nothing here walks a link.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE WAR ROWS OR THE INHERITANCE ROWS. The war rows ask about the
# war he is standing in from the outside - its length, its cause, its declarer.
# This asks what is written in his own name whether or not anybody is currently
# doing anything about it, and the second gate specifically asks about the
# titles nobody is doing anything about, which is the opposite of a war.
bc_something_of_his_is_being_fought_for_trigger = {
	any_claim = {
		pressed = yes
		count >= 1
	}
}

bc_he_is_owed_things_nobody_is_fighting_for_trigger = {
	any_claim = {
		pressed = no
		count >= 3
	}
}

# BATCH 138 - WHETHER THIS PARTICULAR FIELD ALREADY BELONGED TO SOMEBODY.
#
# `is_holy_site`, `is_holy_site_of` and `has_holy_site_parameter` all returned
# zero across the whole mod. The ground triggers have asked what the field is
# made of since plan 1 - hills, bog, forest, the six bands - and never once asked
# what it MEANS. In this setting that is a real omission: men fight on ground
# that other men have walked to on purpose, and the difference between a hillside
