# and a hillside somebody's grandmother was buried under is not a terrain type.
#
# THE PAIR IS DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND THE NEGATION IS LOAD-BEARING. Both
# gates require the field to be a holy site. The first then requires that it is
# NOT holy to the man leading this side, and the second requires that it is. Read
# the first one's `NOT` before deleting it: without it the second gate is a
# strict subset of the first, which is precisely the collision batch 72 shipped
# by accident and the reason this file checks for it now.
#
# THE FAITH IS READ OFF THE SIDE'S COMMANDER, NOT OFF `root`. That is batch 127's
# lesson and batch 131's preferred fix - `root` is not guaranteed inside a
# scripted trigger, so the man whose faith matters is named through
# `scope:combat_side` and the gates work identically whichever file calls them.
# It also makes the pair mean something slightly better than "his" faith would:
# what is being asked is whether the army standing on this ground has any
# business being here, and an army's business is its commander's.
#
# `is_holy_site_of` IS A BARONY TRIGGER AND `combat.location` IS A PROVINCE, SO
# THE `barony` WALK IS REQUIRED. `barony` is documented as province-to-title and
# every province in the game has one, so it is not an optional link.
#
# `combat.location` IS READ WITHOUT A GUARD, WHICH MATCHES EVERY SHIPPED GROUND
# GATE IN THIS FILE. A combat always has a location; the terrain pair above has
# read it bare since plan 1 and it was proven live in probe 4. `side_commander`
# is a different matter - it is genuinely optional and both gates guard it.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE TERRAIN ROWS OR THE BANDS. Those are about footing and what it
# costs to move. Holiness is not a property of the dirt, it is a property of what
# people have decided about the dirt, and the two are completely uncorrelated - a
# holy site can sit on any of the six bands, and the overwhelming majority of
# every band is not one.
#
# RARITY. Holy sites are a fixed and small list per faith, placed by hand on a
# map of thousands of baronies. A battle landing on one is uncommon, and a battle
# landing on one that belongs to the faith of the man leading the side is rarer
# still.
bc_this_ground_is_holy_to_somebody_else_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
		barony = {
			is_holy_site = yes
		}
	}
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		NOT = {
			scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
				barony = {
					is_holy_site_of = scope:combat_side.side_commander.faith
				}
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_this_ground_is_holy_to_his_own_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			barony = {
				is_holy_site_of = scope:combat_side.side_commander.faith
			}
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 139 - THE HOUSEHOLD THAT IS NOT THE MARRIAGE.
#
# `any_concubine`, `any_former_concubine`, `any_child_of_concubine`,
# `is_concubine` and `allowed_concubines` all returned zero across the whole
# mod. Batch 130 opened the marriage axis and read the two things AGOT hides
# inside it, and stopped there. Everything a man's household contains that is
# NOT his marriage has been invisible since plan 1, in a setting that has salt
# wives, paramours and a dynasty which practised the arrangement for three
# hundred years.
#
# THE PAIR IS DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND THE `count = 0` ROW IS THE REASON.
# The first is a man who has one now. The second is a man who does not have one
# now and had one before, and the second says so in its own body rather than
# relying on the first not firing. Without that row the two lists overlap
# heavily - a man commonly holds a current arrangement and a lapsed one at the
# same time - and the second gate stops meaning what its name says. It is not
# defensive clutter. It is the whole distinction.
#
# `any_former_concubine` IS DOCUMENTED AS NOT PERSISTING PAST DEATH, WHICH IS
# EXACTLY THE PROPERTY THIS WANTS. The list is a fact about a living man's own
# history, not an inherited record, so the second gate cannot fire on somebody
# who merely inherited a household. Vanilla reads it at
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_birth_triggers.txt:84` and in its pregnancy
# events; `any_concubine` is read by AGOT itself at
# `common/on_action/death.txt:1945` and `common/scripted_effects/
# 00_agot_lmf_effects.txt:106`.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are iterators over the scoped character's own lists, and an
# empty list is a clean no. Nothing here walks a link.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE MARRIAGE ROWS AND IT IS NOT THE KIN ROWS. Batch 130's two
# gates read the state of a marriage through `primary_spouse` - whether it has
# produced anything, whether he still sleeps in that room - and both require a
# marriage to exist. Neither of these does. A man can pass either of these with
# no wife at all, and can pass batch 130's with no concubine ever. The kin rows
# count relatives; these count an arrangement, which is not a relative and is
# not a marriage and has its own end.
#
# RARITY. The arrangement requires a faith that permits it, which is a minority
# of the map, and then requires the man to have actually made one. The second is
# rarer than the first, being the first plus an ending.
bc_there_is_somebody_at_home_who_is_not_his_wife_trigger = {
	any_concubine = {
		count >= 1
	}
}

bc_that_arrangement_has_already_ended_trigger = {
	any_concubine = {
		count = 0
	}
	any_former_concubine = {
		count >= 1
	}
}

# BATCH 140 - THE TWO APPETITES THAT ARE NOT COURAGE.
#
# Batch 131 opened the AI-personality surface and spent exactly one of the nine
# attributes on it. `ai_boldness` has been read since; `ai_energy`, `ai_zeal`,
# `ai_greed`, `ai_compassion`, `ai_honor`, `ai_rationality`, `ai_vengefulness`
# and `ai_sociability` still return zero over the whole mod apart from the one
# line of batch 131's own header that lists them. The engine keeps nine numbers
# describing what a man wants and the mod reads one of them.
#
# THE SCALE AND THE THRESHOLDS COME FROM VANILLA, NOT FROM INVENTION. Vanilla's
# `common/script_values/00_ai_values.txt:22-34` defines the whole ladder:
# `very_low_positive_ai_value = 10`, `low_positive_ai_value = 20`,
# `medium_positive_ai_value = 35`, `high_positive_ai_value = 50`,
# `very_high_positive_ai_value = 75`, and the same six mirrored negative. Both
# gates below are written against those constants rather than against raw
# numbers, so if Paradox retunes the ladder these move with it.
#
# BOTH ATTRIBUTES ARE LIVE IN SHIPPED FILES. Vanilla reads `ai_zeal` at
# `common/activities/activity_types/pilgrimage.txt:23` (`ai_zeal >= 100`, which
# is above even the very-high constant) and again at `:593`, and weights
# pilgrimage intents by it at `common/activities/intents/
# pilgrimage_intents.txt:62`. `ai_compassion` is read at
# `common/accolade_types/04_ep2_skilled_attributes.txt:755`, at
# `common/activities/activity_types/local_examination.txt:977` and at
# `common/activities/intents/shared_intents.txt:76`.
#
# ONE GUARD, ONE BARE READ, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE USUAL ONE. The first asks
# the scoped character about himself, which needs nothing - whoever the template
# scoped is standing right there. The second walks `side_commander`, which is a
# genuinely optional link, so it carries the `exists` as a sibling and repeats
# it inside the `trigger_if` limit before the number is read. The second is
# named through `scope:combat_side` rather than `root` for batch 127's reason.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 131'S PAIR AND IT IS NOT A THRESHOLD ON ITS NUMBER.
# `ai_boldness` is how much risk a man will accept. Zeal is what he thinks the
# day is FOR, and compassion is what he is willing to spend other people on.
# A cautious man can be a zealot; a bold man can be kind. None of the three
# implies either of the others, and none of these gates asks about boldness at
# all, so no negation is required to keep them apart.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE TRAIT ROWS EITHER. No shipped gate anywhere in the mod reads
# `zealous`, `cynical`, `compassionate` or `callous` - those four appear only as
# weights inside `chance` blocks, which do not gate anything. A trait is a
# binary the character sheet shows; these are the continuous numbers the AI
# actually decides on, and a man can sit in either tail with none of the four
# traits on him.
#
# TWO FACTS, TWO MEN. The first is what the scoped man himself believes he is
# doing here. The second is what the man who gives him his orders is prepared to
# do with him. Neither implies the other and neither is about the other's
# number, so the pair carries no negation for the same reason batch 131's did
# not.
#
# RARITY. The ladder is signed and most of the population sits near the middle,
# so both tails are thin by construction; the first asks for the second-highest
# rung of six and the second for the third-lowest, and the second additionally
# requires the side to have a commander at all.
bc_he_believes_this_more_than_the_men_beside_him_trigger = {
	ai_zeal >= very_high_positive_ai_value
}

bc_the_man_giving_the_orders_will_spend_him_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			ai_compassion <= high_negative_ai_value
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 141 - WHETHER THE CHAIN OF COMMAND IS ALSO A CHAIN OF HOMAGE.
#
# `is_vassal_of`, `is_liege_or_above_of`, `any_vassal`, `num_of_vassals`,
# `any_courtier` and `any_liege_or_above` all returned zero across the whole
# mod. A hundred and forty batches have asked who these men are to each other -
# kin, friends, rivals, hooks, dread, opinion, truces, allies - and not one of
# them has asked the oldest question in the setting, which is who holds land of
# whom. Every man on the field is standing there because somebody owes somebody
# something, and the mod has never once read the ledger it is all built on.
#
# BOTH ARE LIVE IN SHIPPED FILES. Vanilla writes `is_vassal_of = scope:host` at
# `common/activities/activity_types/chariot_race.txt:702` and four times in
# `common/activities/activity_types/coronation.txt`, and AGOT ships the same
# reads at `chariot_race.txt:707` and `coronation.txt:1784`. Vanilla writes
# `is_liege_or_above_of` at `common/activities/activity_types/debate.txt:846`,
# at `common/casus_belli_types/06_ce1_wars.txt:361` and at
# `common/casus_belli_types/07_ep3_wars.txt:4216`, and negates it at
# `common/casus_belli_types/10_tgp_japan_wars.txt:1008`.
#
# THE FIRST IS KNIGHT-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. It asks
# whether the scoped man holds his land of the man commanding this side. A
# commander scoped into his own template IS `scope:combat_side.side_commander`,
# and no man is his own vassal, so this gate is dead in the commander pool and
# would be a silently-never-firing row if it were used there. It is used only on
# the knight side. The second is the mirror-image problem solved the other way:
# it reads the ENEMY side's commander, which is never the scoped man on either
# pool, so it is live everywhere and is used on the commander side where it says
# the most.
#
# BOTH TAKE THE GUARD, AND THE SECOND TAKES TWO. Naming a link as the TARGET of
# a trigger is a read, so `side_commander`, `enemy_side` and
# `enemy_side.side_commander` all carry their `exists` as a sibling and repeat
# it inside the `trigger_if` limit before anything is asked. Both are named
# through `scope:combat_side` rather than `root`, for batch 127's reason.
#
# THE PAIR CANNOT BOTH HOLD AND NO NEGATION IS NEEDED TO SAY SO. The first is
# about the man leading his own side; the second is about the man leading the
# other one. They are two facts about two different men, which is the same
# construction batches 110, 113, 118, 120 and 123-137 use, and the feudal graph
# is acyclic besides - a man cannot hold land of somebody who holds land of him.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE KIN ROWS, THE OPINION ROWS OR THE ALLY ROWS. Kinship is blood
# and does not move; opinion is what a man feels this week; alliance is a
# contract between equals that either can let lapse. Homage is none of those.
# It is a standing legal fact that outlives the feeling, and men who loathe each
# other are bound by it while brothers who love each other are not.
#
# RARITY. The first needs the knight to be a DIRECT vassal of the specific man
# commanding this side - not a courtier, not a household knight, not a vassal of
# his liege - which is a small slice of any army. The second needs the man
# opposite to be his own man at some remove and to be in the field against him
# anyway, which means a rebellion or a succession war, and needs both sides to
# have a commander at all.
bc_he_owes_this_man_his_homage_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		is_vassal_of = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_the_man_opposite_is_his_own_man_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		is_liege_or_above_of = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

# BATCH 142 - THE ROSTER, AND WHO IS ACTUALLY ON IT.
#
# `is_knight`, `is_knight_of`, `any_knight`, `num_of_knights`,
# `any_acclaimed_knight` and `any_courtier_or_guest` all returned zero across
# the whole mod. This is a submod about knights. It has one hundred and
# twenty-seven templates in a file called `bc_knight_phase_events.txt` and it
# has never once asked the engine whose knight anybody is. The word has been
# used as a synonym for "man on a horse" since plan 1; it is a specific
# employment with a specific employer and the engine tracks both.
#
# BOTH ARE LIVE IN SHIPPED FILES. Vanilla writes `is_knight_of = scope:host` at
# `common/activities/activity_types/inspection.txt:647`, `:1074` and `:1081`,
# `is_knight_of = scope:owner` at `monument_expedition.txt:596` and
# `tournament.txt:1437`, and `is_knight_of = root` at `tournament.txt:3711`.
# AGOT ships its own copies at `inspection.txt:647`, `:1088` and `:1095`, at
# `monument_expedition.txt:602`, at `tournament.txt:1765` and at
# `common/activities/activity_types/agot_coronation.txt:1709`.
#
# THE FIRST IS NOT "IS HE A KNIGHT", WHICH WOULD FIRE FOR THE WHOLE POOL. Every
# man the knight templates ever scope is a knight of somebody by definition, so
# a bare `is_knight = yes` would be a gate that passes everybody and gates
# nothing. What is asked instead is whether the man giving the orders on this
# side is the same man who pays him. A side aggregates every army standing on
# it, and its knights come from all of those armies and all of their owners,
# while the commander is one specific man - very often not the owner of the
# force the scoped knight belongs to, and in a realm army with vassal levies
# usually not. So this is a real minority of any given field.
#
# THE SECOND ASKS ABOUT A ROSTER AND NOT ABOUT A REPUTATION. `any_knight`
# iterates the knights a character employs. A commander is frequently a landless
# courtier or a household man himself, and those have no roster at all, so the
# gate first requires him to be a ruler and then requires him to be a ruler
# large enough to keep eight. Vanilla scales the slots with realm size, which
# means eight is a duke's household rather than a count's.
#
# ONE GUARD, ONE BARE READ. Naming a link as the TARGET of a trigger is a read,
# so the first carries `exists` as a sibling and repeats it inside the
# `trigger_if` limit before `is_knight_of` is asked, and it names the man
# through `scope:combat_side` rather than `root` for batch 127's reason. The
# second is an iterator over the scoped character's own roster; an empty roster
# is a clean no and takes no guard.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 141'S HOMAGE ROWS. Vassalage is land held of a man and it
# outlives everybody's feelings about it; knighthood in this engine is
# employment, it can be ended in an afternoon, and it does not move an acre.
# A knight of a man is very often not his vassal, and a vassal is very often not
# on anybody's roster. Neither gate here reads `is_vassal_of` and neither of
# batch 141's reads a roster.
#
# TWO FACTS, TWO MEN, SO NO NEGATION, for the same reason as batches 110, 113,
# 118, 120 and 123-141. The first is a fact about who employs the scoped man.
# The second is a fact about how many men the scoped man employs. Neither
# implies the other in either direction.
#
# RARITY. The first needs the commander of the side to be the scoped knight's
# own employer rather than somebody else's. The second needs a ruler with a
# household of eight sworn swords, which most men who are handed an army are
# not.
bc_he_is_sworn_to_the_man_leading_this_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		is_knight_of = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_he_keeps_a_household_of_sworn_swords_trigger = {
	any_knight = {
		count >= 8
	}
}

# BATCH 143 - HOW MANY SEATS EACH OF THEM ACTUALLY HOLDS.
#
# `any_held_title`, `has_primary_title`, `any_directly_owned_province` and
# `any_sub_realm_county` all returned zero across the whole mod. The mod has
# read RANK eight times - `highest_held_title_tier` on this man or the man
# opposite, `is_powerful_vassal`, `is_independent_ruler` - and has never once
# read HOW MUCH. Rank is the tallest thing a man holds. It says nothing at all
# about whether he holds one of them or nine, and in this setting that is most
# of the difference between a duke and a great house.
#
# THE SYNTAX IS VANILLA'S, VERBATIM. `common/activities/activity_types/
# gruesome_festival.txt:443-445` writes `any_held_title = { count >= 2  tier =
# tier_county }`, which is the count-plus-filter form used below, and
# `common/casus_belli_types/07_ep3_wars.txt:3970-3971` writes
# `every_held_title = { title_tier >= duchy ... }`, which is the comparison
# vocabulary. Note that these are two different vocabularies for the same fact -
# `tier = tier_county` inside the iterator, `title_tier >= duchy` for a
# comparison - and the mod's existing rank reads use a THIRD, the
# `highest_held_title_tier >= tier_kingdom` script-value form. All three are
# live in shipped files. The comparison form is the one wanted here.
# `title_tier >= county` is also live at `common/casus_belli_types/
# 00_claim.txt:458` and `title_tier >= duchy` at
# `common/character_interactions/00_diarch_interactions.txt:784` and `:850`.
#
# THE PAIR IS DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND THE `count = 0` ROW IS THE REASON,
# exactly as in batch 139. Without it the second gate is a strict SUPERSET of
# the first - a man holding two duchies also holds at least one title of county
# tier or better - and the first becomes a subset of the second, which is the
# collision batch 72 shipped by accident. The second gate therefore says in its
# own body that he holds nothing of duchy tier or above, and the two can never
# both pass.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are iterators over the scoped character's own held titles. An
# empty list is a clean no, and nothing here walks a link.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE RANK ROWS. `bc_facing_a_crown_trigger` reads
# `highest_held_title_tier >= tier_kingdom` on the FAR commander; these read the
# scoped man. More importantly rank and quantity come apart in both directions:
# a vassal king holds one title of kingdom tier and may hold nothing else worth
# the name, while a man with four duchies and no crown outranks nobody and
# outweighs almost everybody. The second gate in particular passes men the rank
# reads cannot distinguish from a landless household knight, because rank alone
# cannot tell a count with a seat of his own from a man with nothing.
#
# IT IS NOT THE WEIGHT ROWS EITHER. `is_powerful_vassal` is defined by the
# engine relative to a specific liege's realm and an independent duke is not
# one however much he holds; a powerful vassal may hold exactly one duchy.
# Neither gate below is a subset of that one in either direction.
#
# RARITY. The first needs two seats of duchy tier or better in one pair of
# hands, which on any given map is a handful of men. The second needs the scoped
# man to be landed at all - most knights are household men and hold nothing -
# and then to have stopped below a duchy, which is the great majority of those
# who are landed but a small slice of any army.
bc_he_holds_more_than_one_great_seat_trigger = {
	any_held_title = {
		count >= 2
		title_tier >= duchy
	}
}

bc_he_has_land_of_his_own_and_no_great_seat_trigger = {
	any_held_title = {
		count >= 1
		title_tier >= county
	}
	any_held_title = {
		count = 0
		title_tier >= duchy
	}
}

# BATCH 144 - WHAT THE NAME IS WORTH, WHICH IS NOT HOW MANY CARRY IT.
#
# `dynasty_prestige_level` returned one occurrence across the whole mod, and it
# is a line in the header of the house-size batch saying the mod had never read
# it. That is still true. The mod asks whether two men share a house, whether a
# house is nearly extinct, whether a house is half of a historical feud and
# whether a house has bought a particular legacy perk. It has never asked what a
# house is WORTH, which is the number the engine keeps for exactly that and the
# only one of the four that moves during a game.
#
# THE LADDER AND BOTH FORMS ARE VANILLA'S. `common/character_interactions/
# 00_alliance.txt:1864-1899` walks the whole scale in one block -
# `dynasty_prestige_level = 0`, then `>= 2`, `>= 3`, `>= 4`, `>= 5`, `>= 6`,
# `>= 7` - which is where the threshold below comes from rather than from
# invention, and `common/achievements/standard_achievements.txt:261` compares
# against a `max_dynasty_prestige_level` script value. The COMPARISON form used
# by the second gate is vanilla's too: `common/activities/pulse_actions/
# wedding_pulse_actions.txt:19` writes `dynasty ?= { dynasty_prestige_level >
# scope:host.dynasty.dynasty_prestige_level }` and repeats it at `:32`, `:86`
# and `:105`.
#
# THE SECOND IS RELATIVE AND THE FIRST IS ABSOLUTE, WHICH IS WHY NEITHER IS A
# SUBSET OF THE OTHER. A man out of a fifth-rung house facing a second-rung one
# passes the first and fails the second. Two obscure houses where the far one is
# one rung up passes the second and fails the first. They are independent in
# both directions and no negation is needed to make them so.
#
# THE SECOND COMPARES THE TWO COMMANDERS AND NOT THE SCOPED MAN, ON PURPOSE.
# Vanilla can write `scope:host.dynasty...` because it has a saved scope to hand.
# Inside a scripted trigger there is no guarantee what `root` is - batch 127's
# lesson - so both sides of the comparison are named through `scope:combat_side`,
# which makes the gate mean something better than "his" name would anyway: what
# is asked is which of the two names leading this field is the heavier one, and
# a knight who passes it is standing under the lighter of the two.
#
# FIVE GUARDS ON THE SECOND AND THEY ARE ALL LOAD-BEARING. `side_commander`,
# `enemy_side`, `enemy_side.side_commander` and BOTH dynasties are optional -
# this setting is full of men with no house at all, and the mod's own house-size
# batch records that - and a `dynasty` read that is also the right-hand side of
# a comparison is still a read. Every `exists` is repeated inside the
# `trigger_if` limit.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE HOUSE-SIZE ROWS AND IT IS NOT THE FEUD ROWS. Size and worth
# come apart hard: a house can be down to two living members and still be one of
# the great names of the age, and a house can have forty members and be worth
# nothing to anybody. The feud gates ask whether a specific pair of names has a
# specific history; these ask what either name is worth to people with no
# history in it at all. The perk gates ask what a house has bought; this asks
# what it is.
#
# RARITY. Vanilla's own ladder treats the fifth rung as the top third of a scale
# that runs past seven, and reaching it takes decades of renown, so the first is
# a small number of houses on any map. The second needs both sides to be led,
# both leaders to have a house, and the far one's to be strictly the heavier -
# which is at most half of the fields where all of that is true, and in practice
# fewer, because the heavier name is more often the one with the army.
bc_the_name_he_carries_is_a_great_one_trigger = {
	exists = dynasty
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = dynasty }
		dynasty = {
			dynasty_prestige_level >= 5
		}
	}
}

bc_the_name_opposite_outweighs_the_name_here_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander.dynasty
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander.dynasty
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander.dynasty
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander.dynasty
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander.dynasty = {
			dynasty_prestige_level > scope:combat_side.side_commander.dynasty.dynasty_prestige_level
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 145 - THE GROUND THAT ANSWERS TO HIM AND THE GROUND HE ACTUALLY OWNS,
# WHICH ARE TWO NUMBERS AND NOT ONE.
#
# `any_realm_province`, `any_directly_owned_province` and `has_building_or_higher`
# all returned zero across the whole mod. The mod has read RANK eight times -
# `highest_held_title_tier` and `is_landed` carry the whole station batch - and
# batch 143 read how MANY seats a man holds. It has never once read how much
# GROUND there is, and it has never read the only comparison that makes the
# question interesting, which is the gap between the ground that answers to a
# man and the ground his own hands are on.
#
# BOTH ITERATORS AND BOTH FORMS ARE VANILLA'S. `common/decisions/
# 10_ach_oath_decisions.txt:1523-1526` writes `any_directly_owned_province = {
# count >= 3  has_building_or_higher = ... }` and repeats it at `:1585` and
# `:1647`; `common/achievements/standard_achievements.txt:769` uses the same
# iterator without a count. `events/lifestyles/intrigue_lifestyle/
# intrigue_scheming_events.txt:8324-8325` writes `any_realm_province = { count
# >= 2 ... }`. The `count <=` form on the first gate is vanilla's too:
# `common/character_interactions/00_diarch_interactions.txt:693` writes
# `any_held_county = { count <= 3 }`.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are iterators, and an iterator over an empty list is a clean
# no, not a read of a missing link. This is the same reasoning the concubine
# batch used.
#
# THE FIRST IS A CONJUNCTION AND IT HAS TO BE. `any_realm_province = { count >=
# 20 }` on its own is every great lord on the map and would not be a gate. What
# is being asked is not that his realm is wide - it is that his realm is wide
# AND almost none of it is in his own hands, which is a particular kind of man:
# one who governs entirely through other men and whose army this afternoon was
# raised off ground he has never collected a rent from.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND NOT BY ARGUMENT. The first requires
# `any_directly_owned_province = { count <= 3 }` and the second requires
# `count >= 4` of the same iterator. No man can pass both, in any realm, ever.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE STATION ROWS AND IT IS NOT THE SEAT-COUNT ROWS. Rank is what
# a man is CALLED and the station batch reads it; batch 143 reads how many
# seats he was given. Neither of those tells you whether the ground under those
# seats is worked by him or by somebody else, and the whole point of this pair
# is the difference. A king with three demesne counties and a landed knight
# with five are the same man on this axis, in opposite directions, and the mod
# could not previously say either of them.
#
# THE SECOND IS A SUBSET OF `is_landed = yes` AND THAT IS NOT A COLLISION.
# `is_landed` is read in the commander pool only - the knight file mentions it
# twice and both are header prose about a commander - and the two pools roll
# separately, so no knight template has ever tested it. The fact read is
# quantity in any case, not existence.
#
# RARITY. The first needs a realm of twenty provinces, which on any map is the
# top handful of lords, AND a personal demesne of three or fewer, which most
# lords that size exceed - the engine's own demesne limits push the other way.
# The second needs four counties held personally by a man who is riding as
# somebody's knight rather than commanding, which is a landed vassal who came
# out under a banner that is not his own.
bc_the_ground_that_answers_to_him_is_not_the_ground_he_owns_trigger = {
	any_realm_province = {
		count >= 20
	}
	any_directly_owned_province = {
		count <= 3
	}
}

bc_the_ground_he_owns_is_in_his_own_two_hands_trigger = {
	any_directly_owned_province = {
		count >= 4
	}
}

# BATCH 146 - WHAT THE PEOPLE WHO LIVE ON THIS GROUND THINK OF THE MAN WHO OWNS
# IT, WHICH IS NEITHER WHAT IT IS WORTH NOR WHOSE IT IS.
#
# `county_opinion` returned zero across the whole mod. The mod has read this
# county three ways already - how rich it is (`development_level`, the rich and
# poor country rows), whose it is (`county.holder`, `county_controller`, the
# held-not-owned rows) and what the weather and the terrain on it are doing -
# and has never once asked what the people standing in the doorways think of
# the man whose name is on it.
#
# THE TRIGGER IS LANDED_TITLE SCOPE AND THE PATH IS VANILLA'S. `common/
# activities/activity_types/tour.txt:2490` writes exactly the shape used here -
# `scope:province.county = { county_opinion < 0 }` - and repeats it at `:2497`;
# the same file uses the bare county form at `:469` and `:476`. The bands come
# from `common/activities/intents/chariot_race_intents.txt`, which walks the
# whole ladder: `county_opinion >= 10` at `:80`, `< 0` at `:89`, `<= -20` at
# `:98`, `<= -40` at `:107`, and on the other end `<= 90` at `:291` and `< 50`
# at `:299`. The two numbers below are the outer ends of vanilla's own ladder
# rather than invented ones.
#
# GUARDS, IN THE SHAPE THE GROUND ROWS ALREADY USE. `combat.location` does not
# always resolve, and a province does not always have a county - the rich and
# poor country rows at 1336 and 1348 nest an `exists = county` inside the
# location's own `trigger_if` for exactly that reason, and these do the same.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One is `<= -40` and the other is `>= 50`
# of the same number on the same title. There is no county on any map that can
# pass both.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE RICH AND POOR COUNTRY ROWS AND IT IS NOT THE OWNERSHIP ROWS.
# Development says what the ground makes; it says nothing about whether the
# people who make it are willing. A rich county can be sullen and a poor one
# devoted, and both of those are more common than the reverse. The ownership
# rows ask whose name is on the county and whose men are holding it - a legal
# fact, settled in a chancery. This asks a fact settled in kitchens, and the two
# have no reliable relationship at all: a man can own a county outright, control
# it completely, and be loathed on every acre of it.
#
# NEITHER GATE READS THE SCOPED MAN. Both read the county and its own count, so
# neither depends on what `root` is - batch 127's lesson - and neither is a
# statement about the man the moment is written for. What is being said is that
# the ground itself has a temper, and that an army fighting on ground with a
# temper is not fighting the same battle as an army fighting on quiet ground.
#
# RARITY. Vanilla's own ladder treats `<= -40` as its bottom rung, which needs
# lost control or a foreign culture or faith or a badly chosen law on top of an
# ordinary grievance; `>= 50` is near the top of a scale vanilla caps its own
# checks at ninety. Most counties in most years sit in the middle and pass
# neither, and the county also has to be the one the battle happens to be
# standing in.
bc_the_people_on_this_ground_hate_the_man_who_owns_it_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			exists = county
			trigger_if = {
				limit = { exists = county }
				county = { county_opinion <= -40 }
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_the_people_on_this_ground_are_content_with_the_man_who_owns_it_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			exists = county
			trigger_if = {
				limit = { exists = county }
				county = { county_opinion >= 50 }
			}
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 147 - WHAT THE PARCHMENT SAYS HE OWES, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS WHAT HE
# IS WILLING TO BRING.
#
# `vassal_contract_obligation_level`, `vassal_contract_has_modifiable_obligations`
# and every `feudal_levies_*_level` script value returned zero across the whole
# mod. Batch 141 asked whether the chain of command was also a chain of homage -
# whether this man owes that man anything at all. It never asked HOW MUCH, and
# the engine keeps that as a number on a contract, and the number is the single
# most military fact in the whole vassal system: it is literally what fraction
# of his men he is obliged to hand over.
#
# THE KEY, THE VALUES AND THE GUARD ARE ALL AGOT'S OWN. AGOT writes
# `vassal_contract_obligation_level:feudal_government_levies` against the named
# script values at `common/subject_contracts/contracts/special_contracts.txt:64`,
# `:82`, `:251`, `:318`, `:387` and `:436`, and at
# `common/decisions/30_court_decisions.txt:215`, `:224` and `:308`. The levels
# themselves are vanilla's, defined at `common/script_values/
# 00_goverment_values.txt:87-91` as exempt 0, low 1, normal 2, high 3,
# extortionate 4, and AGOT's own contract group `lp_feudal_vassal` at
# `common/subject_contracts/groups/00_agot_subject_contract_groups.txt:1-13`
# carries `feudal_government_levies`, so the key is live in this setting.
#
# THE GUARD PAIR IS AGOT'S TOO, IN ONE PIECE.
# `special_contracts.txt:384-387` reads `government_has_flag =
# government_is_feudal` and the levy obligation level in the same block, for the
# obvious reason: the key only exists on a feudal contract, and a man on some
# other kind of contract is not a lower number on this scale, he is off the
# scale entirely. `vassal_contract_has_modifiable_obligations` - AGOT's spelling,
# used at `common/character_interactions/00_modifiy_vassal_contract.txt:21` and
# repeatedly after it - covers the rest: it is documented as meaning they have a
# contract at all, they use obligation levels, and they are count or above. A
# top liege has no contract to read; both guards are repeated inside the
# `trigger_if` limit in the usual way.
#
# `government_has_flag` IS ALREADY IN THE MOD ONCE, at 1205, reading
# `government_is_mercenary` as the ARGUMENT of a gate. Here it is a guard rather
# than the fact being asked about, and the two flags cannot both hold anyway.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One is `>= feudal_levies_high_level`,
# which is three, and the other is `<= feudal_levies_low_level`, which is one.
# Nothing sits in both.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE HOMAGE ROWS. Batch 141 asks a yes-or-no about a relationship
# between two named men on this field. These ask a number on a document, about a
# relationship with a man who is not here and may never have been in a battle in
# his life. A knight can owe his homage to the man beside him and owe his levies
# to somebody four hundred miles away, and the mod could not previously say
# either half of that.
#
# RARITY. `normal` is the default level the engine hands out and most contracts
# in most realms sit on it, so both gates are asking for a contract somebody
# went to the trouble of changing - a liege who squeezed, or a liege who was
# squeezed - and then for that man to be standing in a battle on the day. High
# and extortionate together are the top two rungs of five, exempt and low the
# bottom two, and the middle rung is where nearly everybody is.
bc_the_terms_he_holds_by_take_most_of_what_he_can_raise_trigger = {
	government_has_flag = government_is_feudal
	vassal_contract_has_modifiable_obligations = yes
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			government_has_flag = government_is_feudal
			vassal_contract_has_modifiable_obligations = yes
		}
		vassal_contract_obligation_level:feudal_government_levies >= feudal_levies_high_level
	}
}

bc_the_terms_he_holds_by_ask_almost_nothing_of_him_trigger = {
	government_has_flag = government_is_feudal
	vassal_contract_has_modifiable_obligations = yes
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			government_has_flag = government_is_feudal
			vassal_contract_has_modifiable_obligations = yes
		}
		vassal_contract_obligation_level:feudal_government_levies <= feudal_levies_low_level
	}
}

# BATCH 148 - WHAT IS STANDING ON THIS GROUND BESIDES THE TWO ARMIES.
#
# `fort_level`, `has_holding_type`, `has_special_building` and `is_under_siege`
# all returned zero across the whole mod. The mod reads this province for its
# terrain, its weather, its wealth, its owner and now its temper, and has never
# once looked up. Whether there are walls over the field is the oldest fact in
# the world about a battle and the mod could not say it.
#
# `fort_level` IS PROVINCE SCOPE AND BOTH NUMBERS ARE AGOT'S OWN. AGOT writes
# `fort_level >= 10` twice as its own threshold for a great seat, at
# `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_game_start.txt:93` and
# `common/on_action/province_on_actions.txt:770` (repeated at `:782`), and uses
# the low end as `fort_level > 0` at `common/buildings/
# 99_background_graphics_buildings.txt:89` and `common/customizable_localization/
# 00_building_custom_localization.txt:18`. The second gate below is the exact
# complement of that second form. Vanilla uses the same trigger at
# `events/lifestyles/intrigue_lifestyle/intrigue_scheming_events.txt:8326`, in a
# block that is explicitly about counting siegeable holdings.
#
# TEN IS NOT AN INVENTED NUMBER AND IT IS NOT A LARGE ONE BY ACCIDENT. Vanilla's
# castle upgrades hand out fort levels in single digits and its great buildings
# stack a few more on top; AGOT reserves double figures for the handful of seats
# a reader would name without being prompted. A field that passes the first gate
# is a field somebody built something enormous on, on purpose, a long time ago.
#
# GUARDS IN THE SHAPE THE GROUND ROWS USE. `combat.location` does not always
# resolve; the guard is repeated inside the `trigger_if` limit. No county read is
# needed here - `fort_level` sits directly on the province - so these are one
# level shallower than the rich and poor country rows at 1336 and 1348.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Ten or more against exactly nothing.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE TERRAIN ROWS AND IT IS NOT THE DEVELOPMENT ROWS. Terrain is
# what the ground was before anybody arrived; this is what was put on it
# afterwards. Development is what the ground earns, and a rich county with no
# walls and a poor county with a great fortress on it are both ordinary things.
#
# NEITHER GATE READS THE SCOPED MAN, so neither depends on what `root` is, and
# neither is a claim about anybody's character. Both are claims about the field.
#
# RARITY. The first is the small set of provinces in the world with double-figure
# fortification, and then the battle has to happen to be standing in one of them,
# which is rarer still because armies fighting under walls that size are usually
# there for the walls and not for each other. The second wants a province with
# nothing on it worth the name, which is a real category and not a large one,
# since most ground that gets fought over has been worth holding at some point.
bc_this_field_lies_under_a_great_wall_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			fort_level >= 10
		}
	}
}

bc_there_is_nothing_standing_on_this_ground_at_all_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			fort_level = 0
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 149 - HOW MANY MEN EACH OF THEM COULD PUT IN A FIELD, WHICH IS NOT HOW
# MANY ARE IN THIS ONE.
#
# `max_military_strength`, `current_military_strength`,
# `current_raised_military_strength` and `is_commanding_army` all returned zero
# across the whole mod. This is a battle mod. It has read rank, renown, seats,
# provinces, contracts and the ground itself, and it had never once read the
# plainest military number the engine keeps about a man.
#
# BOTH FORMS ARE IN THE TREE ALREADY. AGOT writes `current_military_strength >
# scope:attacker.eighty_percent_of_current_military_strength` at
# `common/casus_belli_types/09_mpo_wars.txt:571` and again at `:1799`, and
# `scope:actor.current_military_strength < scope:recipient.
# military_strength_75_percent_value` at `common/character_interactions/
# 00_agot_hostile_interactions.txt:195`; the character-to-character comparison
# used by the second gate is AGOT's at `common/character_interactions/
# 00_agot_first_ranger_interactions.txt:279` and `:283`. The script value the
# first gate leans on is defined in AGOT's own file at
# `common/script_values/00_interaction_values.txt:852` as current strength
# multiplied by eight tenths, and it evaluates on whatever scope reads it.
# Vanilla reads `max_military_strength` off another character at
# `common/character_interactions/00_alliance.txt:2008`.
#
# THE FIRST IS ABOUT MOBILISATION AND THE SECOND IS ABOUT CAPACITY, AND THOSE
# ARE OPPOSITE ENDS OF THE SAME SENTENCE. What a man COULD raise is a fact about
# his lands. What he HAS raised is a fact about his decisions this season. A
# great power with a tenth of itself in the field and a small one with all of
# itself in the field are the two men these gates find, and neither number tells
# you the other.
#
# NO NEGATION, AND THE REASON IS THE USUAL ONE. The two gates read two different
# facts about two different men - the first reads the side's commander, the
# second reads the scoped man against that commander - so neither can be a
# subset of the other, and the pools roll separately in any case.
#
# THE SECOND IS KNIGHT-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION, WHICH IS BATCH 141'S LESSON AGAIN.
# A commander scoped into his own template IS `scope:combat_side.side_commander`,
# and no man is larger than himself, so on the commander pool this gate would be
# a row that never fires. It is given to the knights, where it means something
# quite sharp: the man taking the orders out here could, if it came to it, put
# more men on a field than the man giving them.
#
# GUARDS. `side_commander` is optional and both gates name it, so both carry the
# `exists` as a sibling and repeat it inside the `trigger_if` limit. The second
# gate's read is the RIGHT-HAND SIDE of a comparison, which is still a read.
#
# RARITY. The first wants a man who has called out at least four fifths of
# everything available to him, which is not what most lords do in most wars -
# levies left unraised, vassals not summoned and garrisons kept back are the
# normal condition - and then wants him leading and losing. The second wants a
# knight whose own lands outweigh his commander's, which happens when a great
# man rides under a marshal, a regent or a kinsman, and is uncommon precisely
# because men of that size usually insist on the command.
bc_everything_he_could_raise_is_already_on_this_field_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			current_raised_military_strength >= eighty_percent_of_current_military_strength
		}
	}
}

bc_he_could_put_more_men_in_a_field_than_the_man_commanding_him_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		max_military_strength > scope:combat_side.side_commander.max_military_strength
	}
}

# BATCH 150 - WHAT THE TWO PEOPLES ON THIS FIELD MAKE OF EACH OTHER.
#
# `cultural_acceptance`, `has_cultural_pillar`, `has_doctrine`,
# `has_same_culture_as` and `has_same_faith_as` all returned zero across the
# whole mod. The mod reads `faith` exactly once in a scripted trigger - line 250,
# on the far commander, as a plain inequality - and reads `culture` only for
# named cultural traditions. It has never asked the question that sits
# underneath both, which is not what either side believes but how much of each
# other they can stand.
#
# THE TRIGGER IS CULTURE SCOPE AND BOTH BANDS ARE SHIPPED NUMBERS. Vanilla's
# `common/activities/activity_types/tour.txt:217-220` writes
# `cultural_acceptance = { target = root.culture  value <= 25 }` and repeats the
# same band at `:446`; `common/achievements/ep1_achievements.txt:125-128` uses
# `value >= 95` at the far end. AGOT ships the same file and the same call sites
# at `common/activities/activity_types/tour.txt:224`, `:453`, `:465` and `:2516`.
# The lower band below is vanilla's own; the upper one is set below vanilla's
# achievement number on purpose, because ninety-five is a figure written to be
# hard to reach on purpose and seventy-five is what two peoples who have lived
# beside each other for generations actually sit at.
#
# BOTH SIDES ARE NAMED THROUGH `scope:combat_side`, NOT `root`. That is batch
# 127's lesson and batch 144's construction: inside a scripted trigger there is
# no guarantee what `root` is, and naming both commanders explicitly makes the
# gate mean something better than "his" culture would - what is asked is what
# the two peoples LEADING this field make of each other, which is a fact about
# the battle rather than about whichever man happens to be scoped.
#
# `culture` TAKES NO GUARD. Every character has one; it is not an optional link
# in the sense `side_commander` or `dynasty` are. The three `exists` rows that
# are here are there for the commanders and the enemy side, and every one of
# them is repeated inside the `trigger_if` limit.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Twenty-five or less against seventy-five
# or more of the same number on the same pair of cultures.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE FAITH ROW AT 250 AND IT IS NOT THE TRADITION ROWS. The faith
# row asks whether two men happen to believe different things, which is a yes or
# a no and says nothing about temperature; two faiths can differ and be
# comfortable, or share a name and loathe each other. The tradition rows ask
# whether a culture holds a particular named custom. This asks what two peoples
# have made of each other over time, which is the only one of the three that
# moves during a game.
#
# RARITY. Acceptance is a slow number. It sits near the middle for most pairs
# most of the time, drifts up between neighbours who intermarry and down between
# ones who do not, and both gates want an outer quarter of the scale AND both
# sides of this battle to be led. Two peoples at twenty-five or less have
# generally not been fighting each other often enough to have got used to it.
bc_the_two_peoples_on_this_field_are_strangers_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.side_commander.culture = {
			cultural_acceptance = {
				target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander.culture
				value <= 25
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_the_two_peoples_on_this_field_know_each_other_well_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.side_commander.culture = {
			cultural_acceptance = {
				target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander.culture
				value >= 75
			}
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 151 - WHAT KIND OF PLACE THIS FIELD BELONGS TO.
#
# `has_holding_type` returned zero across the whole mod apart from the one line
# of batch 148's header that lists it as untouched. Batch 148 asked how tall the
# thing standing on this ground is; it never asked what the thing IS. A castle
# barony, a market town and a sept are three completely different places to have
# a battle in, and the mod could describe none of them.
#
# `is_under_siege` IS NOT IN THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP AT ALL and is therefore not used
# here, for the same reason `devastation` was refused: an undocumented keyword
# is not worth the zero-error baseline. `has_special_building` is documented and
# still unread, and is left for a later batch on purpose - it would not be
# disjoint from either row below, since a special building can sit in any kind
# of holding.
#
# THE TWO HOLDING NAMES ARE VANILLA'S OWN, DEFINED AT
# `common/holdings/00_holdings.txt:83` (`city_holding`) and `:135`
# (`church_holding`), alongside `castle_holding` at `:1` and `tribal_holding` at
# `:54`. Vanilla reads the first form at
# `common/activities/pulse_actions/survey_actions.txt:86`, `:100`, `:121`,
# `:135`, `:462` and `:476`; AGOT reads it at
# `common/casus_belli_types/00_agot_nights_watch_wars.txt:46`. AGOT reads the
# second form throughout `common/buildings/00_temple_buildings.txt`, at `:3292`,
# `:3380`, `:3464` and `:3558` among many others, and vanilla and AGOT both
# gate coronations on it - `common/activities/activity_types/coronation.txt:3639`
# in vanilla and `:3665` in AGOT.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. A barony holds one holding and it is one
# type. No province can pass both, and no negation is needed to say so.
#
# GUARDS IN THE SHAPE THE GROUND ROWS USE. `combat.location` does not always
# resolve; the guard sits as a sibling and is repeated inside the `trigger_if`
# limit. `has_holding_type` sits directly on the province, so these are one level
# shallower than the rich and poor country rows at 1336 and 1348, exactly like
# batch 148's pair.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 148'S PAIR. That one reads a number and this one reads a
# kind, and neither implies the other in either direction: a wealthy free city
# can carry a higher fort level than a small castle, and a sept can be walled or
# open. Nothing here is a subset of `fort_level >= 10` or of `fort_level = 0`.
#
# NEITHER GATE READS THE SCOPED MAN, so neither depends on what `root` is. Both
# are claims about the field, like the terrain rows and unlike almost everything
# else the mod asks.
#
# RARITY. Most baronies in the world are castles; cities and temples are the
# minority holdings by design, since a county that is all market and no keep does
# not survive. On top of that the battle has to be standing in that particular
# barony rather than in one of the others in the same county, and armies pick
# their ground for reasons that have nothing to do with what is built on it.
bc_this_field_belongs_to_a_town_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			has_holding_type = city_holding
		}
	}
}

bc_this_field_belongs_to_the_faith_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			has_holding_type = church_holding
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 152 - WHAT HE CAME FOR, AND WHAT THE MAN ACROSS THE FIELD WILL NOT DO.
#
# Batch 131 opened the AI-personality surface on `ai_boldness` and batch 140
# spent two more of the nine on `ai_zeal` and `ai_compassion`. `ai_greed`,
# `ai_honor`, `ai_energy`, `ai_rationality`, `ai_vengefulness` and
# `ai_sociability` still return zero over the whole mod apart from the two header
# lines that list them. Six of the nine numbers the engine keeps about what a man
# wants are still unread; this batch spends two.
#
# THE CONSTANTS ARE VANILLA'S LADDER, CITED IN FULL IN BATCH 140'S HEADER ABOVE.
# `common/script_values/00_ai_values.txt:22-34`. Both gates are written against
# the constants rather than raw numbers, for the reason given there.
#
# BOTH ATTRIBUTES ARE LIVE IN SHIPPED FILES AND BOTH ARE READ AT THESE HEIGHTS.
# Vanilla gates a tour's demands on `ai_greed >= 75` at
# `common/activities/activity_types/tour.txt:160` and again at `:947` - seventy-
# five is the raw value of `very_high_positive_ai_value`, which is the constant
# the first gate below uses - and mirrors it at `:164` and `:974`. AGOT reads
# `ai_greed >= 50` at `common/council_tasks/00_spymaster_tasks.txt:189` and
# `ai_greed > 0` at `common/council_tasks/00_steward_tasks.txt:87`. For honour,
# vanilla and AGOT ship the same line at
# `common/activities/intents/shared_intents.txt:81`, which is
# `ai_honor >= high_positive_ai_value` - the exact form and the exact constant
# the second gate below uses.
#
# ONE BARE READ AND ONE GUARDED WALK, THE USUAL DIFFERENCE. The first asks the
# scoped character about himself and needs nothing. The second walks
# `enemy_side` and then that side's `side_commander`, two genuinely optional
# links, so both `exists` rows sit as siblings and both are repeated inside the
# `trigger_if` limit before the number is read. The second is named through
# `scope:combat_side` rather than `root` for batch 127's reason.
#
# TWO FACTS, TWO MEN, AND NO NEGATION FOR THE SAME REASON AS BATCHES 131 AND 140.
# The first is what the scoped man is here to get. The second is a fact about
# somebody on the other side of the field entirely. Neither implies the other,
# neither is a threshold on the other's number, and a man can be as grasping as
# he likes without it saying anything about who he happens to be facing.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE OTHER THREE AI ROWS AND IT IS NOT THE TRAIT ROWS. Boldness is
# how much risk he will accept, zeal is what he thinks the day is for, and
# compassion is what he will spend other people on. Greed is what he expects to
# be handed at the end of it, which none of the three touches. Honour is what the
# far man will refuse to do to win, which is not the same question as whether he
# is cruel. And no shipped gate anywhere in the mod reads `greedy`, `generous`,
# `just` or `arbitrary` - those appear only as `chance` weights, which gate
# nothing, and a man can sit in either tail with none of the four traits on him.
#
# RARITY. The ladder is signed and most of the population sits near the middle,
# so both tails are thin by construction. The first asks for the top rung of six.
# The second asks for the fourth rung of six on a man who has to exist at all -
# the far side has to be led, which is the same requirement the far-commander
# rows have always carried.
bc_he_came_here_expecting_to_be_paid_trigger = {
	ai_greed >= very_high_positive_ai_value
}

bc_the_man_across_the_field_will_not_stoop_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			ai_honor >= high_positive_ai_value
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 153 - THE THING ON THIS GROUND THAT NOBODY BUILT FOR A BATTLE.
#
# Batch 151 read `has_holding_type` and said in its own header that
# `has_special_building` was documented, still unread, and being left for a later
# batch on purpose, because it would not be disjoint from either of that batch's
# rows - a special building can sit in a castle, a city or a temple. This is that
# later batch, and the way to make the pair disjoint is to ask about the SLOT
# rather than about the holding.
#
# THE TWO KEYS ARE BOTH IN THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP, both province scope, both yes/no:
# `has_special_building` ("does the province (holding) have a special building?")
# and `has_special_building_slot` ("does the province (holding) have a special
# building slot?"). A slot is where one may be put; a building is one that is
# there. Every province with a building has a slot, and the interesting set is
# the provinces that have the slot and nothing standing in it.
#
# BOTH FORMS ARE LIVE IN SHIPPED FILES. Vanilla reads `has_special_building =
# yes` at `common/customizable_localization/00_building_custom_localization.txt:
# 178`, `:186`, `:194`, `:203`, `:212` and `:221`, in the achievement loc at
# `common/customizable_localization/10_ach_custom_loc.txt:749`, `:758`, `:767`,
# `:776` and `:796`, and through `title_province` at
# `common/customizable_localization/00_hold_court_custom_joe.txt:85`, `:121` and
# `:157`. AGOT reads the same form at `common/suggestions/01_suggestions.txt:750`
# and ships the loc file with those lines commented. For the slot, vanilla gates
# a university visit on `has_special_building_slot = yes` at
# `common/activities/activity_types/university_visit.txt:213`, and AGOT ships the
# same gate at `:219` of its own copy.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY AN EXPLICIT NOT, not by construction, and the NOT is
# the whole point of the second row. A province that has a building necessarily
# has the slot it is standing in, so without the negation the second gate would
# be a strict superset of the first and the two would fire together constantly.
# With it, the second is exactly the empty-handed half.
#
# GUARDS IN THE SHAPE THE GROUND ROWS USE, and one level shallower than the rich
# and poor country rows at 1336 and 1348, since neither key needs a county read.
# `combat.location` does not always resolve; the guard sits as a sibling and is
# repeated inside the `trigger_if` limit.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 148'S PAIR AND IT IS NOT BATCH 151'S. Fort level is how much
# wall has been raised, which is a number and is about defence. Holding type is
# what the place is for. This is whether somebody, at some point, decided this
# particular spot deserved a thing that exists nowhere else - and the second row
# is the sadder version, a place that was marked out for exactly that and never
# got it. Neither row is a subset of any of those four: a special building can
# stand at any fort level, in any holding type, and an empty slot says nothing
# about either.
#
# NEITHER GATE READS THE SCOPED MAN, so neither depends on what `root` is.
#
# RARITY. Special buildings are a handful of provinces in the world by design -
# they are the map's named exceptions, and AGOT's are the places a reader would
# name unprompted. Slots are commoner than buildings but they are still a small
# set, and the second gate additionally wants the slot to be sitting empty in the
# year this battle is happening, which is a fact about somebody's unfinished
# ambition and not about the map. On top of both, the fight has to be standing in
# that exact barony rather than in one of the others in the same county.
bc_something_stands_on_this_ground_that_stands_nowhere_else_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			has_special_building = yes
		}
	}
}

bc_this_ground_was_marked_out_for_something_that_never_came_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			has_special_building_slot = yes
			NOT = { has_special_building = yes }
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 154 - HOW LONG EACH OF THEM HAS BEEN AT WAR WITHOUT A DAY OFF.
#
# `days_of_continuous_war` returned zero across the whole mod. This is a battle
# mod with a hundred and fifty batches of content in it and it had never once
# asked the oldest question anybody asks a soldier, which is how long he has been
# doing this. It has read the war's existence, its allies and its size, and never
# its length.
#
# THE KEY IS CHARACTER SCOPE AND THE DUMP'S OWN DESCRIPTION IS BACKWARDS. The
# 1.19.0.6 entry reads "Number of days the character has been in peace (0 if in
# peace)", which cannot be both; every shipped use in the tree treats it as days
# of unbroken war, and the parenthesis is the true half - a man at peace scores
# nothing. Both gates below are written on that reading, and both are written at
# figures the shipped files already use, so neither depends on my arithmetic.
#
# BOTH FIGURES ARE SHIPPED. Vanilla gates a vassal event on
# `days_of_continuous_war > 730` at `events/relations_events/vassal_events.txt:
# 2177` and AGOT ships that same line at that same number, and uses it again in
# its own content at `common/story_cycles/
# agot_story_cycle_scenario_rogue_prince.txt:230`. The ten-year figure is
# vanilla's at `common/character_interactions/03_fp2_interactions.txt:2625` and
# AGOT's at `:2691` of its own copy, as `days_of_continuous_war <= 3650`; this
# uses the other side of the same number. The tree also uses `> 150` at
# `events/travel_events/travel_events_bp3.txt:1343`, `>= 5` at
# `events/lifestyles/warfare_lifestyle/martial_authority_events.txt:259` and
# `>= 9125` at `events/harm_events.txt:8862`, all of which both games ship, so
# the scale runs from a working week to a lifetime and these two sit in it.
#
# ONE BARE READ AND ONE GUARDED WALK. The first asks the scoped character about
# himself and needs nothing. The second walks `enemy_side` and then that side's
# `side_commander`, two genuinely optional links, so both `exists` rows sit as
# siblings and both are repeated inside the `trigger_if` limit. The second is
# named through `scope:combat_side` rather than `root` for batch 127's reason.
#
# TWO MEN, TWO DIFFERENT FIGURES, AND NO NEGATION. The first is two years and is
# about whoever the template scoped. The second is ten years and is about a man
# on the far side of the field. They are not the same claim, they are not
# thresholds on each other, and one being true tells you nothing about the other:
# two years of unbroken war is a hard season, ten is a way of life, and the two
# men counting them have never met.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE WAR ROWS THE MOD ALREADY HAS. Those ask whether there is a war,
# who is in it and how big it is - facts about the war. This asks how long the
# MAN has been inside one, which is a different number: a lord who fought three
# short wars back to back with a fortnight of peace between them scores that
# fortnight as a reset, and a lord who has sat in one endless siege for two years
# without drawing a sword scores the whole of it.
#
# RARITY. Most wars are settled in a season, and the counter resets on the first
# day of peace, so two unbroken years is already a man whose luck or whose
# neighbours have been unusual. Ten is the far tail - a man who has not had a
# single peaceful day in a decade - and the second gate additionally requires the
# far side to be led at all.
bc_he_has_not_had_a_peaceful_day_in_two_years_trigger = {
	days_of_continuous_war > 730
}

bc_the_man_across_the_field_has_been_at_this_for_ten_years_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			days_of_continuous_war > 3650
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 155 - THE GAP BETWEEN HIS HANDS AND THE FAR COMMANDER'S.
#
# `prowess_diff` returned zero across the whole mod. `prowess` is on nearly every
# `chance` block in the file and is read as an absolute threshold in dozens of
# them, and in a hundred and fifty-four batches nothing has ever compared one
# man's hands to another's. An absolute prowess row says whether he is good. It
# cannot say the only thing that matters if the two of them ever end up within
# reach of each other, which is who is better.
#
# THE FORM AND THE FIGURE ARE BOTH VANILLA'S. `prowess_diff = { target = X
# value <= -15 }` appears at `common/character_interactions/
# 00_trait_interactions.txt:689` under vanilla's own comment "Don't challenge
# people hopelessly out of your league", and again at `:881`, at
# `common/character_interactions/00_artifact_interactions.txt:2405`, at
# `common/character_interactions/01_fp1_interactions.txt:786` and at
# `common/character_interactions/00_perk_interactions.txt:1595`. Every one of
# those five is fifteen. AGOT ships all of them unchanged.
#
# THE SIGN IS SCOPED MINUS TARGET, AND VANILLA'S COMMENT IS THE PROOF. The block
# above sits on `scope:actor` with `target = scope:recipient` and subtracts a
# thousand from the AI's willingness to duel, so a NEGATIVE difference has to
# mean the actor is the weaker man. The first gate below is therefore the one
# where the far commander is out of the scoped man's league, and the second is
# the reverse.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Fifteen below against fifteen above on
# the same subtraction between the same two men. No negation is needed and none
# is written.
#
# NAMING THE FAR COMMANDER AS A TARGET IS A READ, so it takes the full guard.
# Both `exists` rows sit as siblings and both are repeated inside the
# `trigger_if` limit before the comparison is made, and the man is named through
# `scope:combat_side` rather than `root` for batch 127's reason. This is the
# rule the mod has used since the `is_vassal_of` rows: a link used as a target is
# not a free mention.
#
# BOTH GATES WORK IN BOTH POOLS, and batch 141's lesson does not bite here.
# That lesson was about gates comparing the scoped man to HIS OWN side's
# commander, which is a never-firing row on the commander pool because a scoped
# commander is that man. The far commander is a different person from everybody
# on this side of the field, so a commander may be fifteen below him and a knight
# may be fifteen above him, and both rows mean what they say in either file.
#
# THIS IS NOT A COMPETENCE GATE AND IT IS NOT THE MARTIAL ROWS. Martial is how
# well he moves an army; prowess is what happens if somebody reaches him.
# Fifteen points of prowess is not a matter of degree - it is the distance
# between a man who has trained since he could stand and a man who has not - and
# the gate says nothing about whether either of them is any good at the job they
# are actually doing today.
#
# RARITY. Prowess is tightly distributed and fifteen is most of its usable range,
# so the tails are thin at both ends; vanilla treats the same figure as the point
# where an AI with any judgement refuses a duel outright. Both gates additionally
# require the far side to be led at all.
bc_the_far_commander_is_out_of_his_league_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		prowess_diff = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			value <= -15
		}
	}
}

bc_the_far_commander_is_out_of_his_depth_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		prowess_diff = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			value >= 15
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 156 - HOW MANY STEPS OF RANK THERE ARE BETWEEN THE TWO MEN IN CHARGE.
#
# `tier_difference` returned zero across the whole mod. `highest_held_title_tier`
# is read in fifteen places and every one of them is an absolute band - is he a
# duke, is he a king. Nothing has ever asked the question a herald would ask
# first, which is not what either man is but how far apart they are.
#
# THE FORM IS SHIPPED AND THE SIGN IS IN THE DUMP'S OWN EXAMPLE. Vanilla writes
# `tier_difference = { target = scope:recipient value = N }` six times in a row
# at `common/character_interactions/00_education_interactions.txt:1643`, `:1653`,
# `:1663`, `:1673`, `:1683` and `:1693`, walking N from 1 to 5 and then to -1.
# The 1.19.0.6 entry gives the sign explicitly: a baron scoped against a king
# scores -3. Scoped minus target, and the range is -5 to 5.
#
# THE DUMP LISTS THE FULL SET OF OPERATORS for this key, not just equality, so
# the two bands below are written as inequalities rather than as vanilla's six
# separate equality rows. That is the same reading of the same `Traits:` line the
# mod has relied on for every numeric trigger it uses.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Two steps below against two steps above
# on the same subtraction between the same two men.
#
# NAMING THE FAR COMMANDER AS A TARGET IS A READ, so both gates take the full
# guard, both `exists` rows repeated inside the `trigger_if` limit, and the man
# is named through `scope:combat_side` rather than `root` for batch 127's reason.
# Same construction as batch 155's pair, for the same reason.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 144'S RENOWN PAIR AND IT IS NOT THE TIER BANDS. Renown is
# what a family has accumulated and it moves; a tier is what a man holds today
# and it is a step, not a number. And the absolute bands say what he is - this
# says what he is NEXT TO, which is the fact that decides how the afternoon gets
# written down afterwards by people who were not here.
#
# THE TWO GATES DO NOT FIRE AT THE SAME RATE IN THE TWO POOLS, AND THAT IS
# INTENDED RATHER THAN OVERLOOKED. A knight is frequently unlanded, so the first
# gate is a commoner fact in the knight file than in the commander file, and the
# second is correspondingly rarer there - a knight two full steps ABOVE the man
# running the far side is a great lord standing in somebody's line. The templates
# below are assigned on that basis: the first goes to a commander, the second to
# a knight, and each lands where it is the unusual one.
#
# RARITY. Two steps is not a near miss. It is the distance from a count to a
# king, and it needs both men to be led and both to be that far apart on the day.
bc_the_man_across_the_field_outranks_him_twice_over_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		tier_difference = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			value <= -2
		}
	}
}

bc_he_outranks_the_man_across_the_field_twice_over_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		tier_difference = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			value >= 2
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 157 - HOW MUCH OF HIMSELF EACH MAN ACTUALLY BRINGS TO A DAY.
#
# Batch 131 opened the AI-personality surface on `ai_boldness`, batch 140 spent
# `ai_zeal` and `ai_compassion`, and batch 152 spent `ai_greed` and `ai_honor`.
# `ai_energy`, `ai_rationality`, `ai_sociability` and `ai_vengefulness` were all
# still at zero reads over the whole mod. The three prose mentions of
# `ai_energy` that a census turns up are in those three headers listing what was
# left; none of them is a call site.
#
# THE FORM AND THE CONSTANTS ARE SHIPPED AND UNAMBIGUOUS. Vanilla reads this
# attribute against the named ladder in its own scripted triggers -
# `ai_energy >= low_positive_ai_value` at
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_childhood_triggers.txt:174` and
# `ai_energy <= low_negative_ai_value` ten lines below it at `:184`, which is the
# same signed pair this batch writes one rung further out. The upper tail is
# shipped at `common/decisions/dlc_decisions/mpo/mpo_decisions.txt:4113` as
# `ai_energy >= high_positive_ai_value` and the lower at `:4187` as
# `ai_energy <= medium_negative_ai_value`. The 1.19.0.6 dump lists the full set
# of operators for the key and confines it to the character scope.
#
# BOTH GATES ARE BARE SELF-READS. The attribute is read off the scoped man about
# himself, so there is no optional link anywhere in either row and neither takes
# a guard. That is the same shape as batch 152's first gate and batch 140's pair.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Opposite tails of one signed ladder on
# one man. Nothing needs to be negated because the arithmetic already forbids it.
#
# THIS IS NOT ANY OF THE FOUR AI ROWS ALREADY SHIPPED. Boldness is how much risk
# he will accept, zeal is what he thinks the day is for, compassion is what he
# will spend other people on, greed is what he expects to be handed at the end.
# Energy is none of those. It is how much of himself he puts into anything at
# all, and a man can be bold and idle, or tireless and entirely uninterested in
# what the war is nominally about. Nor is it the trait rows: no shipped gate in
# the mod reads `lazy` or `diligent`, which appear only as `chance` weights, and
# the weights gate nothing. A man sits in one of these tails or he does not,
# whatever traits he is carrying.
#
# RARITY. The ladder is signed and the bulk of the population sits near the
# middle of it. Each gate asks for the second rung from the end on a scale of
# six, in one direction only, which is a genuinely small slice of the men who
# turn up to command or to fight.
bc_he_has_not_wanted_to_be_anywhere_for_a_long_time_trigger = {
	ai_energy <= high_negative_ai_value
}

bc_he_has_never_been_able_to_sit_still_trigger = {
	ai_energy >= high_positive_ai_value
}

# BATCH 158 - HOW LONG THE MAN HAS BEEN THE MAN IN CHARGE OF ANYTHING.
#
# `years_as_ruler` returned zero across the whole mod. The station batch reads
# `is_landed` and `highest_held_title_tier`, batch 149 counted the provinces he
# holds in his own two hands, and batch 156 measured the steps of rank between
# the two commanders. Every one of those asks what he holds. None of them has
# ever asked how long he has held it, which is the first thing the men standing
# behind him would say about him if you asked them instead.
#
# THE FORM IS SHIPPED IN BOTH TREES AND BOTH TAILS ARE SHIPPED. Vanilla reads
# `years_as_ruler >= 5` and `years_as_ruler >= 10` at
# `common/script_values/10_ach_values.txt:1674` and `:1680`. AGOT reads
# `years_as_ruler >= 3` at
# `common/decisions/agot_decisions/00_agot_lmf_decisions.txt:303` and
# `years_as_ruler < 1` at `:354` in the same file. Bare comparison, character
# scope, no target and no block. The 1.19.0.6 dump gives the full operator set.
#
# THE MINUS ONE IS THE WHOLE TRAP AND THE FIRST GATE HANDLES IT EXPLICITLY. The
# dump's own description says the key returns -1 when the character is not a
# ruler at all, so a bare `years_as_ruler <= 1` would fire for every landless
# man on the field and would not be a gate, it would be a formality. AGOT can
# write `< 1` bare because its decision has already established a ruler by the
# time that line is read; nothing here has. So the first gate carries the floor
# row as well as the ceiling row, and the two together say what is meant: he is
# a ruler, and he has been one for less than two years.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Under two years against thirty or more,
# on one number about one man.
#
# BOTH ARE BARE SELF-READS. Nothing optional is walked in either row, so neither
# takes a guard, and neither names anybody but the scoped man.
#
# BOTH IMPLY `is_landed = yes` AND THAT IS NOT A COLLISION, for batch 149's
# reason restated. `is_landed` is read in the commander pool only - the knight
# file's two mentions of it are header prose about a commander - and the two
# pools roll separately. More to the point the fact read here is duration, not
# existence: two men equally landed, equally titled and holding equally many
# provinces can sit at opposite ends of this number, and nothing shipped could
# previously tell them apart.
#
# RARITY. The first needs a man who took the seat inside the last two years and
# is already commanding a battle in the field and losing it. The second needs
# three decades of continuous rule, which on top of an ordinary accession age
# means a man well into his fifties who is riding as somebody's knight rather
# than running his own side of the day.
bc_he_has_had_the_seat_for_less_than_two_years_trigger = {
	years_as_ruler >= 0
	years_as_ruler <= 1
}

bc_he_has_held_his_own_ground_for_thirty_years_trigger = {
	years_as_ruler >= 30
}

# BATCH 159 - HOW FIRMLY ANYBODY AT ALL HOLDS THE COUNTRY THEY ARE FIGHTING IN.
#
# `county_control` returned zero over the whole mod. The six matches a census
# turns up are all `county_controller`, which is a different key and answers a
# different question: it names the man whose writ the county is under. Nothing
# has ever read the NUMBER - how much of a writ it actually is.
#
# THE GROUND ROWS ALREADY SHIPPED ASK WHOSE IT IS AND WHAT IS ON IT. Terrain in
# the six closed bands, the holding type in batch 151, the special building and
# the empty slot in 153, the holder at `:1068`, the controller at `:2145`, county
# opinion in the poor-country rows. Every one of them is about identity or about
# what has been built. None of them is about whether the writ reaches the far
# hedge, which is the fact that decides whether a stranger with an army can get a
# guide, buy bread, or find out what is behind the next rise.
#
# THE FORM IS SHIPPED IN THE EXACT SHAPE THIS NEEDS. Vanilla writes
# `scope:province.county = { county_control <= 20 }` at
# `common/activities/activity_types/tour.txt:1994`, one of five bands walked down
# the same block from `<= 100` at `:1973`. That is the same file and the same
# construction the poor-country header already cites for `county_opinion`. The
# named constants are defined at
# `common/script_values/00_county_control_values.txt:20` and `:25` -
# `low_county_control = 33` and `high_county_control_limit = 95` - and vanilla
# reads the first of them as a trigger threshold at
# `common/activities/pulse_actions/feast_pulse_actions.txt:556` and at
# `common/laws/03_imperial_policies.txt:374`.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Thirty-three or under against ninety-five
# or over, on one number about one county. Sixty-two points of daylight between
# them.
#
# THE GUARD IS THE NESTED ONE, copied from bc_the_ground_is_his_own_trigger at
# `:1068`. The battle's location is optional and the county off that province is
# reached through it, so the outer `exists` sits as a sibling, the inner `exists`
# sits inside the outer `trigger_if`, and the number is only read inside a second
# `trigger_if` that repeats it.
#
# RARITY. A county sitting at ninety-five or above is one that has been quiet and
# well administered for years, which a county with two armies standing in it
# generally has not been. A county at thirty-three or below has usually been
# taken recently, or is being held by somebody who cannot reach it, and armies do
# march through those - but most of the map, most of the time, sits in the wide
# middle that neither of these rows will look at.
bc_nobody_out_here_really_answers_to_anybody_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county }
			scope:combat_side.combat.location.county = {
				county_control <= low_county_control
			}
		}
	}
}
