bc_this_country_is_held_down_to_the_last_hedge_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county
		trigger_if = {
			limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location.county }
			scope:combat_side.combat.location.county = {
				county_control >= high_county_control_limit
			}
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 160 - WHAT KIND OF VASSAL THE ENGINE HAS ALREADY DECIDED HE IS.
#
# `has_vassal_stance` returned zero over the whole mod. The engine keeps, for
# every vassal, a running answer to the question "what does this man want out of
# serving you", and the mod has never once asked for it.
#
# THE FORM IS SHIPPED AND THE DUMP GIVES THE EXAMPLE VERBATIM. 1.19.0.6 lists
# `has_vassal_stance = glory_hound` as its own illustration of the key. Vanilla
# reads `has_vassal_stance = parochial` at
# `common/buildings/temple_citadel_buildings.txt:816` and `= zealot` twenty lines
# below it at `:836`, and `= courtly` at
# `common/activities/pulse_actions/feast_pulse_actions_oltner.txt:1260`. Bare
# key, bare value, no block and no target.
#
# THERE ARE EXACTLY FOUR STANCES AND A VASSAL HAS EXACTLY ONE. They are defined
# at `common/vassal_stances/00_vassal_stances.txt` - `courtly` at `:1`,
# `glory_hound` at `:172`, `parochial` at `:334`, `zealot` at `:510` - and each
# carries a `score` block. The engine scores all four and the man holds whichever
# wins. So THE TWO GATES BELOW ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, not by any negation
# written here: one man cannot top two of them at once. AGOT ships no
# `common/vassal_stances` folder of its own, so these are the four in play.
#
# A MAN WITH NO LIEGE HAS NO STANCE AND THAT IS A CLEAN NO. The key is read off
# the scoped character with nothing optional walked, so neither row takes a
# guard; an independent ruler simply fails both, the same way an empty iterator
# fails without needing an `exists`.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE AI-PERSONALITY ROWS AND THE DIFFERENCE IS REAL, not a technical
# dodge. `glory_hound`'s score is built out of `ai_boldness` minus `ai_compassion`
# minus half of `ai_honor`, all three of which the mod already reads as absolute
# thresholds in batches 131, 140 and 152. But a threshold asks whether a number
# clears a bar, and a stance asks which of four appetites is the strongest one in
# a particular man - a moderately bold man with no compassion and no scruples
# tops this list, and a very bold man who is also kind does not. Nor is either
# gate a subset of the thresholds: neither implies any particular value of any
# of the three, only that the sum beat three rival sums.
#
# RARITY. One stance in four, on a man who has to be somebody's vassal at all,
# on the side that is losing or winning respectively. The scoring is not uniform
# across the four - `courtly` is the broad default for a quiet courtier - so
# neither of the two chosen here is anywhere near a quarter of the field.
bc_he_is_here_because_of_what_it_will_look_like_afterwards_trigger = {
	has_vassal_stance = glory_hound
}

bc_he_would_rather_be_at_home_minding_his_own_trigger = {
	has_vassal_stance = parochial
}

# BATCH 161 - WHAT HIS PEOPLE THINK A LIFE IS ACTUALLY FOR.
#
# `has_cultural_pillar` returned zero over the whole mod. Its single appearance
# is a line of header prose in batch 150 listing what the culture surface still
# had unread. The tradition pair at `:3625` reads how a people fights, batch 150
# reads what two cultures make of each other, and neither has ever asked the one
# question the pillars answer, which is what the culture is FOR.
#
# A CULTURE HAS EXACTLY ONE ETHOS AND THAT IS WHERE THE DISJOINTNESS COMES FROM.
# Ethos is one of the four pillar slots and it holds a single value. AGOT ships
# `common/culture/pillars/00_ethos.txt` with vanilla's seven - `ethos_bellicose`
# at `:2`, `ethos_stoic` at `:27`, then bureaucratic, spiritual, courtly,
# egalitarian and communal - and adds five of its own in
# `00_agot_ethos.txt`: festive, mystic, methodical, assertive, fervent. Twelve
# in play, one per culture, so the two gates below cannot both be true of one
# man and nothing needs negating.
#
# THE FORM IS SHIPPED AND IT IS THE ONE THE MOD ALREADY USES FOR CULTURE. Vanilla
# writes `has_cultural_pillar = ethos_bellicose` at
# `common/accolade_names/00_accolade_names.txt:4424` and at
# `common/accolade_types/04_ep2_skilled_attributes.txt:224`, and
# `has_cultural_pillar = ethos_spiritual` at `00_accolade_names.txt:4406`. The
# key is read inside a `culture = { }` block, exactly as
# bc_his_people_meet_it_where_it_stands_trigger reads its tradition at `:3626`,
# and `culture` off a character is not an optional link, so neither row takes a
# guard.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE TRADITION ROWS AND IT IS NOT BATCH 150. A tradition is one
# habit out of a dozen a culture may carry; the ethos is the single word the
# engine uses for what the culture values above everything else, and a culture
# can stand and fight while being stoic, bureaucratic or communal about it.
# Neither gate implies either shipped tradition and neither tradition implies
# either gate. Batch 150 asked what two peoples make of EACH OTHER, which is a
# number between two cultures; this asks what one of them is, on its own, with
# nobody to compare it to.
#
# RARITY. Counted over AGOT's own culture files, twenty-seven of a hundred and
# fifty-nine cultures carry `ethos_bellicose` and twenty-seven carry
# `ethos_stoic`, so each gate is about one man in six before the side row and
# the readiness row are applied on top of it. That is wider than most rows in
# this file and it is deliberately the plainest fact in the batch - what is rare
# is not the culture, it is a moment where the culture is the thing worth saying.
bc_his_people_think_a_fight_is_the_point_of_the_week_trigger = {
	culture = { has_cultural_pillar = ethos_bellicose }
}

bc_his_people_think_saying_it_hurts_is_the_shameful_part_trigger = {
	culture = { has_cultural_pillar = ethos_stoic }
}

# BATCH 162 - WHAT HIS FACE HAS BEEN DOING FOR HIM SINCE HE WAS A BOY.
#
# `has_trait_rank` AND BOTH BEAUTY GROUPS CENSUSED AT ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD,
# README included. The keyword has never been used here and the two groups have
# never been named, in script or in prose. The mod reads a great deal about men
# - what they are good at, what they believe, who they answer to, what the war
# has already done to them - and has never once asked what they look like.
#
# THE FORM IS SHIPPED IN BOTH TREES AND THIS IS THE EXACT BLOCK. AGOT's own copy
# of the file family this mod extends writes
# `has_trait_rank = { trait = wounded rank = 1 }` in
# `common/combat_phase_events/00_knight_phase_events.txt` at `:32`, `:39`,
# `:47`, `:105`, `:112`, `:119` and `:292`. Vanilla writes the comparator form
# on the very groups used below - `has_trait_rank = { trait = beauty_good
# rank >= 1 }` at `events/activities/tour_activity/tour_general_events.txt:2041`
# and again at `:2368`, and `rank >= 2` at `:2091`. Nothing here is invented.
#
# THE RANK-ZERO TRAP IS REAL AND BOTH ROWS ARE WRITTEN AROUND IT. The 1.19.0.6
# documentation warns that not having the trait and holding rank 0 count as the
# same thing, so `rank < X` on its own is always true of a man who has no trait
# in the group at all. Every plain-faced man in the game would pass such a row.
# Both rows below are therefore `>=`, which reads upward from a rung that only
# exists if the trait exists, and neither needs a floor row.
#
# RANK TWO, NOT RANK ONE, AND THE WEIGHTS ARE WHY. AGOT ships the ladder at
# `common/traits/00_traits.txt` with the `group = beauty_bad` line of the first
# rung at `:7748` and the matching `group = beauty_good` line at `:7874`, and
# `beauty_good_3` at `:7958`. The birth weights run 0.5 for the first rung,
# 0.25 for the second and 0.15 for the third on both sides. Gating at rank 1
# would take nine tenths of everyone who has any of it; gating at rank 3 alone
# would make both templates something a player might never see. Rank 2 takes
# the top two rungs, which is the same choice the physique pair made at
# `bc_knight_built_for_it` by OR-ing the top two `has_trait` rows - written
# here with the keyword that does it properly instead.
#
# THE TWO GATES CANNOT BOTH BE TRUE AND THE ENGINE ENFORCES IT, not the script.
# `beauty_bad_1` lists `beauty_good` in its `opposites` block at `:7735` and
# `beauty_good_3` lists `beauty_bad` at `:7960`; the two ladders are opposed
# group to group, so no character carries a rung on both. Nothing needs
# negating, and in any case the two rows are asked about two different men.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE PHYSIQUE BATCH AND IT IS NOT THE PROWESS ROWS. Physique is
# what he can carry and how hard he is to move. Prowess is how well he fights.
# This is what happens in other men's faces before he has done anything at all,
# which is a fact about them rather than about him, and it does not predict
# either of the other two - AGOT is full of enormous ugly men and beautiful
# ones who cannot hold a shield up.
#
# RARITY. Rank two or better is one man in something like six on each side of
# the ladder before the readiness row and the side row are applied on top, and
# the far rung is genetically constrained on top of that. It is a fact that
# most men on a field do not have, in either direction.
bc_men_have_been_flinching_at_his_face_his_whole_life_trigger = {
	has_trait_rank = {
		trait = beauty_bad
		rank >= 2
	}
}

bc_men_have_been_staring_at_his_face_his_whole_life_trigger = {
	has_trait_rank = {
		trait = beauty_good
		rank >= 2
	}
}

# BATCH 163 - THE CRAFT HE ACTUALLY HAS, ON A DAY THAT DOES NOT WANT IT.
#
# NO COMMANDER-CATEGORY TRAIT HAS EVER APPEARED IN AN `is_valid` GATE ANYWHERE
# IN THIS MOD, which is a strange gap for a mod about battles. The mod HANDS
# THEM OUT: bc_style_award_effect in `bc_reputation_effects.txt:128` awards
# aggressive_attacker, unyielding_defender, flexible_leader, cautious_leader,
# reckless and organizer off a style streak, and the terrain ladder below it
# awards desert_warrior, rough_terrain_expert, winter_soldier and the rest off
# a terrain streak. The only place one is ever READ is two `chance` rows on
# winter_soldier, at `bc_knight_phase_events.txt:578` and `:5338`, and a chance
# row is a weight, not a gate. `logistician` and `reaver` censused at zero over
# the whole mod, README included.
#
# THESE TWO ARE OUTSIDE THE AWARD TABLES AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON THEY WERE
# CHOSEN. Gating on aggressive_attacker or organizer would close a loop on
# itself: this mod gives a commander the trait for fighting a certain way, and
# would then start firing moments at him for holding a trait it handed him
# three battles ago, which reads as the mod admiring its own bookkeeping.
# Neither of the two traits below can be granted by anything in this mod, in
# either ladder, so both gates are always a statement about a life the man had
# before he ever entered a chronicle.
#
# BOTH ARE SHIPPED, BOTH ARE `category = commander`, AND NEITHER IS INVENTED.
# AGOT's `common/traits/00_traits.txt` has `logistician` at `:13451` with
# `category = commander`, `flag = commander_trait_flag`, `supply_duration` and
# a pile of terrain attrition modifiers, and `reaver` at `:15373` with the same
# category and flag, `raid_speed = 0.4` and `hostile_county_attrition = -0.3`.
# Both carry XP tracks.
#
# THE TRACK IS DELIBERATELY NOT READ AND THIS IS NOT THE BEAUTY BATCH. Both
# traits have rungs, and `has_trait_rank` would reach them. It is not asked
# here, because the question is not how far along the ladder he is - it is
# whether the craft is in him at all, and the first rung already means a man
# who has spent years doing this specific thing. Reading the rung would turn a
# statement about what a man is into a statement about how decorated he is.
#
# NOTHING FORBIDS ONE MAN FROM HOLDING BOTH AND NO NEGATION IS WRITTEN. The
# commander traits are not `opposites` of each other - the mod's own note at
# `bc_reputation_effects.txt:108` records that reckless and cautious_leader are
# the only opposed pair among the twelve it can award, verified across the
# whole of AGOT's trait file. So a man could be both a quartermaster and a
# raider. The two rows below are asked about two different men on two different
# sides, and the pools roll separately, so the overlap can never produce one
# battle claiming both things about one person.
#
# RARITY. Commander traits arrive through lifestyle perks, education and a
# narrow set of events, and most men who end up leading a wing of somebody
# else's army have none of them at all. These are the two nobody takes on
# purpose either: one is the reward for having been made responsible for feeding
# an army, the other for having spent a decade taking things off people.
bc_his_whole_craft_was_used_up_before_the_first_horn_trigger = {
	has_trait = logistician
}

bc_he_learned_this_work_taking_things_off_people_trigger = {
	has_trait = reaver
}

# BATCH 164 - TWO MARKS ANOTHER SCRIPT PUT ON A MAN AND THIS MOD NEVER READ.
#
# EVERY `has_character_modifier` IN THIS MOD READS ONE OF THIS MOD'S OWN
# MODIFIERS. Sixteen call sites across the two phase-events files, the trigger
# file and bc_scripted_effects, and all sixteen name a `bc_` rung - momentum,
# faltering, pursuit, spent_horse. The mod has never once read a modifier that
# somebody else's script wrote onto a character. `agot_former_kingsguard`
# censused at zero over the whole mod, README included, and so did `berserker`.
#
# THE MODIFIER, NOT THE TRAIT, AND GETTING THIS WRONG WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A DEAD
# TEMPLATE. AGOT defines a `former_kingsguard` TRAIT at
# `common/traits/00_agot_traits.txt:392` and it is the obvious thing to reach
# for. It is also useless here: the only place AGOT ever adds it is
# `common/on_action/death.txt:2369`, inside the death handling, so no living
# character carries it. The living mark is the character modifier, added in the
# dismissal block at `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_kingsguard_effects.txt:323`
# in the same four lines that fire `remove_trait = kingsguard`. A commander gate
# written on the trait would have validated cleanly and never fired once.
#
# THIS IS THE EXACT COMPLEMENT OF bc_is_kingsguard_trigger AND THE ENGINE
# GUARANTEES IT. That gate was widened on 2026-08-17 to read the `kingsguard`
# trait as well as the seven council positions; AGOT's dismissal effect removes
# that trait and adds this modifier in one block, so a man cannot satisfy both.
# The disjointness here is not a convention this file is observing, it is
# somebody else's code enforcing it.
#
# `berserker` IS WRITTEN BY THIS EXACT FILE FAMILY AND HAS NEVER BEEN READ BY
# IT. AGOT's own `common/combat_phase_events/00_knight_phase_events.txt` hands
# it out at `:373` and `:395`, off its berserker-rage phase event, on a knight
# who went past the point of stopping. It is a battlefield fact, written in the
# same folder these templates live in, and this mod has a hundred and forty-nine
# knight templates and asked about it in none of them.
#
# NOT A SUBSET OF THE PROWESS ROWS AND THE DIFFERENCE MATTERS. Prowess says how
# good he is. This says something came apart in him once, in a fight, badly
# enough that the game wrote it down - and a man of prowess four can hold it
# while a man of prowess sixteen may never have lost hold of himself in his
# life. Neither implies the other in either direction.
#
# NOTHING FORBIDS ONE MAN FROM HOLDING BOTH AND NO NEGATION IS WRITTEN. A
# dismissed white cloak may very well be a berserker; that is arguably how he
# came to be dismissed. The two rows are asked about two different men on two
# different sides and the pools roll separately, so no single battle can claim
# both things about one person.
#
# RARITY. The modifier requires a man to have been sworn to a lifetime
# appointment and then thrown out of it, which is a small number of people in
# any run and most of them are somebody's household knight rather than a wing
# commander. The trait requires having lost control of yourself in a fight that
# the engine was watching.
bc_they_took_the_white_cloak_back_off_him_trigger = {
	has_character_modifier = agot_former_kingsguard
}

bc_something_in_him_has_come_off_its_hinges_before_trigger = {
	has_trait = berserker
}

# BATCH 165 - THE VOW A MAN BROKE AND THE VOW A MAN TOOK ON TOP OF HIS FIRST.
#
# NEITHER TRAIT HAS EVER BEEN NAMED IN THIS MOD. `broke_vows` and
# `order_member` both census at zero over the whole tree, README included. The
# mod has read faith forty-three times and every one of those readings was a
# COUNT or a comparison - how many sins, how many virtues, whether the far side
# prays to something else. Not one of them has ever asked about a specific
# promise a specific man made out loud in front of people.
#
# NEITHER IS A SIN TRAIT, SO NEITHER IS INSIDE THE `num_sinful_traits` GATE
# ABOVE. That gate counts traits a man's own faith's doctrine marks sinful.
# `broke_vows` carries no sin flag at all: it is `category = fame` at
# `common/traits/00_agot_lmf_traits.txt:17`, with `same_faith_opinion = -20`,
# `zealot_opinion = -50`, `monthly_piety = -1` and `inheritance_blocker = all`.
# It is not a moral score. It is a record of one event.
#
# `order_member` IS THE SAME CATEGORY WITH THE SAME SEVERITY POINTING THE OTHER
# WAY. `common/traits/00_traits.txt:11332`: `category = fame`, `can_not_marry`,
# `inheritance_blocker = all`, `martial = 1`, `prowess = 4`. AGOT hangs a
# per-faith name block off it at `:11350`, so a man of the Seven carrying the
# inactive `agot_warriors_son` trait reads as a Warrior's Son rather than as a
# generic order member. Both traits shut the same door on a man from opposite
# sides of it: neither one can inherit anything, ever.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT AND IT IS AGOT'S CODE THAT MAKES THEM SO, NOT A NEGATION
# WRITTEN HERE. `remove_religious_traits_effect`
# (`common/scripted_effects/00_agot_lmf_effects.txt:543`, carrying the comment
# "used for when elopement results in broken vows") strips `order_member` and
# adds `broke_vows` in the same three lines at `:567`. It does the same for
# `septon` at `:545`, `devoted` at `:562` and `silent_sister` at `:572`. A man
# who breaks an order's vows stops being in the order in the same instant, by
# somebody else's script, and this file does not have to say so itself.
#
# BOTH GRANTS ARE LIVE AND BOTH WERE CHECKED BEFORE EITHER TEMPLATE WAS
# WRITTEN. The broken side runs from eight call sites -
# `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_lmf_misc_on_actions.txt:282`,
# `00_agot_lmf_effects.txt:3386`, `00_scheme_scripted_effects.txt:15227` and
# `:15286`, `events/agot_events/agot_engagement_events.txt:2599`,
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_birth_events.txt:1864`, and
# `agot_lmf_heir_events.txt:2266` and `:15871`. The kept side is handed out by
# `common/scripted_effects/00_holy_order_effects.txt:5` and `:66`, by
# `events/religion_events/holy_order_events.txt:1194`, and by
# `common/on_action/title_on_actions.txt:980` when an order title changes
# hands. Batch 164 came within one line of shipping a gate on a trait only the
# dead can hold; this is now a step of the method rather than a lucky catch.
#
# NOT THE NIGHT'S WATCH AND NEITHER GATE CONTAINS THE OTHER. The Wall already
# has its own wrapper in this file, over AGOT's own membership check, and it
# is a different institution: a Warrior's Son is not on the Wall and a ranger
# is not in a holy order. The one place AGOT writes
# `order_member` onto a black brother is a character-restoration block at
# `events/agot_events/agot_nights_watch_events.txt:1447`, guarded by a variable
# the character was saved with, and it restores what he already had.
#
# RARITY. Holy orders are small, celibate, inheritance-blocked institutions and
# the men inside them are not the men who come into armies by birth; the broken
# side needs a man who was IN one of those institutions and left it the wrong
# way. Both are rarer than any single trait this mod has gated on so far, which
# is most of the reason for asking.
#
# BOTH ARE ROOT-RELATIVE, BARE `has_trait`, NO WALK AND NO GUARD. The first is
# read on the commander and the second on the knight.
bc_the_thing_he_swore_is_on_record_as_broken_trigger = {
	has_trait = broke_vows
}

bc_he_belongs_to_an_order_before_he_belongs_to_a_lord_trigger = {
	has_trait = order_member
}

# BATCH 166 - THE TWO MEN WHO WERE SHOWN SOMETHING BEFORE IT HAPPENED.
#
# THIS MOD HAS NEVER ONCE READ AGOT'S MAGIC. A hundred and sixty-five batches
# have asked about skill, trait, rank, terrain, weather, blood, oath, wealth,
# stress, illness and the shape of a man's house, and `greensight` and
# `dragon_dreams` both census at zero over the whole tree, README included.
# They are the two traits in this setting that say a man has been given
# information nobody gave him, and the chronicle has walked past both of them
# a hundred and sixty-five times.
#
# THE TWO DEFINITIONS ARE ALMOST THE SAME OBJECT WITH DIFFERENT ANCESTORS.
# `dragon_dreams` at `common/traits/00_agot_traits.txt:1974` is
# `category = fame` with `intrigue = 4`, `general_opinion = -10`,
# `high_valyrian_opinion = 10`, `stress_gain_mult = 0.25` and an
# `enemy_hostile_scheme_phase_duration_add`. `greensight` at `:2005` is
# `category = fame` with `intrigue = 2`, `general_opinion = -5`,
# `the_pact_religion_opinion = 5`, `monthly_piety_gain_mult = 0.25`, and the
# same stress line and the same scheme line. Both cost the same hundred in the
# ruler designer. The engine treats them as one mechanic wearing two different
# bloodlines.
#
# NOTE WHAT BOTH OF THEM DO TO OTHER PEOPLE. `general_opinion` is NEGATIVE on
# both and the faith or blood bonus is positive. A man who dreams true is worth
# more to his own kind and less to everybody else, which is the correct reading
# and is also the reason neither of these templates lets him be believed.
#
# NEITHER IS A CULTURE GATE OR A FAITH GATE AND NEITHER IS A SUBSET OF ONE.
# The mod's culture and faith rows ask what a man was raised inside.
# `agot_birth_magic_traits_effect`
# (`common/scripted_effects/00_agot_birth_effects.txt:66`) is a `random_list`
# run once at birth: `9949` weight for nothing at `:68`, `50` for the dream
# flag at `:71` behind an `any_ancestor` Targaryen test, and `1` for the green
# flag at `:96` behind an `any_ancestor` test on a greenseer faith doctrine or
# crannogman culture. Being of the right blood is a PREREQUISITE and almost
# never the outcome; the overwhelming majority of every eligible people never
# gets either flag, so neither gate can stand in for a culture row.
#
# AND THE FLAG IS NOT THE TRAIT. This is the part that makes the pair worth
# shipping. Birth writes a dormant character flag, and the flag only becomes a
# trait when somebody aims a hostile scheme at the sleeper and he sees it
# coming: `events/agot_events/agot_greensight_events.txt:134` and
# `events/agot_events/agot_dragon_dreams_events.txt:148` both sit in a
# scheme-discovery event, both consume the flag and add the trait in the same
# three lines, and both hand the man `major_stress_gain` for the privilege.
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_magic_triggers.txt:12` and `:43` are where
# the rest of AGOT reads the dormant side. Two other live grants exist -
# `events/agot_filler/00_agot_dragon_filler_events.txt:8295` and
# `events/dlc/fp2/fp2_struggle_events.txt:4560`.
#
# THESE GATES READ THE TRAIT AND NOT THE FLAG, DELIBERATELY. The flag is a
# man who might one day dream true. The trait is a man who already has, once,
# and was proved right by events he could not stop. Only the second one is a
# fact about the person standing on this field.
#
# NOTHING FORBIDS ONE MAN FROM CARRYING BOTH AND NO NEGATION IS WRITTEN. The
# birth list picks at most one, but a life is long and AGOT grants both traits
# from other places. The two rows are asked about two different men on two
# different sides and the pools roll separately, so no single battle can claim
# both things about one person.
#
# RARITY, AND IT IS TWO RARITIES MULTIPLIED. First a one-in-ten-thousand roll,
# or fifty in ten thousand for the dragon side, and only for a man whose
# ancestry already qualified. Then a dormant flag that never becomes anything
# at all unless a hostile scheme is aimed at him and he foresees it. These are
# the rarest gates in this file by a long way, which is exactly the point of
# spending two templates on them.
#
# BOTH ARE ROOT-RELATIVE, BARE `has_trait`, NO WALK AND NO GUARD. The first is
# read on the commander and the second on the knight.
bc_he_was_shown_something_once_and_it_was_real_trigger = {
	has_trait = greensight
}

bc_the_fire_in_his_sleep_belongs_to_his_blood_trigger = {
	has_trait = dragon_dreams
}

# BATCH 167 - THE WORK HE DID WITH HIS OWN HANDS AND THE THING HE IS CALLED.
#
# `torturer` AND `murderer` BOTH CENSUS AT ZERO OVER THE WHOLE TREE, README
# included. This is odd, because the file directly above already asks what a
# man's own world has convicted him of and answers it with `tyranny`. Tyranny
# is a running total of takings. Neither of these is a total of anything.
#
# THE TWO DEFINITIONS COULD NOT BE LESS ALIKE AND THAT IS WHY THEY PAIR.
# `torturer` at `common/traits/00_traits.txt:1839` is `category = lifestyle`
# with `dread_gain_mult = 0.5`, `prowess = 4`, `levy_size = 0.1`, an
# `enemy_hostile_scheme_phase_duration_add`, `ai_honor = -30` and
# `ai_vengefulness = 20`. It is a set of capabilities. `murderer` at `:12589`
# is `category = fame` with `general_opinion = -15` and a negative ruler
# designer cost and NOTHING ELSE - no skill, no dread, no scheme line. It gives
# a man no power of any kind. It is purely a thing he is called.
#
# NEITHER IS THE DREAD ROWS AND NEITHER IS THE TYRANNY ROW. Dread is a feeling
# in other people that decays the moment he stops feeding it, and this file
# reads it about twenty times. `torturer` does not raise his dread; it
# multiplies the RATE at which frightening acts convert into dread, which is a
# statement about what he is willing to do rather than about how frightened
# anybody currently is. A man can carry the trait with a dread of nothing,
# having done the work quietly on people whose absence nobody noticed.
#
# `murderer` IS THE EXACT INVERSE OF THIS FILE'S SECRET ROWS, WHICH IS THE
# POINT OF PUTTING IT ON A WINNING KNIGHT. A secret is a fact that has not got
# out. `general_opinion = -15` means this one did: it is applied to everybody
# in the world at once, with no proximity test and no expiry. The knight
# template is built on that. The men beside him are not discovering anything.
# They made their peace with it years ago and are standing next to him anyway.
#
# `sadistic` IS NOT THIS AND THE MOD ALREADY USES IT CORRECTLY. This mod reads
# `sadistic` twice, both times as a `chance` modifier and never as a gate, and
# that is the right split. `sadistic` is what a man ENJOYS. `torturer` is what
# he has DONE, repeatedly, well enough for the game to write it down. The
# commander template below keeps `sadistic` in the chance block for exactly the
# reason it is already used that way and does not promote it into the gate.
#
# NEITHER GATE CONTAINS THE OTHER. The work in the cellar is lawful in most
# realms and is done to men who are already prisoners; it does not require
# having murdered anybody. And the corridor does not require having ever owned
# a cellar. There is nothing to negate here and the two rows are asked about
# two different men on two different sides in any case.
#
# BOTH GRANTS ARE LIVE. `torturer` arrives with the intrigue perk of the same
# name - `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_character_history_perks_effects.txt`
# `:1063` adds the perk and the trait in the same block - and also from
# `00_agot_effects.txt:4234` (a wanderer random list), from
# `00_agot_citadel_effects.txt:1282`, from
# `events/agot_events/agot_ruins_events.txt:490` and from
# `agot_scenario_defiance_of_duskendale_events.txt:120`. There is a weighted
# roll for it in
# `common/scripted_character_templates/00_agot_character_templates.txt:2307`.
# `murderer` is written by the harm events when a killing lands and a killer is
# attributed - `events/harm_events.txt:7628` and `:7710`, both inside a
# `hidden_effect` beside `add_kinslayer_trait_or_nothing_effect` - and by
# `events/agot_filler/00_agot_the_north_filler_events.txt:3273` and `:3425`,
# `agot_targaryen_invasion_events.txt:2830`, `court_yearly_events.txt:3849` and
# `common/character_interactions/00_agot_religious_interactions.txt:242`.
#
# RARITY. The first needs a man who spent a perk point on the work and then
# kept doing it; the second needs a killing that both succeeded and was pinned
# on him afterwards, which is two failures of a scheme rather than one.
#
# BOTH ARE ROOT-RELATIVE, BARE `has_trait`, NO WALK AND NO GUARD. The first is
# read on the commander and the second on the knight.
bc_he_has_done_the_work_himself_in_a_cellar_trigger = {
	has_trait = torturer
}

bc_it_is_common_knowledge_that_he_killed_somebody_trigger = {
	has_trait = murderer
}

# BATCH 168 - TWO BODIES SOMEBODY ELSE'S SCRIPT HAS ALREADY RULED ON.
#
# THE MOD READS INJURY CONSTANTLY AND HAS NEVER READ THE ONE THAT IS NOT AN
# INJURY. `maimed`, `one_legged`, `one_eyed`, `disfigured`, `greyscale`, the
# three `wounded` rungs and the physique ladders are all over both phase-event
# files. `crippled` censuses at zero, and so does `drowned_man`.
#
# `crippled` IS A CATEGORY BOUNDARY IN THIS SETTING, NOT A WOUND. AGOT defines
# it at `common/traits/00_agot_traits.txt:1462` as `category = health` with
# `prowess = -30`, `martial = -3`, `diplomacy = -1`, `fertility = -0.5`,
# `dread_baseline_add = -5`, `dread_loss_mult = 0.75` and `ai_energy = -20`.
# Then it puts him inside `is_malformed_trigger`
# (`common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_lmf_trait_triggers.txt:44`) next to
# `dwarf`, `hunchbacked`, `inbred`, `albino`, `scaly`, `leper` and `scurvy`.
# That is not a list of hurts. It is a list of things the world has decided a
# man IS.
#
# AGOT'S OWN SCRIPT MAKES THIS A COMMANDER GATE AND THIS FILE DID NOT CHOOSE
# IT. `agot_has_traits_preventing_knighthood_trigger`
# (`common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_knighting_triggers.txt:2`) names
# `crippled` at `:14`, in the same OR as `blind`, `one_legged`, `one_handed`,
# `incapable`, `infirm` and all three `physique_bad` rungs, under the comment
# "Simply having any of these traits will disqualify you from knighthood."
# The knight half of this mod is closed to him by the setting itself.
#
# AND THE ENGINE STILL LETS HIM COMMAND, WHICH IS THE WHOLE TEMPLATE. There is
# no `disables_combat_leadership` on this trait and no `incapacitating` line.
# Compare `seclusion` at `common/traits/00_agot_lmf_traits.txt:1`, which
# carries both. AGOT wrote a body that may not be knighted and may still be
# handed an army, and this mod has never once said anything about that man.
#
# IT IS NOT A PROWESS ROW. The prowess rows ask how good a man is with a
# weapon in his hand. `prowess = -30` does not answer that question badly, it
# removes the question. Note also the two dread lines pointing the same way:
# the engine's own reading is that such a man is harder to be frightened of and
# then holds what fear he does get for longer than most.
#
# `drowned_man` IS THE OTHER HALF AND ITS DEFINITION CONTAINS A JOKE THE
# CHRONICLE CAN USE. `common/traits/00_agot_traits.txt:1921`: `physical = no`,
# `category = fame`, `same_faith_opinion = 10`, `clergy_opinion = 10`,
# `monthly_piety = 1`, `prowess = 2`. The engine files it as NOT PHYSICAL. The
# rite it records is holding a man under salt water until he stops and then
# working him until he starts again, which is the most physical thing in the
# whole trait file. The template is written into that gap.
#
# NOT BATCH 165'S ROW AND AGOT SAYS SO ITSELF. `order_member` is a man who
# joined a fighting order and can lose it. This is a priesthood.
# `remove_religious_traits_effect` at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_lmf_effects.txt:543` strips `order_member`
# at `:567` and adds `broke_vows` in the same breath - and strips
# `drowned_man` at `:550` and adds NOTHING. AGOT's own script holds that the
# two offices are not the same kind of promise, and neither gate contains the
# other.
#
# BOTH GRANTS ARE LIVE. `crippled` is added by
# `events/agot_events/agot_dragon_slaying_events.txt:6334`,
# `agot_religious_decision_events.txt:127` and
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_lover_events.txt:13586`, and removed again
# only behind a `heal_crippled` character flag at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_effects.txt:4439`. `drowned_man` comes from
# `00_agot_effects.txt:499` and `:518`, from
# `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_councillor_effects.txt:344`, and it is on
# the map at start from `history/characters/00_agot_char_iron_islands.txt:557`.
#
# NOTHING FORBIDS ONE MAN FROM HOLDING BOTH AND NO NEGATION IS WRITTEN. The
# rite is survivable and does not always leave a man whole. The two rows are
# asked about two different men on two different sides and the pools roll
# separately, so no single battle can claim both things about one person.
#
# RARITY. The first needs a specific catastrophe that AGOT hands out from three
# places and takes back from almost none. The second needs a man of one faith,
# holding one of its offices, who is also standing in a battle line.
#
# BOTH ARE ROOT-RELATIVE, BARE `has_trait`, NO WALK AND NO GUARD. The first is
# read on the commander and the second on the knight.
bc_his_body_gave_out_before_the_war_did_trigger = {
	has_trait = crippled
}

bc_they_held_him_under_until_he_stopped_trigger = {
	has_trait = drowned_man
}


# BATCH 169 - HOW MANY MEN HATE HIM, AND HOW MANY WOULD COME FOR HIM.
#
# THE MOD HAS READ RELATIONS THIRTY TIMES AND HAS NEVER ONCE COUNTED THEM.
# Every read in this mod is pairwise - has_relation_agot_* nine times,
# has_relation_bodyguard four, has_relation_guardian four, has_relation_ward
# four, has_relation_lover three, has_relation_friend once at
# bc_knight_phase_events.txt:1906, has_relation_rival twice at :1984 and
# :2071, and the rivalry ladder's three removal tests. All of them ask "is
# THIS man that to him", aimed at the side commander or the enemy's. The one
# count anywhere in the file, num_of_relation_favorite_child >= 1 at :2886,
# is a presence test wearing a count's clothes. Nothing has ever asked how
# many.
#
# BOTH KEYS ARE IN THE 1.19.0.6 DUMP with character scope and the full
# comparison set, and both are live in AGOT's own script with numeric bands
# rather than presence tests: num_of_relation_friend at mpo_decisions.txt:1935
# (< 3) and health_on_actions.txt:206 (>= 1), num_of_relation_rival at
# agot_lmf_feast_events.txt:538 (>= 1) and :507 (<= 4), and
# agot_lmf_heir_events.txt:7329 (< 3).
#
# THE BAND IS THREE BECAUSE ONE WOULD BE A SUPERSET. num_of_relation_rival
# >= 1 is true of every man the shipped pairwise gate is true of, which makes
# it a strict superset of bc_knight_knows_that_man's question and not a new
# one. At three the two come apart in both directions: a man can hold three
# standing enmities none of which is the commander opposite him, and he can be
# the enemy commander's rival with a count of exactly one. The same argument
# holds on the friend side against bc_knight_for_a_friend.
#
# THE ALIASES MEAN THESE COUNT MORE THAN THEIR NAMES SAY, AND THAT IS WANTED.
# `nemesis` carries relation_aliases = { rival } (00_scripted_relations.txt:81)
# and both `best_friend` (:34) and `blood_brother` (:323) carry
# relation_aliases = { friend }, so the first count includes nemeses and the
# second includes best friends and sworn brothers. Neither desc below asks
# which rung a bond sits on. The question in both is how many men, and the
# looser count is the correct one for it.
#
# THE FIRST COUNT IS PARTLY THIS MOD'S OWN WORK. bc_rivalry_effects.txt sets
# grudge, rival and nemesis on commanders who keep beating the same man, and
# clears them when the counter runs out. A campaign that has been running long
# enough for this gate to open is one where the chronicle has been writing
# into the same ledger it is now reading, which is the point of it being a
# count and not a trait.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on whichever man
# the caller has already entered through scope:combat_side, and no optional
# link is traversed to reach either. The counts themselves cannot fail; a man
# with no such relation returns zero, which is a clean no.
#
# NO NEGATION BETWEEN THEM. The two gates read two different facts about two
# different men - one about a commander's enemies, one about a knight's
# friends - and the two pools roll separately, so the usual reason for making
# a pair disjoint by construction does not apply here.
#
# THREE IS RARE ENOUGH TO BE A GATE ON BOTH SIDES, and AGOT's own content says
# so without being asked: both mpo_decisions.txt:1935 and
# agot_lmf_heir_events.txt:7329 use `< 3` as the ORDINARY branch, which is the
# same sentence as saying that three or more is the exception. Standing
# rivalries expire, friendships are hard to acquire and easy to lose to a
# death, and most commanders in most years hold none of either. A man carrying
# three at once has had a career.
bc_at_least_three_men_alive_count_him_an_enemy_trigger = {
	num_of_relation_rival >= 3
}

bc_at_least_three_men_alive_count_him_a_friend_trigger = {
	num_of_relation_friend >= 3
}


# BATCH 170 - THE DESK HE SITS AT, AND THE TONGUES HE ANSWERS IN.
#
# `is_councillor` WAS AT ZERO AND THE MOD ALREADY HAD OPINIONS ABOUT COUNCILS.
# bc_commander_phase_events.txt:4544 gates on has_council_position =
# councillor_marshal and :4609 on councillor_castellan, both of them men whose
# seat is about war. Nothing has ever asked the plainer question of whether a
# man sits on anybody's council at all, and the answer to that one is
# interesting precisely where the two shipped gates are not.
#
# SO THE FIRST GATE CARRIES BOTH SHIPPED SEATS AS EXPLICIT NEGATIONS. A bare
# `is_councillor = yes` is true of every marshal and every castellan, which
# makes it a strict superset of two gates already in the mod and therefore not
# a new question. Written with the two exclusions it becomes one: he sits on
# somebody's council and his seat is NOT one of the two that has anything to
# do with fighting. He is a chancellor, a steward, a spymaster or a spiritual
# head, which is to say a man somebody keeps for a skill that is no use at all
# on the ground he is standing on this afternoon.
#
# THE EXCLUSIONS ARE WRITTEN AS TWO `NOT` ROWS AND NOT AS ONE `NOR`. Both
# spellings compile and both mean the same thing, but two rows survive a third
# war seat being added later by anybody who reads this comment, and a `NOR`
# invites somebody to add the third key inside it without noticing that the
# gate has changed shape.
#
# `has_council_position` TAKES NO GUARD AND NEITHER DOES `is_councillor`. Both
# are bare character-scope reads on a man the caller has already entered, and
# a man on no council answers no to all three rows without traversing
# anything. The engine keys are in the 1.19.0.6 dump; vanilla uses
# `is_councillor = yes` live at 00_vassal_interactions.txt:1645 and
# `is_councillor = no` at 00_scheme_interactions.txt:2407.
#
# THE SECOND GATE IS THE LANGUAGE COUNT, WHICH IS NOT THE LANGUAGE QUESTION
# THE MOD ALREADY ASKS. bc_shares_court_language_trigger and its negation at
# :2445 and :2454 ask whether one man can follow the orders being shouted
# around another man - a fact about a pair. `num_of_known_languages` asks how
# many tongues one man carries, which neither implies nor is implied by it: a
# man with three languages may hold none that the far commander's hall uses,
# and a man who shares that hall's tongue may know only his own.
#
# THREE IS VANILLA'S OWN BAR AND IT IS A HIGH ONE. The tradition at
# 00_ritual_traditions.txt:1585 pairs `num_of_known_languages >= 3` with
# `piety_level >= 3` and charges its unfulfilled-criteria penalty to anybody
# who misses either, which is vanilla saying in script that three tongues is
# an accomplishment rather than a baseline. Vanilla also keeps a
# `language_soft_cap` named value (00_culture_values.txt:516) for exactly the
# reason that most characters never approach it. A man who holds three has
# spent years somewhere he was not born.
#
# THE TWO GATES ARE NOT NEGATED AGAINST EACH OTHER, for the standing reason:
# they read two different facts about two different men, one about a
# commander's employment and one about a knight's education, and the two pools
# roll separately.
bc_his_seat_on_the_council_has_nothing_to_do_with_war_trigger = {
	is_councillor = yes
	NOT = { has_council_position = councillor_marshal }
	NOT = { has_council_position = councillor_castellan }
}

bc_he_can_follow_three_tongues_without_help_trigger = {
	num_of_known_languages >= 3
}


# BATCH 171 - THE NUMBER BEHIND THE NAME.
#
# `has_trait_xp` APPEARED NOWHERE IN THIS MOD IN A HUNDRED AND SEVENTY
# BATCHES. Every trait read here, and there are hundreds of them, is
# `has_trait = x`: a yes or a no about a name. But a large family of the
# traits this mod already reads are TRACKED traits, and the engine keeps a
# number from 0 to 100 behind each of them. `unyielding_defender` at 33 and
# `unyielding_defender` at 100 are the same key and the same word, and every
# gate in this mod has been unable to tell them apart.
#
# THE FIRST GATE IS THE TOP OF A TRACK. `military_engineer` (00_traits.txt:
# 12004) is `category = commander` with `flag = commander_trait_flag` and a
# single unnamed `track` with rungs at 33, 66 and 100. The rungs are not
# cosmetic: at 66 the trait starts granting `advantage = 1`, and at 100 it
# grants `advantage = 2`, `martial = 1` and a fourth stack of
# `siege_phase_time = -0.1`. `value >= 100` is therefore the actual ceiling of
# the track and not an arbitrary band.
#
# THE TRAIT IS AT ZERO IN THIS MOD, SO THERE IS NO SUBSET PROBLEM. This is
# worth stating because there would have been one almost anywhere else:
# `has_trait_xp = { trait = X value >= n }` implies `has_trait = X`, so
# writing this against `aggressive_attacker`, `unyielding_defender`,
# `organizer`, `cautious_leader`, `winter_soldier` or any of the terrain
# experts would have produced a strict subset of a gate already shipped.
# `military_engineer` and `tourney_participant` were both at zero occurrences
# over the whole mod, which is why they are the two the batch uses.
#
# NO `track` KEY ON THE FIRST, A `track` KEY ON THE SECOND, AND THAT IS THE
# RULE RATHER THAN A CHOICE. The 1.19.0.6 dump: "Track name is required if the
# trait has multiple tracks, otherwise should not be provided."
# `military_engineer` has one track and vanilla's own name block for it
# (00_traits.txt:12004) omits the key. `tourney_participant` has four -
# `foot`, `bow`, `horse` and `wit` (00_traits.txt:15072) - and every read
# vanilla makes of it names one.
#
# SIXTY-FIVE IS VANILLA'S OWN SECOND RUNG on all four of those tracks, which
# run 30, 65, 100. `foot` is the one that means the melee on the ground rather
# than the horse, the bow or the wit, and it is the only one of the four that
# describes what a man is doing while a battle is happening to him.
#
# BOTH READS ARE BARE CHARACTER-SCOPE AND TAKE NO GUARD. A man without the
# trait at all returns a clean no from `has_trait_xp` rather than erroring,
# which is why vanilla's own icon block at 00_traits.txt:14786 stacks four of
# them inside one `AND` without testing `has_trait` first.
#
# NEITHER GATE IS RARE BY ACCIDENT. The first is the literal maximum of a
# commander track, reached by a man who has spent a working lifetime on the
# one part of war that involves arithmetic. The second is the upper half of a
# tourney track, which a man only fills by turning up to the lists year after
# year and winning on foot when he gets there. Neither is a thing that happens
# to somebody; both are things a man did on purpose for twenty years.
#
# NO NEGATION BETWEEN THEM. Two different facts about two different men, on
# two tracks that share nothing, and the two pools roll separately.
bc_nobody_alive_builds_a_siege_engine_better_trigger = {
	has_trait_xp = {
		trait = military_engineer
		value >= 100
	}
}

bc_he_has_gone_deep_into_the_foot_melees_trigger = {
	has_trait_xp = {
		trait = tourney_participant
		track = foot
		value >= 65
	}
}


# BATCH 172 - MORE THAN HE CAN HOLD, AND MORE THAN HE CAN CARRY.
#
# TWO NUMBERS THE ENGINE KEEPS ABOUT EVERY CHARACTER AND THIS MOD HAS NEVER
# ONCE LOOKED AT. `domain_limit`, `domain_limit_available` and
# `domain_limit_percentage` were all at zero over the whole mod, and so were
# `current_weight` and `base_weight`. The mod has a hundred and seventy-one
# batches of opinions about what a man is, what he did and who he knows, and
# has never asked how much land is in his hands or how much of him there is.
#
# THE FIRST IS NOT A STATION GATE AND NOT A WEALTH GATE. Station is tier and
# the mod reads it eighteen times through `highest_held_title_tier`; wealth is
# `gold` and the mod reads that in every direction including `gold < 0` twice.
# `domain_limit_available < 0` is neither. It is the gap between what a man
# holds personally and what the game says one man can administer, and it goes
# negative for reasons that have nothing to do with rank or with money: an
# inheritance that landed all at once, a conquest he could not hand out, a
# title he took back off somebody and has not yet found a use for. A duke can
# be comfortably inside it and a count can be two over.
#
# `< 0` IS VANILLA'S OWN LINE AND IT IS THE ONE THE GAME SHOUTS ABOUT.
# 00_realm_actions.txt:83 uses exactly `domain_limit_available < 0` as the
# condition for the important action that tells a player he is over his limit,
# and 09_dlc_mpo_scripted_effects.txt:655 uses the same comparison. The dump
# is explicit that the negative side is intended: "Negative values also work
# for checking characters that are above their limit."
#
# THE SECOND IS THE PLAINEST FACT ABOUT A MAN THAT THIS MOD HAS NEVER READ.
# `current_weight` is character scope with the full comparison set and the
# dump's own example is `current_weight > 10`. `overweight_value` is a vanilla
# named value, defined as 50 in 10_health_values.txt:367 beside
# `underweight_value = -50`, and vanilla compares against it directly at
# 00_activity_scripted_modifiers.txt:2866 and :2962. Using the named value
# rather than the literal means that if a patch moves the line, this gate
# moves with it.
#
# NO WEIGHT TRAIT IS INVOLVED AND THERE IS THEREFORE NO SUBSET PROBLEM. The
# mod reads no weight-related trait anywhere - `obese`, `overweight`,
# `underweight` and `malnourished` were all at zero - so this gate is not a
# narrowing of anything already shipped. It is also not the same question as a
# trait would be: the number is continuous and the trait is a threshold with
# its own acquisition rules, and a man can sit above the line for a season
# without the game ever having handed him a word for it.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on a man the
# caller has already entered. A landless man has no domain to be over, which
# resolves as a clean no rather than an error, and every character has a
# weight whether anybody has ever remarked on it or not.
#
# RARITY IS DIFFERENT ON THE TWO SIDES AND BOTH ARE FINE. Being over the
# domain limit is a state the AI actively works to leave, because the penalty
# for it is severe, so it is a snapshot of a man caught mid-reshuffle. Sitting
# above the weight line is not rare in the population at large, but sitting
# above it while also being a knight who is winning a battle is a much smaller
# set, and the second half of that sentence is doing the work.
#
# NO NEGATION BETWEEN THEM. Two different facts about two different men, one
# about a map and one about a body, and the two pools roll separately.
bc_he_holds_more_ground_than_one_man_can_run_trigger = {
	domain_limit_available < 0
}

bc_there_is_a_great_deal_of_him_trigger = {
	current_weight >= overweight_value
}


# BATCH 173 - WHICH OF HIS FIVE IS THE BEST ONE.
#
# THE MOD READS SKILL NUMBERS CONSTANTLY AND HAS NEVER ONCE ASKED WHICH NUMBER
# IS HIS LARGEST. A hundred and some `add = 30` rows weigh a man against an
# absolute bar - `prowess >= 12`, `martial >= 12`, `stewardship >= 12` - and
# two gates at bc_scripted_triggers.txt:332 and :340 read the enemy commander's
# `martial` against 16 and 8. All of those are thresholds. `highest_skill` and
# `highest_skill_including_prowess` are the other question entirely: not how
# much of a thing he has, but which of the things he has is the most of him.
#
# A THRESHOLD AND A RANKING ARE NOT SUBSETS OF EACH OTHER IN EITHER DIRECTION.
# A man with diplomacy 8 can have diplomacy as his best skill, because the rest
# of him is worse; a man with diplomacy 16 can have martial 18 and fail this
# same gate. The two questions cut the population across each other, which is
# why neither one narrows the other.
#
# THE TWO KEYS DIFFER ON EXACTLY ONE THING AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS HERE.
# `highest_skill` is the dump's "highest skill (excluding Prowess)"; the other
# includes it. Vanilla's steal_herd_outcome_events.txt:295 passes `prowess` to
# the including form, which is the proof that `prowess` is a legal argument to
# it and not to the other, and 00_skill_effects.txt:27 uses the same key as a
# bare `trigger = ` alias. AGOT branches five ways on the excluding form at
# yearly_events_7.txt:3739-3759 and picks a character's idle portrait animation
# off it at b_agot_animations.txt:53-113, so both forms are live in this build.
#
# TIES PASS. The dump says so twice, in both entries: "True even when there are
# multiple skills with the same highest value." A man who is equally the best
# talker and the best scholar he could be passes the first gate below, and that
# is correct - the gate asks whether nothing about him is better, not whether
# something about him is unique.
#
# THE PAIR IS DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The first gate carries an explicit
# `NOT = { highest_skill_including_prowess = prowess }`, so a man whose arm
# beats all five of his skills cannot be the subject of it. Without that line
# the first would have been the weaker statement - diplomacy leads the five,
# never mind the body - and the two would have overlapped on every charming man
# who can also fight. With it, the first says the best thing about him is that
# men like him, full stop, and the second says the best thing about him is his
# arm, full stop, and no man alive satisfies both.
#
# RARITY. Five skills split roughly five ways before ties, and the losing side
# and the prowess exclusion both cut into that fifth. The second gate is not
# rare among knights, who are frequently chosen for exactly this, but a knight
# whose body outranks all five of his skills AND who is winning AND whose phase
# has come apart into a scramble is a much smaller set than the first clause on
# its own.
#
# NO GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on a man the caller has already
# entered. Every character has five skills and a prowess whether anybody has
# ever ranked them or not.
bc_the_best_thing_about_him_is_that_men_like_him_trigger = {
	highest_skill = diplomacy
	NOT = { highest_skill_including_prowess = prowess }
}

bc_the_best_thing_about_him_is_his_own_arm_trigger = {
	highest_skill_including_prowess = prowess
}


# BATCH 174 - WHAT WAS SETTLED BEFORE HE WAS BORN.
#
# THE MOD HAS READ THE BAD HALF OF A MAN'S INHERITANCE SINCE BATCH SEVENTY-SIX
# AND HAS NEVER READ THE GOOD HALF, OR THE ARRANGEMENT THAT PRODUCED EITHER.
# bc_his_body_was_dealt_to_him_trigger is `num_of_bad_genetic_traits >= 1` and
# it is a gate about being written off. `num_of_good_genetic_traits` and
# `parent_relatedness` were both at zero over the whole mod. The first is the
# same engine count with the sign reversed, and it is not a subset of anything
# shipped, because a count of the good ones says nothing at all about how many
# bad ones sit beside it.
#
# THE SECOND GATE PINS THE FIRST OFF THE SHIPPED ONE BY CONSTRUCTION. Reading
# `num_of_good_genetic_traits >= 2` on its own would have overlapped with
# bc_his_body_was_dealt_to_him_trigger on every man who was handed a strong
# back and a bad hip in the same hour, and the two moments would have been
# fighting over the same subject. `num_of_bad_genetic_traits = 0` beside it -
# the dump's own worked example for that key - makes the statement whole rather
# than partial: not that some of what he was given was good, but that all of it
# was.
#
# `parent_relatedness` IS NOT A TRAIT READ AND IS NOT ABOUT HIM. It is the
# engine's own arithmetic on the two people who made him, documented as "the
# percentage of shared DNA between the character's parents", traversing four
# generations and sixty-two ancestors, with the dump's own worked comparison
# written as `relatedness > 0.25`, where 0.0 is unrelated and 1.0 is identical
# twins. A man can carry `inbred` and have parents who were strangers, and a
# man can be the child of a brother and a sister and carry nothing at all. That
# is why this is a different question from the trait count and not a narrowing
# of it.
#
# 0.25 IS THE THRESHOLD AND 0.125 IS THE REASON IT IS NOT LOWER. Full siblings
# sit at 0.5, half-siblings and an uncle with his niece sit at 0.25, and first
# cousins sit at 0.125. In this setting a marriage between first cousins is not
# a scandal, it is house policy, and a gate that opened at 0.125 would fire for
# a large slice of every noble house on the map. A gate that fires for nearly
# everybody is not a gate. `>= 0.25` takes the sibling, the half-sibling and
# the uncle, and stops exactly where the ordinary begins.
#
# NO NEGATION BETWEEN THE TWO. They are two different facts about two different
# men - one about what a man was handed and one about who handed it to him -
# and the commander and knight pools roll separately, so the pair below can
# never be competing to describe the same afternoon.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads. A character with
# no recorded parents resolves the second as a clean no rather than an error,
# which is the correct answer to a question about an arrangement nobody wrote
# down.
bc_everything_he_was_born_with_was_good_trigger = {
	num_of_good_genetic_traits >= 2
	num_of_bad_genetic_traits = 0
}

bc_his_father_and_mother_were_near_kin_trigger = {
	parent_relatedness >= 0.25
}


# BATCH 175 - HOW MANY WORDS THERE ARE FOR HIM.
#
# THE MOD NAMES PERSONALITY TRAITS CONSTANTLY AND HAS NEVER ONCE COUNTED THEM.
# `has_trait = brave`, `has_trait = patient`, `has_trait = arrogant` appear on
# chance blocks all through both event files, and every one of those asks
# whether a particular word is on a particular man. `number_of_personality_
# traits` asks the other thing: how many words there are on him at all. It was
# at zero over the whole mod, as were `number_of_traits` and
# `number_of_lifestyle_traits`.
#
# A NAME AND A COUNT DO NOT NARROW EACH OTHER. A man with four personality
# traits may or may not be brave; a brave man may have that as his only word or
# as one of four. Neither gate below is a subset or a superset of any
# `has_trait` row already shipped, in either direction, and the same argument
# was made and checked at batch one hundred and seventy-one for `has_trait_xp`
# and at one hundred and seventy-three for `highest_skill`.
#
# BOTH THRESHOLDS ARE VANILLA'S OWN NAMED VALUES AND NEITHER IS A LITERAL.
# 00_basic_values.txt:1359-1361 defines `personality_trait_minimum = 2`,
# `personality_trait_standard = 3` and `personality_trait_limit = 4` in three
# consecutive lines, and vanilla compares against the last of them directly -
# yearly_events_4.txt:1371 and 00_prison_interactions.txt:4864 both gate trait
# gains on `number_of_personality_traits < personality_trait_limit`. Writing 4
# and 2 as literals here would work today and would quietly drift the day a
# patch decides a man can hold five words. Written this way, the gates move
# with the game.
#
# THE PAIR IS DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION AND THE GAP BETWEEN THEM IS THE WHOLE
# ORDINARY POPULATION. Four or more on one side, fewer than two on the other,
# and everybody the game handed its standard three to falls between the two and
# is described by neither. That gap is the point: this is a gate about the men
# at the ends of the distribution, not a gate about having a personality.
#
# RARITY IS REAL ON BOTH ENDS. The engine hands out three by default and treats
# four as the ceiling, so the first gate takes only men who are full up, and
# the second takes only men the game never finished writing - a courtier who
# came out of a pool thin, a child who was given one word and grew up before
# anybody added another. Neither is common, and both then require the man to be
# standing in a battle on the correct side of it.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope counts on a man the
# caller has already entered, and a man with no personality traits at all
# answers the second one truthfully rather than erroring.
bc_there_is_no_room_in_him_for_another_word_trigger = {
	number_of_personality_traits >= personality_trait_limit
}

bc_one_word_covers_the_whole_of_him_trigger = {
	number_of_personality_traits < personality_trait_minimum
}


# BATCH 176 - HOW MANY OF THESE HE HAS BEEN IN.
#
# THE MOD HAS READ A MAN'S YEARS AND HAS NEVER READ HIS WARS. `age` is gated at
# 21, 45, 55 and 65 across the two event files, `years_as_ruler` carries batch
# one hundred and fifty-eight's pair at under two and thirty or more, and
# `participated_wars` was at zero over the whole mod. Years are how long the
# world has had him. Wars are how many times it has asked him this particular
# question, and the two numbers come apart constantly: a man can hold a quiet
# seat for twenty years and be standing in his first one this afternoon, and a
# man can inherit at forty and be in his fourth inside a decade.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_the_green_boy AND IT IS NOT BATCH 158'S PAIR. The green boy
# reads `age <= 21` and is about a young man leading. The tenure pair reads
# `years_as_ruler` and is about how long the seat has been his. Neither of them
# can tell you whether a man has ever done this before, and that is the only
# thing either gate below asks.
#
# THE CURRENT WAR COUNTS, WHICH IS WHY THE LOW GATE IS `<= 1` AND NOT `= 0`.
# Vanilla's only use of the key in the whole game is
# 00_reactive_advice.txt:1030, `participated_wars >= 2`, with the comment "you
# are starting your 2nd war" written beside it, so the war a man is presently
# in is included in the count. `= 0` would be unreachable for anybody standing
# in a battle - a gate that can never fire is worse than no gate - and `<= 1`
# is the honest way to write "this is the first".
#
# BOTH CARRY `is_landed = yes` AND THAT IS DELIBERATE RATHER THAN DECORATIVE.
# The engine counts wars a character has been in as a participant, and a
# landless man's count is not a record of what he has done with his life; it is
# a record of what the engine bothered to write down about him. Requiring land
# makes the number mean the same thing on both sides of the pair, and the man
# it then describes - a lord with a war record, or a lord without one - is a
# man the mod can say something true about.
#
# THE `is_landed` IMPLICATION IS NOT A COLLISION, for batch 149's reason
# restated at 158. `is_landed` is read in the commander pool only, the knight
# file's mentions of it are header prose about a commander, and the two pools
# roll separately. The fact read here is a count of wars in any case, not the
# existence of land.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One or fewer against eight or more, on one number
# about one man, with the whole ordinary middle described by neither.
#
# RARITY. The first needs a landed man who has reached a battlefield without
# ever having been in a war before and is losing when he gets there. The second
# needs eight wars on one living man, which is a career rather than an
# accident, on somebody who is riding as a knight under another man's banner
# rather than commanding.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both rows are bare character-scope reads on a man the
# caller has already entered.
bc_he_has_never_had_to_do_this_before_trigger = {
	is_landed = yes
	participated_wars <= 1
}

bc_this_is_the_eighth_time_for_him_trigger = {
	is_landed = yes
	participated_wars >= 8
}


# BATCH 177 - WHETHER THE MAN OPPOSITE IS THE SAME KIND OF MAN.
#
# THE MOD HAS COMPARED THE TWO COMMANDERS SEVERAL WAYS AND HAS NEVER COMPARED
# THEIR CHARACTER. `bc_foe_is_kin`, `bc_foe_is_house`, `bc_foe_is_countryman`
# and `bc_foe_is_heathen` read the other man's blood, house, tongue and faith;
# `bc_foe_is_formidable` and `bc_foe_is_outmatched` read his martial at 16 and
# 8; `bc_the_name_opposite_outweighs_the_name_here` reads his renown; and
# `bc_he_shares_an_afternoon_with_the_man_opposite` reads whether the two of
# them stand in the same memory. All of that is identity, ability, standing and
# history. None of it is what the two men are LIKE. Over the whole mod
# `number_of_traits_in_common`, `number_of_personality_traits_in_common`,
# `number_of_opposing_traits`, `number_of_opposing_personality_traits`,
# `number_of_virtue_traits_in_common` and `trait_compatibility` all returned
# zero.
#
# PERSONALITY TRAITS, NOT ALL TRAITS. `number_of_traits_in_common` counts every
# trait either man carries, so two commanders who both took a martial education
# and both walk with a limp come out "alike" on a number that has nothing to do
# with how either of them will behave this afternoon. The personality variants
# count only the words the game uses to predict what a man does, and that is
# the only comparison worth putting in a chronicle about a decision.
#
# THREE IS DELIBERATELY ABOVE VANILLA'S OWN BARS. Vanilla asks for two in
# common at `councillor_spouse_diplomacy_events.txt:1344-1347` and for one
# opposing at `yearly_events_4.txt:154-157`, and in both places the result is
# an opinion nudge rather than a gate. Two shared words between two nobles is a
# coincidence and one opposing word is the ordinary state of two strangers.
# Three of either is a man looking across a field at somebody assembled from
# the same short list, or at somebody assembled from its far end.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE CONSTRUCTION IS BATCH 175'S. Vanilla sets
# `personality_trait_limit = 4` at `00_basic_values.txt:1361`, so a man who
# shares three of his words with the other has at most one word left to oppose
# him with and cannot reach three opposing. The two gates cannot both pass on a
# character at or under that limit, which is every character the engine handed
# his own traits to.
#
# BOTH TAKE THE STANDING FAR-SIDE GUARD. Naming
# `scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander` as a trigger TARGET is a read,
# so each gate carries `exists` as a sibling and repeats it inside a
# `trigger_if` before the comparison is asked, exactly as
# `bc_foe_is_formidable_trigger` and
# `bc_he_shares_an_afternoon_with_the_man_opposite_trigger` already do.
#
# RARITY. Three shared personality traits, drawn from a list of thirty-odd by
# men who carry three or four each, is uncommon between any two characters, and
# asking for it between the two specific men giving orders on opposite sides of
# one field is rarer again. Three opposing is rarer still, because opposition
# is defined pairwise on a much shorter list than the traits themselves.
bc_the_man_opposite_is_the_same_kind_of_man_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		number_of_personality_traits_in_common = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			value >= 3
		}
	}
}

bc_nothing_in_him_agrees_with_the_man_opposite_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		number_of_opposing_personality_traits = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
			value >= 3
		}
	}
}


# BATCH 178 - THE WRITTEN RULE HE HOLDS HIS OWN MEN BY.
#
# THE WHOLE REALM-LAW SYSTEM RETURNED ZERO OVER THE MOD. `has_realm_law` zero,
# `has_realm_law_flag` zero, `has_realm_law_in_group` zero,
# `realm_law_group_at_maximum_level` zero, `realm_law_group_at_minimum_level`
# zero. A hundred and seventy-seven batches have read who is sworn to whom -
# `is_vassal_of`, `is_liege_or_above_of`, `is_powerful_vassal`,
# `is_independent_ruler`, batch 141's homage rows, batch 142's rosters - and not
# one of them has ever read the written rule all of that runs under. Homage
# is a relationship between two men. A law is a paragraph that outlives both
# of them and decides what the relationship is allowed to demand.
#
# AGOT SHIPS ITS OWN `00_realm_laws.txt` AND KEEPS THE VANILLA GROUP. Its
# `crown_authority` is declared at line 22 with `cumulative = yes` and
# `default = crown_authority_1`, and the four levels sit at lines 29, 56, 144
# and 238. So a ruler under this mod holds exactly one of `crown_authority_0`
# through `crown_authority_3` at any moment, and holds it because somebody
# moved it off the default.
#
# THE LEVELS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN ASKED FOR AS A GROUP MAXIMUM. The dump gives
# `realm_law_group_at_maximum_level` and `realm_law_group_at_minimum_level` as
# "does the scoped character have the maximum/minimum level of the realm law
# group given", and says nothing at all about what either returns for a
# character who holds no law in that group - a tribal ruler runs
# `tribal_authority` instead, and a landless man runs nothing. `has_realm_law`
# has no such hole: it is false when the man does not hold that law, for any
# reason at all, including having no realm to hold it over.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE ENGINE ENFORCES IT. The group is cumulative
# and a ruler holds one level of it, so the top and the bottom cannot both pass
# on the same man. There are two whole levels between them and they are the two
# that most of Westeros is actually sitting on.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 141 AND NOT BATCH 142. Those read links - whether this man
# is that man's vassal, whether this man employs that man as a knight. Both
# gates here read a paragraph in a lawbook and neither of them names another
# character at all.
#
# RARITY. The default is `crown_authority_1`, so neither gate can pass on a
# realm nobody has legislated in. The first needs three increases pushed
# through a council that has to be talked into every one of them; the second
# needs a ruler who went the other way, which is a thing men do when they are
# buying something with it.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both rows are bare character-scope reads on a man the
# caller has already entered.
bc_the_law_in_his_realm_is_absolute_trigger = {
	has_realm_law = crown_authority_3
}

bc_the_law_in_his_realm_is_a_courtesy_trigger = {
	has_realm_law = crown_authority_0
}

# BATCH 179 - THE HALL HE KEEPS AND WHAT HIS RANK EXPECTS.
#
# THE COURT GRANDEUR SYSTEM RETURNED ZERO OVER THE MOD.
# `court_grandeur_current` zero, `court_grandeur_current_level` zero,
# `court_grandeur_base` zero, `court_grandeur_minimum_expected_level` zero. A
# hundred and seventy-eight batches have asked what a man owns, what he is
# owed and what he owes, and not one of them has asked what the place he
# lives in looks like against what his rank is assumed to require.
#
# THE ENGINE KEEPS TWO NUMBERS ABOUT A MAN'S COURT. One is what the court
# actually is - the grandeur it has earned from its rooms, its artifacts, its
# servants and the amenities he pays to keep running. The other is what a man
# of his tier is expected to keep, which rises with the titles and does not
# care how he came by them. Most halls sit at the expected number, because a
# man notices when his own roof embarrasses him and does something about it
# before anybody says so out loud. The two gates below read the men on either
# side of that.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE MONEY QUESTION AND NOT THE ARTIFACT QUESTION. What is in
# his treasury is a number he can change this afternoon, and what hangs on
# his wall is one object. Grandeur is neither. It is the standing condition
# of a place he is not currently in, measured against what his rank is
# assumed to require, and it moves over years rather than days.
#
# BOTH GATES OPEN WITH `court_grandeur_current_level >= 1`. The engine
# returns level 0 for anybody who has no court at all, and a bare zero would
# then read as a hall far below the expectation rather than as the absence of
# a hall. AGOT guards the same read the same way in its own interactions
# before it compares the two levels.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One row is `<` and the other is `>` on the same
# pair of values, so no man can pass both, and the very common case of a hall
# sitting exactly at the expected level passes neither. No negation is needed
# and none is written.
#
# RARITY. A hall has to be pushed off the expected level and held there.
# Below it means the money went somewhere else for long enough to show in the
# stonework; above it means he bought grandeur his tier never asked him for
# and has kept paying for it since. The ordinary landed man is at neither.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both rows are bare character-scope reads on a man
# the caller has already entered.
bc_his_hall_falls_short_of_his_rank_trigger = {
	court_grandeur_current_level >= 1
	court_grandeur_current_level < court_grandeur_minimum_expected_level
}

bc_his_hall_outshines_his_rank_trigger = {
	court_grandeur_current_level >= 1
	court_grandeur_current_level > court_grandeur_minimum_expected_level
}

# BATCH 180 - HOW MANY OF HIS OWN ARE WAITING AT HOME.
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER COUNTED A MAN'S CHILDREN. `any_child` returned a single
# hit over the whole tree and that hit is the substring sitting inside
# `any_child_of_concubine` in a header at line 4939; `num_of_children` is not
# in the 1.19.0.6 dump at all; `has_children` and `is_parent_of` returned
# zero. A hundred and seventy-nine batches have read who a man is sworn to,
# who he made, who he owes and who he married, and not one of them has read
# who is at home waiting for him to come back.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE SPOUSE QUESTION AND NOT THE HEIR QUESTION. `is_married` is
# read fifteen times and reads a living arrangement. `any_heir_title` carries
# the inheritance pair and reads what a man stands to receive. Neither counts
# the people who exist because of him, and that count comes apart from both
# constantly: a childless man can be married and stand to inherit a kingdom,
# and a man with six children can have neither.
#
# THE LOW GATE IS `count < 1` BECAUSE THAT IS HOW VANILLA WRITES IT.
# 00_adoption.txt:30 asks `any_child = { count < 1 }` for exactly this
# condition, and `count < 2` sits beside it at 00_adoption.txt:271.
#
# THE LOW GATE ALSO CARRIES `age >= 45` AND THAT IS NOT DECORATION. A man of
# twenty with no children has a life in front of him and the absence says
# nothing about him at all. At forty-five it has stopped being a delay and
# become the shape of the life, and that is the only version of the fact
# worth writing a moment about.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_knight_the_old_sword, which gates `prowess >= 8` with
# `age >= 55` and is about a body that has been doing this too long. That
# gate sits ten years further on, and the argument here is a count of
# children rather than a count of years.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Six or more and fewer than one cannot both be
# true of the same man. No negation is needed and none is written.
#
# RARITY. Six living children is a full nursery that came through, which is
# not the common outcome, and a man who has reached forty-five with nobody is
# rarer still. Most men are somewhere between the two, and the between passes
# neither gate.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. `any_child` is a character-scope list read on a man
# the caller has already entered, and an empty list is a valid answer to it
# rather than an error.
bc_there_are_six_of_them_at_home_trigger = {
	any_child = { count >= 6 }
}

bc_there_is_nobody_of_his_at_home_trigger = {
	age >= 45
	any_child = { count < 1 }
}

# BATCH 181 - WHETHER THE OTHER MAN'S LAND TOUCHES HIS.
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER ASKED WHO BORDERS WHOM. `character_is_realm_neighbor` and
# `character_is_land_realm_neighbor` both returned zero over the whole tree,
# and the eight hits on the word "neighbour" are every one of them header
# prose. A hundred and eighty batches have read what two men are to each
# other - sworn, kin, married, owed, hated, knighted by the same hand - and
# not one of them has read the plainest thing two lords in Westeros can be to
# each other, which is next to each other.
#
# THE ENGINE'S DEFINITION IS NARROWER THAN THE ENGLISH WORD. The dump gives
# it as "is the scoped character a realm neighbor of the target? Meaning
# they're independent or has the same liege, and border you". Two vassals of
# two different kings do not qualify however close their fields lie. What
# does qualify is two independent houses whose lands meet, or two men who
# kneel to the same crown and are fighting anyway. Both of those are the
# ordinary shape of a Westerosi war and neither of them is the ordinary shape
# of a Westerosi battle, most of which are fought by somebody's vassal
# against somebody else's.
#
# VANILLA WRITES IT EXACTLY THIS WAY. `character_is_land_realm_neighbor =
# scope:attacker` at `03_fp2_wars.txt:40` and `07_ep3_wars.txt:4199`, and
# negated at `01_fp1_wars.txt:108` and `06_ce1_wars.txt:365`. The land
# variant is used in both gates below rather than the plain one, because a
# border that exists only across water is not a thing a man rides over.
#
# TWO DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT MEN, SO NO NEGATION IS WRITTEN. The
# first asks whether the man commanding the far side is the lord whose fields
# meet his. The second asks whether the man he is fighting under is. A knight
# can pass the second while his own commander passes the first, and that is
# not a collision - the two pools roll separately and the two gates name
# different targets. This is the standing exception, precedent at batches
# 110, 113, 118, 120, 123 and after.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_he_is_sworn_to_the_man_leading_this_side_trigger. That reads
# a relation between two men and this reads the map. A hedge knight sworn to
# a lord he has served for twenty years holds no land at all and fails the
# second gate; a lord who has never sworn anything to the man beside him and
# simply came when the call went out passes it.
#
# BOTH TAKE THE STANDING GUARD. Naming a side commander as the TARGET of a
# trigger is a read, so each gate carries `exists` as a sibling and repeats
# it inside a `trigger_if` before the read, in the shape
# bc_foe_is_formidable_trigger uses.
#
# RARITY. A landless man borders nobody and fails both. Of the landed, the
# engine's independent-or-same-liege requirement cuts out every battle
# between two crowns' vassals, which is most of them. What is left is the
# war next door, which is rare on the map and very loud when it happens.
bc_the_man_opposite_holds_the_next_valley_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander }
		character_is_land_realm_neighbor = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_the_man_he_rode_out_for_holds_the_next_valley_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		character_is_land_realm_neighbor = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
}

# BATCH 182 - WHETHER EITHER OF THE TWO WHO MADE HIM IS STILL LIVING.
#
# THE MOD HAS NEVER LOOKED UPWARD. `has_father` and `has_mother` both
# returned zero over the whole tree, `is_child_of` returned zero, and the
# single hit on `is_parent_of` is the word sitting inside batch 180's own
# header at line 7706. The twenty-three hits on the words "father" and
# "mother" are every one of them header prose. Batch 180 counted the people
# who exist because of him. Nothing anywhere has asked after the two people
# he exists because of.
#
# THE DUMP IS UNUSUALLY PLAIN HERE. `has_father` is "does the character have
# a valid living father?" and `has_mother` is the same for the mother, both
# character scope, both yes/no. The word carrying the weight is LIVING. These
# do not ask whether a man is recorded as having had parents, which is true
# of very nearly everybody; they ask whether either of them is still above
# ground this afternoon.
#
# AGOT WRITES IT THIS WAY ITSELF. `common/scripted_effects/00_war_effects.txt`
# guards a father scope with `has_father = yes` at :620 and a mother scope
# with `has_mother = yes` at :635, when a declaration of war has to reach the
# parents of the man it was declared on. Vanilla has the identical pair at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_war_effects.txt:482` and `:497`.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 180 TURNED AROUND. That batch reads `any_child` and
# counts the household a man built. This reads the household he came out of,
# and the two facts come apart constantly: a man of fifty with six children
# buried both his parents twenty years ago, and a childless man of
# twenty-five usually still has both of his.
#
# THE COMMANDER ROW CARRIES `age >= 40` AND THAT FLOOR IS THE WHOLE POINT OF
# IT. Every man alive was somebody's son at twenty and the fact says nothing
# about him. At forty it has turned strange: he has held a seat for years, he
# has men who call him lord, and there is still somebody breathing who
# remembers him being carried. The floor is what turns a universal fact into
# a rare one.
#
# THE KNIGHT ROW TAKES NO FLOOR because the pair of answers does that work
# instead. Father gone and mother still living is an arrangement of a family
# rather than a stage of a life, and it is not where most men are. The young
# mostly have both, the old mostly have neither, and a great many of the
# knights the engine hands this mod are generated men whose parents were
# never written down at all, who answer no to both rows.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The first requires a living father and the second
# requires that there is none. No man can pass both, and no further negation
# is needed beyond the one already standing in the second row.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on a man the
# caller has already entered, and in every other script that uses them they
# ARE the guard - they exist so that nothing has to risk stepping into an
# empty parent scope. Neither of these rows steps anywhere.
bc_his_father_is_alive_to_hear_of_this_trigger = {
	age >= 40
	has_father = yes
}

bc_his_mother_is_the_last_one_left_trigger = {
	has_father = no
	has_mother = yes
}

# BATCH 183 - WHICH OF HIS FIVE IS THE WORST ONE.
#
# `lowest_skill` RETURNED ZERO ACROSS THE WHOLE MOD. Batch 173 asked which of
# a man's five numbers is his largest and stopped there. Nothing anywhere has
# asked the other end of the same list, and the other end is the one that
# decides what happens when a day asks him for something he has never been
# any good at.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 173 TURNED AROUND, BECAUSE IT IS A DIFFERENT KEY WITH
# DIFFERENT ARGUMENTS. That batch reads `highest_skill = diplomacy` and
# `highest_skill_including_prowess = prowess`. These read `lowest_skill` with
# `martial` and `intrigue`. No gate here shares a key and an argument with
