# any gate there, and the populations barely overlap: the best talker in
# Westeros can be worst at war or worst at guile or neither.
#
# A RANKING IS STILL NOT A THRESHOLD. The mod weighs `martial >= 12` in
# dozens of `chance` rows and reads the far commander's `martial` against 16
# and 8 at bc_scripted_triggers.txt:332 and :340. A man with martial 14 can
# have it as his lowest number, because the rest of him is better than that,
# and a man with martial 5 can have intrigue 3 and fail the first gate below.
# Neither question narrows the other.
#
# VANILLA'S OWN FORM. `limit = { lowest_skill = $SKILL$ }` at
# `events/education_and_childhood/chinese_disciple_events.txt:897`, which AGOT
# ships unchanged at `:899` in its own copy of that file, so the trigger takes
# a bare skill keyword as its argument and is live in this build. The dump
# gives it as "Is the skill the lowest skill (excluding Prowess) of the
# character?", character scope. The excluding form is the one used here on
# purpose: prowess is what a man does with his hands and it has no business
# in a ranking about what he is worst at THINKING.
#
# TIES PASS, AND THAT IS WHY THE FIRST GATE CARRIES A NEGATION. The dump says
# it in the entry itself - "True even when there are multiple skills with the
# same lowest value" - so a man whose martial and intrigue sit level at the
# bottom would otherwise pass both rows at once. `NOT = { lowest_skill =
# intrigue }` on the first row removes exactly that man and leaves the pair
# disjoint by construction, which is the trick batch 173 used on its own
# first gate for the same reason.
#
# WHY THESE TWO OF THE FIVE. Martial is the number the day is asking about,
# so a commander who is worst at it is a man being examined on his weakest
# subject in front of everybody. Intrigue is the number about what a man
# keeps back, so a knight who is worst at it is a man with nothing held in
# reserve and no second version of himself to fall back on, which on a field
# that is going well is not a failing at all.
#
# RARITY. Five skills split roughly five ways before ties, and each gate then
# takes only the half of that fifth on the right side of the battle. Batch
# 173 accepted the same arithmetic at the other end of the same list.
#
# NO GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on a man the caller has
# already entered. Every character has five skills whether anybody has ever
# ranked them or not.
bc_the_least_of_him_is_what_today_wants_trigger = {
	lowest_skill = martial
	NOT = { lowest_skill = intrigue }
}

bc_there_is_no_second_thought_in_him_trigger = {
	lowest_skill = intrigue
}

# BATCH 184 - WHICH WAY THE MARRIAGE WAS WRITTEN.
#
# `matrilinear_marriage` AND `matrilinear_betrothal` BOTH RETURNED ZERO OVER
# THE WHOLE TREE, and so did the word itself. Batch 88 opened marriage and
# several batches have worked it since - who the marriage joined, what it
# produced, whether it ended, whether the wife is close family to the man
# commanding the other side. Every one of those reads the arrangement as a
# thing that happened between two houses. None of them has ever read the one
# clause of it that Westeros cares about most, which is whose name the
# children get.
#
# THE DUMP AND AGOT'S OWN USE. `matrilinear_marriage` is "Is the marriage
# with the spouse matrilinear?", character scope, yes/no. AGOT reads it bare
# on a character beside a sex row at `events/mpo_chaotic_kurultai_succession
# .txt:909-910`, which is `is_male = yes` followed by `matrilinear_marriage =
# yes` - the exact form used below - and reads it eight more times from
# inside a spouse iteration in `events/marriage_effect_events.txt`.
#
# THE KEY IS NOT A SECOND `is_married`. Batch 88's header records why a bare
# `is_married = yes` is not a gate: it is nearly every landed man over
# twenty. This is the narrow clause inside that arrangement, and it cannot be
# true of an unmarried man at all, so it carries the marriage requirement
# without spending it.
#
# THE SAME FLAG MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS ABOUT THE TWO PEOPLE IT BINDS, AND
# THAT IS WHY THE SEX ROW IS PART OF THE GATE RATHER THAN DECORATION. For the
# man it means he handed over the thing a Westerosi man is raised to believe
# is not his to hand over: his sons are hers, and whatever he does today is
# written down against a name he was not born to. For the woman it means the
# opposite - the line runs through her, her husband came to her house, and
# the children out here in the world are hers before they are anybody's. One
# clause, two facts, and no way to write either of them without the row that
# says which of the two is standing on this field.
#
# THIS IS NOT bc_the_orders_are_taken. That template gates `is_female = yes`
# and winning and is about a host that has stopped discussing the fact; it
# reads sex and nothing else. This reads a clause in a marriage contract, and
# the sex row is there to say which side of that clause the subject is on. A
# woman who never married, or married the ordinary way, passes that template
# and fails this one.
#
# THE LOSING ROW SITS ON THE MAN AND THE WINNING ROW ON THE WOMAN, WHICH IS
# THE RULE THE HOST BLOCK AT bc_commander_phase_events.txt:4004 ALREADY SET.
# A fact about a woman on a losing field becomes the host's explanation for
# the defeat, and this mod does not write that sentence. A man who married
# into his wife's line and is losing is not that sentence at all; the man
# doing the judging in his case is himself.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. `is_male = yes` against `is_female = yes` on the
# same clause. No negation is needed and none is written.
#
# RARITY. Both halves are thin and that is the point. Matrilinear marriage is
# the arrangement a house accepts when it has no sons and will not let the
# name stop, so it is uncommon everywhere and rarer still among the men who
# end up leading a host. The second gate additionally needs a woman in the
# knight line, which AGOT supplies from the North, the Iron Islands, Dorne
# and beyond the Wall, and not from anywhere else.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both rows are bare character-scope reads on a man or
# a woman the caller has already entered, and an unmarried subject is a clean
# no rather than an error.
bc_his_sons_will_carry_her_name_trigger = {
	is_male = yes
	matrilinear_marriage = yes
}

bc_the_line_runs_through_her_trigger = {
	is_female = yes
	matrilinear_marriage = yes
}

# BATCH 185 - THE PART OF HIM THAT WAS NEVER WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE.
#
# `has_sexuality` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE TREE, and so did
# `is_attracted_to_men`, `is_attracted_to_women`, `is_attracted_to_gender_of`,
# and so did the words "sexual" and "attracted" in any comment in any file.
# A hundred and eighty-four batches have read who a man loves. The lover seam
# sits at `:2735` and `:2742` and asks whether the two commanders are lovers;
# the rivalry, friendship, marriage and bastardy seams ask that same kind of
# question about other pairs of named people. Every one of them reads a bond
# between two people who both exist. Not one of them has ever read the thing
# that decided which bonds were ever going to be available to him at all.
#
# THE DUMP. `has_sexuality` is "Does the character's sexuality match the
# scripted? (heterosexual, homosexual, bisexual, asexual, none)", character
# scope. Children carry `none` until one is rolled for them, which costs
# nothing here, because nobody who has not come of age leads a host or rides
# in a knight line.
#
# LIVE IN BOTH SHIPPED TREES. Vanilla writes the bare form at
# `events/courtier_guest_management_events/courtier_guest_management_events
# .txt:942`, the negated form at `events/activities/tour_activity/
# tour_travel_events.txt:324` and again at `:2116`, and the homosexual row
# directly beside the bisexual one at `events/councillor_task_events/
# councillor_spouse_events/councillor_spouse_diplomacy_events.txt:1326-1327`.
# AGOT reads it at `common/activities/intents/shared_intents.txt:126` and
# `:256`, at `common/character_interactions/00_adoption.txt:59-60`, at
# `00_marriage_interactions.txt:2713`, and at `00_scheme_interactions
# .txt:2000`.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE LOVER SEAM AND CANNOT BE CONFUSED WITH IT.
# `has_relation_lover` asks whether one particular arrangement exists right
# now with one particular person. It can be true of a man of any sexuality
# and it is false of nearly every man on the field on nearly every day. This
# reads what the man is, which stays true of him alone in a tent with nobody
# in it and no arrangement anywhere. A man can pass either one and fail the
# other, in either direction, and neither row implies a single thing about
# the other.
#
# WHAT THE TWO ROWS ARE ACTUALLY FOR. The first is a man who has spent his
# whole life managing the distance between what he is and what the men around
# him assume he is, and who has got very good at reading a room as a direct
# result. The second is a man none of it ever reached, who was never turned
# around by anybody and has therefore never had that particular part of his
# attention spent. Both are private facts and both stay private in the
# writing. The chronicle records a man's bearing and never his bed.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. A character carries exactly one sexuality, so
# `homosexual` and `asexual` cannot both hold of the same man. No negation is
# needed and none is written.
#
# RARITY. Sexuality is rolled at birth off a game rule and on the default
# setting the large majority of characters come out heterosexual. Both gates
# sit in the small remainder that is left over, and the second is the smaller
# half of it. Neither one is a gate that fires for nearly everybody.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on the subject
# the caller has already entered.
bc_the_thing_he_never_said_out_loud_trigger = {
	has_sexuality = homosexual
}

bc_none_of_it_ever_reached_him_trigger = {
	has_sexuality = asexual
}

# BATCH 186 - THE THING THAT IS IN HIM AND HAS NEVER ONCE COME OUT.
#
# `has_inactive_trait` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE TREE. The word "inactive"
# appears twice in the mod and both are prose - the diarchy rarity note at
# `:4012` and the physique list at `:6962`. `depressed` returns zero as a
# word and as a key. Madness was opened long ago at `:1302` with
# bc_his_mind_is_not_his_own_trigger, four rows of `has_trait` across
# `lunatic_1`, `lunatic_genetic`, `possessed_1` and `possessed_genetic`, and
# other batches have read the body he was dealt through
# `num_of_bad_genetic_traits`. Every one of those reads something that is
# true of him where anybody can see it. None of them has ever read the trait
# that is on his sheet and switched off.
#
# THE DUMP. `has_inactive_trait` is "Does the character have this trait or a
# trait of this trait group amongst their inactive traits?", character scope.
# The inactive list is a separate list from the trait list, which is the
# whole reason the engine ships a second trigger for it.
#
# VANILLA PROVES THE TWO LISTS ARE SEPARATE, IN ONE EVENT.
# `events/trait_specific_events/trait_specific_events.txt:343-366` is the
# event that gives a man the madness. Its trigger is `NOT = { has_trait =
# lunatic }` - so the event only runs on a man who does NOT answer yes to the
# trait - and its immediate then reads `if = { limit = { has_inactive_trait =
# lunatic_genetic } make_trait_active = lunatic_genetic } else = { add_trait
# = lunatic_1 }`. A man carrying the dormant gene reaches that `if` and takes
# the first arm. He therefore fails `has_trait` and passes
# `has_inactive_trait` at the same instant, and vanilla is built on that
# being so. The same pattern runs at `:446` for `possessed_genetic`, at
# `:562` for `depressed_genetic`, and at
# `trait_specific_ongoing_events.txt:1834`.
#
# AGOT SHIPS BOTH KEYS UNCHANGED. `depressed_genetic` at
# `common/traits/00_traits.txt:5945` and `lunatic_genetic` at `:6012`, the
# second one under vanilla's own comment calling it schizophrenia.
#
# DISJOINT FROM THE MADNESS GATE, AND NOT BY ACCIDENT. Because an inactive
# trait does not answer `has_trait`, a man carrying only the dormant gene
# fails all four rows of bc_his_mind_is_not_his_own_trigger. The two gates
# cannot both be true of the same man on the same afternoon, so neither
# template can ever be a subset of the other, and the two never write about
# the same subject. The same reasoning covers
# bc_his_body_was_dealt_to_him_trigger: a switched-off trait is not applying
# its modifiers to anybody, which is what switched off means, so a man whose
# only bad genetic trait is the sleeping one reads a plain zero there.
#
# THAT IS ALSO THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE PAIR. These are men who have been
# waiting their whole lives for a thing that has not happened. Nobody has
# ever seen it in either of them, nothing has ever tested as anything, and
# both of them know exactly which of their kin it took and roughly what age
# it took them at. The first has spent forty years listening to himself for
# it. The second stopped listening a long time ago and got on with the day.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION BETWEEN THE TWO ROWS AS WELL. The second gate
# negates the first's key outright, so a man carrying both sleeping traits
# goes to the first row and only the first row.
#
# RARITY. Both are thin and neither pretends otherwise. A dormant bad gene
# has to be inherited and then not triggered, and the men it runs in are a
# small and specific set of houses. This is the opposite of a gate that fires
# for nearly everybody; it is a gate that waits.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are bare character-scope reads on the subject
# the caller has already entered, and a man with an empty inactive list is a
# clean no rather than an error.
bc_the_thing_he_was_born_carrying_trigger = {
	has_inactive_trait = lunatic_genetic
}

bc_it_never_woke_in_him_trigger = {
	has_inactive_trait = depressed_genetic
	NOT = { has_inactive_trait = lunatic_genetic }
}

# BATCH 187 - HOW HOT HIS FAITH IS RUNNING THIS YEAR.
#
# `fervor` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE TREE, as a key and as a word. The mod
# has read faith a great deal and always in one of two ways: what his faith
# calls a sin or a virtue, which is the doctrine seam at `:6730` and at
# `:1946`, and whose ground he is standing on, which is
# bc_knight_the_ground_under_him_belongs_to_the_faith at
# `bc_knight_phase_events.txt:11255`. Both of those ask what the faith SAYS.
# Neither of them, and nothing else in a hundred and eighty-six batches, has
# ever asked how loud it is saying it this year.
#
# THE DUMP. `fervor` is "What is the faith's fervor?", faith scope, with the
# full set of comparators. It is a number the whole faith carries and it
# moves - wars, temples, heresies and clergy all push it about - so the same
# man on the same field can pass this gate one decade and fail it the next.
#
# THE RUNGS ARE VANILLA'S OWN, NOT INVENTED HERE. Vanilla's hunt pulse walks
# the ladder at `common/activities/pulse_actions/hunt_pulse_actions.txt:1161`,
# `:1165` and `:1169` in the order 75, 50, 25, which is the game telling us
# where it thinks high and low are. The block form used below is vanilla's
# too: `common/character_interactions/00_religious_interactions.txt:2114-2128`
# writes `scope:actor.faith = { fervor < 50 }` and `scope:recipient.faith = {
# fervor >= 50 }` as adjacent rows, and `:2124` writes `fervor <= 25`.
#
# AGOT KEEPS IT LIVE AND MOVES IT. `common/buildings/
# 00_agot_monastery_buildings.txt:227-229` calls `change_fervor` off a temple
# holding, so this is not a vanilla number sitting frozen under the mod; it
# is a dial AGOT's own content turns.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE DOCTRINE SEAM AND CANNOT BE FOLDED INTO IT. A doctrine gate
# asks a fixed question about the rules he lives under and changes its answer
# only when he changes faith. This asks a number that changes without him
# doing anything at all, and it is the same number for every man who shares
# his faith, on both sides of the field. A man whose faith counts half his
# habits as sins can be in a year when nobody is listening, and a man whose
# faith forbids him almost nothing can be in a year when everybody is.
#
# NOT A SUBSET OF THE HOLY-GROUND TEMPLATE EITHER. That one reads the county
# under his feet and is a fact about a place. This is a fact about a
# congregation four hundred miles away.
#
# WHAT THE TWO ROWS ARE FOR. The first man is losing in a year when the
# faith is at full voice, which means the afternoon is already spoken for -
# whatever happens here will be read out as a judgement by people who were
# not present, and he knows it while it is still happening. The second man is
# winning in a year when hardly anybody is praying about anything, so nothing
# is coming down from above to hold the line, and what holds it is the man on
# either side of him and nothing else.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. One scalar, one faith, and 75 against 25. The two
# gates cannot both be true. No negation is needed and none is written.
#
# RARITY. Fervor spends most of its life in the middle of the range and the
# two ends are where the engine itself marks high and low. Neither of these
# is a gate that fires for nearly everybody, and both of them will be true of
# whole decades at a time rather than of scattered individuals, which is the
# right shape for a fact about a faith rather than about a man.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Every character has a faith, and the read sits
# inside the faith block the way the culture gates at `:1059`, `:1471` and
# `:3645` sit inside theirs.
bc_the_faith_is_running_hot_trigger = {
	faith = { fervor >= 75 }
}

bc_nobody_much_is_praying_this_year_trigger = {
	faith = { fervor <= 25 }
}


# BATCH 188 - WHAT KIND OF COMPANY IS ACTUALLY STANDING ON THIS FIELD.
#
# `has_maa_of_type` RETURNED ZERO OVER THE WHOLE MOD, AND SO DID `onager`,
# `mangonel`, `trebuchet` AND `house_guard`. Batch 129 read the men-at-arms
# system once, with `maa_regiments_count`, and its own header admitted what it
# was doing: it counted companies because counting was the answerable
# question. How many is not what kind. A hundred and eighty-seven batches have
# measured the size of a host, the ground under it, the weather on it, the war
# around it and the man giving the orders, and not one of them has ever asked
# what is actually parked in the line.
#
# THE DUMP PUTS THIS ON THE SIDE AND NOT ON THE MAN. "Does this combat side
# have at least one MaA of the given regiment type?", combat_side scope. That
# is the useful half of it: the side pools everything every army standing on
# it brought, allies and summoned vassals included, rather than only the
# companies the commander pays for out of his own books.
#
# AGOT USES THIS TRIGGER IN A FILE OF EXACTLY THIS KIND. `has_maa_of_type =
# scorpions` and `has_maa_of_type = water_wizards` sit inside
# `common/combat_phase_events/00_commander_phase_events.txt:613` and `:623`,
# both of them as the `limit` of an `if` inside a `prev.enemy_side` block; the
# same read appears at `common/on_action/combat_on_actions.txt:757` and twice
# in `00_agot_dragon_scripted_modifiers.txt` at `:459` and `:473`. Vanilla's
# own use is `events/war_events/combat_events.txt:2037`. The four type keys
# are AGOT's own, from `common/men_at_arms_types/00_maa_types.txt`: `onager`
# at `:727`, `mangonel` at `:925`, `trebuchet` at `:1119` and `house_guard`
# at `:1249`.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 129 AND IS NOT A SUBSET OF IT. `maa_regiments_count >= 4`
# and `= 0` are counts on one man's personal books; these are types present
# anywhere on a side that may be six lords deep. A commander with nothing
# professional of his own can stand beside engines somebody else hauled up
# the road, and a man with four companies bought and paid for may have
# brought none of these four types to this particular field.
#
# ENGINES ON ONE SIDE, THE HOUSE COMPANY ON THE OTHER, AND THE PAIR IS
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The first passes when the side has an onager, a
# mangonel or a trebuchet standing in it. The second asks for the house guard
# and then denies all three engines, so no side can answer yes to both, and
# the two are read on different men in different pools besides.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. `scope:combat_side` is saved before either gate is
# asked and is read bare elsewhere in this file - `bc_field_is_bloody_trigger`
# at `:191` does `scope:combat_side = { percent_enemies_killed >= 40 }` with
# nothing in front of it. Only the walks that can end nowhere, `combat_war`
# and the far commander, take the `exists` and the repeated `trigger_if`.
#
# RARITY. Siege weapons are bought to open walls and are the last thing a man
# wants in a column that has to turn and fight in the open, so a side with one
# in it is a side that was on its way somewhere else. `house_guard` requires
# `warfare_legacy_5` on somebody's dynasty (`00_maa_types.txt:1249`), which is
# five completed rungs of one legacy - a handful of houses late in a long
# game and almost none at any bookmark. Neither of these is most fields.
bc_there_are_engines_on_this_field_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		OR = {
			has_maa_of_type = onager
			has_maa_of_type = mangonel
			has_maa_of_type = trebuchet
		}
	}
}

bc_the_house_guard_is_standing_here_trigger = {
	scope:combat_side = {
		has_maa_of_type = house_guard
		NOR = {
			has_maa_of_type = onager
			has_maa_of_type = mangonel
			has_maa_of_type = trebuchet
		}
	}
}


# BATCH 189 - THE THING HE IS OWED AND HAS NOT GOT.
#
# THE MOD READS ARTIFACTS EXACTLY ONCE AND ONLY AS POSSESSION.
# `bc_he_carries_valyrian_steel_trigger` at `:1785` walks
# `any_equipped_character_artifact` and asks AGOT's own
# `agot_is_valyrian_steel_artifact` of what is on the man's belt. That is a
# question about what he HAS. `has_any_artifact_claim`,
# `has_outstanding_artifact_claims`, `any_personal_claimed_artifact` and
# `any_house_claimed_artifact` were all at zero occurrences over the whole
# mod, and those are the questions about what he is OWED. In a world whose
# swords have names and outlive the men who carry them, the mod has never once
# looked at the empty scabbard.
#
# THE TWO KEYS ARE VANILLA'S AND AGOT SHIPS BOTH LIVE. `has_any_artifact_claim
# = yes` is the first line of the artifact casus belli's
# `allowed_for_character` block at `common/casus_belli_types/
# 01_ep1_wars.txt:8`, in AGOT's copy as well as vanilla's.
# `has_outstanding_artifact_claims = yes` gates four separate artifact
# interactions at `common/character_interactions/00_artifact_interactions.txt`
# `:1661`, `:2505`, `:3386` and `:4515`, and AGOT puts it on its OWN Valyrian
# steel demand at `common/character_interactions/
# 00_agot_valyrian_steel_interactions.txt:328`. That last one is the whole
# argument for this batch in one line: AGOT built a bespoke interaction around
# the idea that a man is owed a blade somebody else is wearing.
#
# THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO KEYS IS THE BATCH. The dump: the first is
# "any artifact claims at all", the second is "any artifact claims that can be
# pressed". A claim that cannot be pressed is a claim on something whose
# holder is out of reach, or beyond a border, or nobody the engine will let
# him move against. So the first gate is a man who could go and take the thing
# back and has not, and the second is a man who is owed something and has no
# road to it at all.
#
# THE CHEAP KEY IS WRITTEN FIRST ON BOTH GATES AND THAT ORDER IS DELIBERATE.
# The dump marks `has_any_artifact_claim` CHEAP and
# `has_outstanding_artifact_claims` EXPENSIVE, and these are combat phase
# gates, asked of every candidate on every phase of every battle. Putting the
# cheap key first means the expensive one is only ever reached for the small
# number of men who hold a claim at all; for everybody else the row fails on
# the first line. Do not reorder them.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Both require a claim; the first requires that at
# least one of them can be pressed and the second denies it. No character can
# answer yes to both, and no negation of a whole gate is needed to say so.
#
# NEITHER TAKES A GUARD. Both are plain yes/no reads on the scoped character
# with no scope walk in either, the same as `has_father` and `has_mother` in
# batch 180.
#
# RARITY. Artifact claims are made by inheritance disputes, by theft, by a
# holding changing hands with the contents still in it, and by AGOT's own
# Valyrian steel machinery. They are real and they are not common, and the
# split between pressable and not splits an already small set in two.
bc_there_is_something_of_his_in_another_mans_hands_trigger = {
	has_any_artifact_claim = yes
	has_outstanding_artifact_claims = yes
}

bc_what_he_is_owed_is_out_of_reach_trigger = {
	has_any_artifact_claim = yes
	NOT = { has_outstanding_artifact_claims = yes }
}

# BATCH 190 - WHAT THE HOST UNDER HIM IS RUNNING ON.
#
# CENSUS. `army_supply` appears three times in this mod and `supplies` twice,
# and every one of the five is an ordinary English word inside a comment - a
# toast that "supplies" a fact, the "ordinary supply of fear", a rung
# "starving" the one above it. `commanding_army` appears exactly once, in
# batch 149's header, noting that `is_commanding_army` returned zero.
# `is_army_in_raid`, `forage`, `provision` and `victual` return nothing at all.
# This mod has counted the host's men, its regiments, its rank, its ground and
# its weather, and has never once asked what it is eating or why it came.
#
# BOTH KEYS ARE LIVE SCRIPT, NOT DUMP ENTRIES. AGOT writes
# `scope:army = { army_supply <= 25 }` at
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_army_events.txt:708` and again at `:4112`,
# and `army_supply <= 50` at `:700`. `is_army_in_raid` is in AGOT's own
# scripted triggers at `common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_lmf_triggers.txt:198`,
# and it is read off the two armies of a battle at
# `events/travel_events/travel_danger_events_joe.txt:921` and `:922`, which is
# the exact situation these two gates sit in. The walk is precedented in
# combat as well: `side_primary_participant.commanding_army = { ... }` is
# written inside a combat event at `events/war_events/combat_events.txt:2545`,
# and AGOT saves `commanding_army` off a character at
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_army_events.txt:178`.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 149. That batch read two numbers about a man's LANDS -
# what he could raise and what he has raised - and both would read the same
# in his own hall with no war on at all. These two are facts about the HOST
# STANDING HERE: its larder and its errand. A great lord with everything
# raised can be starving in the ninth week of a march, and a small one can
# still be carrying full sacks on the second day. Neither number is anywhere
# inside the other.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The first wants supply at or under a quarter; the
# second names a raid AND requires supply above a quarter, so no army alive
# can satisfy both, and neither gate is a subset of the other. Batch 129
# counted what one man keeps on his own books and batch 188 asked what kind of
# company is standing on the field; neither of them touches the army's stores
# or the reason it marched.
#
# GUARDS. This is a two-step walk that can end nowhere twice over -
# `side_commander` is optional, and a commander need not be leading an army at
# all - so both gates carry the full `exists` on the whole chain as a sibling
# and repeat it inside the `trigger_if` limit before any read happens.
#
# RARITY. The first wants a column that has burned three quarters of its
# stores, which happens to armies deep in hostile ground late in a campaign
# and to nobody at home, and then wants that man leading and losing. The
# second wants a host that came for plunder rather than for a war and got
# caught at it - ironborn ashore, wildlings south of the Wall, a neighbour's
# outriders run down among the fields - which is a small fraction of the
# battles fought in Westeros and none at all of the ones fought over a throne.
bc_the_column_under_him_is_starving_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander.commanding_army
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander.commanding_army
		}
		scope:combat_side.side_commander.commanding_army = {
			army_supply <= 25
		}
	}
}

bc_this_host_came_here_to_plunder_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander.commanding_army
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander.commanding_army
		}
		scope:combat_side.side_commander.commanding_army = {
			is_army_in_raid = yes
			army_supply > 25
		}
	}
}


# BATCH 191 - THE OTHER WAR, THE ONE THAT IS NOT THIS ONE.
#
# CENSUS. `is_in_civil_war` returns zero across the whole mod. The word "civil
# war" appears six times and every one of those is a comment about
# `is_civil_war`, which is a different key on a different scope: it is read on
# the war behind this combat by bc_war_is_a_rebellion_trigger at :964 and asks
# whether THIS quarrel is a rising. `is_a_faction_member` and
# `has_targeting_faction` at :2785 ask who is conspiring against whom, which
# is a state of intent rather than a state of war. Nothing in the mod has ever
# asked whether the man on this field has a second war going on somewhere else.
#
# THE KEY IS LIVE IN BOTH TREES. `is_in_civil_war = no` is written in vanilla
# at `common/activities/activity_types/tour.txt:38` and `:45`, at
# `common/character_interactions/06_ep3_interactions.txt:1845`, `:1849`,
# `:7538` and `:7541`, and at
# `common/character_interactions/06_ep3_scheme_interactions.txt:2187` and
# `:2213`; AGOT ships the same lines at `tour.txt:45` and `:52`,
# `06_ep3_interactions.txt:1909` and `:1913`, and
# `06_ep3_scheme_interactions.txt:2266` and `:2292`. Character scope, plain
# yes/no, no target.
#
# BOTH GATES DENY THAT THIS WAR IS THE CIVIL WAR, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT.
# `is_in_civil_war` on a man fighting in a rising would be true because of the
# rising he is standing in, which would make it a strict superset of
# bc_war_is_a_rebellion_trigger and worth nothing. Both gates therefore carry
# the negation of `is_civil_war` on the war behind this combat, so what is left
# is exactly the case that gate cannot see: an ordinary war between realms,
# fought by a man who also has his own people in arms behind him.
#
# TWO DIFFERENT MEN, WHICH IS BATCH 149'S ARRANGEMENT. The first reads root,
# who in the commander pool is the man the template is about. The second reads
# the far side's commander, so it says something about the enemy rather than
# about the man asking. Neither can be a subset of the other and the two pools
# roll separately in any case.
#
# GUARDS. `combat_war` is optional and `enemy_side.side_commander` is optional,
# so every read of either is nested in a `trigger_if` whose limit repeats the
# `exists`, with the `exists` also standing as a sibling so that a missing
# scope fails the gate instead of passing it vacuously. `is_in_civil_war` on
# root is a bare character-scope test and needs no guard, and it is written
# first in the first gate because it is the cheapest line in the block.
#
# RARITY. Both want a man carrying two wars at once, which is a specific and
# unhappy condition: most lords fighting a neighbour are not simultaneously
# being defied at home, and most men being defied at home have the sense not to
# march out against anybody else while it lasts. The second wants that to be
# true of the far commander while this side is winning, which is the shape of
# a realm losing a battle it should have won.
bc_he_has_another_rising_behind_him_trigger = {
	is_in_civil_war = yes
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war }
		scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war = {
			NOT = { is_civil_war = yes }
		}
	}
}

bc_the_far_lord_has_a_rising_behind_him_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			is_in_civil_war = yes
		}
	}
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war }
		scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war = {
			NOT = { is_civil_war = yes }
		}
	}
}


# BATCH 192 - THE PLOT THAT IS ALREADY RUNNING WHILE THIS IS GOING ON.
#
# CENSUS. `is_scheming_against` returns zero across the whole mod. The mod does
# read schemes once - the murder batch at :3848 and :3854 uses `any_scheme` and
# `any_targeting_scheme` with `scheme_type = murder` - and that batch asks
# whether a man has a death running SOMEWHERE, or has one running against him
# from SOMEWHERE. Neither of them has ever asked whether the somewhere is on
# this field.
#
# NEITHER GATE CONTAINS THE MURDER PAIR AND NEITHER IS CONTAINED BY IT, and
# both counterexamples are easy. A murder scheme aimed at a man three hundred
# leagues from here answers yes to bc_he_has_a_murder_running_somewhere and no
# to the first gate below. A seduction aimed squarely at the man across the
# grass answers yes to the first gate below and no to the murder gate. The two
# pairs overlap heavily in practice and contain each other nowhere, which is
# the test.
#
# THE KEY IS LIVE IN BOTH TREES AND THE BLOCK FORM IS THEIRS. Vanilla writes
# `is_scheming_against = { type = murder target = scope:target }` at
# `common/activities/intents/shared_intents.txt:58` and
# `is_scheming_against = { target = $VICTIM$ type = murder }` at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_murder_effects.txt:385` and `:484`; AGOT ships
# both of those files with the same lines, and uses the key again in its own
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_hunt_triggers.txt:879`. The dump documents three
# forms and the one used below is the third - `{ target = X }` on its own,
# "considers all schemes".
#
# ALL SCHEMES AND NOT MURDER ONLY, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE CHOICE FROM THE MURDER
# BATCH AND IS MADE FOR THE SAME REASON. There, restricting to murder was what
# made a gate out of `any_scheme`, which on its own fires for a large fraction
# of the AI. Here the target does that work instead: naming one specific man
# who is standing on this field today is narrow enough on its own, and adding
# `type = murder` on top would make the first gate a strict subset of the
# shipped one, which is exactly what may not happen.
#
# TWO DIFFERENT MEN, WHICH IS BATCH 149'S ARRANGEMENT. The first asks whether
# root is working against the far commander; the second asks whether root is
# working against the man he is taking orders from. A commander is his own side
# commander, so the second gate on the commander pool would be a man scheming
# against himself and would never fire; it is a knight row by construction, the
# way batch 149's second gate is.
#
# GUARDS. Both walks are optional and naming an optional link as the TARGET of
# a trigger is a read, which is the rule the dread batch wrote down. So each
# gate carries the `exists` as a sibling and repeats it inside a `trigger_if`
# limit. Neither gate changes scope: `is_scheming_against` is asked of root and
# only its target is walked.
#
# RARITY. A scheme is a rare thing to have running on any given day, it has to
# be running against this one man out of everybody the schemer has ever met,
# and then he has to be standing in a battle with him. The second is rarer
# still: it wants a man in the line who is working against the man he is
# fighting for, and who is nonetheless having a good afternoon.
bc_he_is_working_against_the_man_across_from_him_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		is_scheming_against = {
			target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

bc_he_is_working_against_the_man_he_follows_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		is_scheming_against = {
			target = scope:combat_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 193 - THE SADDLES NOBODY IS SITTING IN.
#
# `number_of_knights` and `max_number_of_knights` both returned zero across the
# whole mod, as did `number_of_powerful_vassals`, `number_of_election_votes`
# and `levies_to_liege`. Batch 142 counted a household once, with `any_knight =
# { count >= 8 }`, and that is the only roster this mod has ever counted. It
# has never asked how many swords a man is ENTITLED to seat - a number the
# engine derives from his lands rather than from his choices - nor whether his
# two numbers agree.
#
# BOTH KEYS ARE LIVE IN BOTH TREES, AND SO IS THE PAIRING. Vanilla compares the
# two at `common/character_interactions/00_courtier_and_guest_interactions.txt`
# line 296, at `common/decisions/tutorial_decisions.txt:1172` and at
# `events/councillor_task_events/marshal_task_events.txt:102`, and the other
# way round at `common/important_actions/00_war_actions.txt:246`. AGOT ships
# its own copies at `00_courtier_and_guest_interactions.txt:325`, at
# `marshal_task_events.txt:102` and at three places in
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_army_events.txt` - `:644`, `:687` and
# `:3643`. Bare thresholds are live too: `number_of_knights > 6` sits in both
# trees at `common/character_interactions/06_ep3_laamp_interactions.txt`.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 142'S HOUSEHOLD. That gate asks whether a man keeps eight
# sworn swords. These ask whether the swords he keeps come to half or less of
# what his lands would let him keep. Three or fewer is disjoint from eight or
# more by arithmetic, so neither of these can be a subset or a superset of it,
# and neither of them can be stated at all without the entitlement number,
# which batch 142 never touches.
#
# TWO MEN, ONE FACT, AS IN BATCH 191. The first asks it of the man giving the
# orders on this side; the second asks it of the man giving them on the far
# side. Neither implies the other in either direction, the two pools roll
# separately, and a field where both hold is a field where two short-handed
# lords have found each other, which is a perfectly ordinary afternoon and not
# a contradiction.
#
# ONE BARE PAIR, ONE GUARDED WALK. The first reads two scalars off the scoped
# character and takes no guard, the way every bare character-scope read in this
# file does. The second walks to the far side's commander, which can end
# nowhere, so it carries both `exists` lines as siblings and repeats them
# inside the `trigger_if` limit before either number is read.
#
# RARITY. A man must be large enough to seat six and be riding with three or
# fewer, which is the exact shortfall vanilla itself thinks worth an important
# action notice. It is the condition of a house that has just buried its
# household, or of an heir who has not got round to hiring one. It is not the
# condition of a lord who has been in charge for ten years.
bc_half_his_saddles_came_empty_trigger = {
	number_of_knights <= 3
	max_number_of_knights >= 6
}

bc_the_far_lord_rode_out_short_handed_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander = {
			number_of_knights <= 3
			max_number_of_knights >= 6
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 194 - THE TRICK THE OTHER MAN HAS AND HE HAS NOT.
#
# `character_has_commander_trait_scope_does_not` returned zero across the whole
# mod. The mod knows perfectly well that the commander traits are a separate
# short list - `bc_reputation_effects.txt` hands them out at `:486` and `:509`
# behind `number_of_commander_traits < 3`, and two headers in this file already
# quote `category = commander` and `flag = commander_trait_flag` - and it has
# never once asked, as a gate, which of the men on a field is carrying one that
# another is not.
#
# THIS IS NOT BATCH 177'S COMPARISON. That batch counts PERSONALITY traits,
# three in common or three opposing, and personality is what a man is like.
# These read the commander list, which is not what a man is like but what he
# has been taught to do with a line of men. The two lists do not intersect: no
# trait carries both `category = personality` and `category = commander`, so
# neither of these gates can be a subset or a superset of either of batch
# 177's, and a man may pass one pair and fail the other in any combination.
#
# NOR IS IT `bc_foe_is_formidable`. That gate reads the far commander's martial
# at 16, a number. A commander trait is not a number and is not implied by one:
# the engine hands these out through the warfare lifestyle, the marshal's task
# and a small set of events, so a middling man may carry two and a man with
# eighteen martial may carry none.
#
# THE DIRECTION MATTERS AND IT IS EASY TO GET BACKWARDS. The key reads "does
# the CHARACTER named on the right have a commander trait the SCOPE does not".
# Vanilla writes it in the pupil's scope with the teacher on the right at
# `events/councillor_task_events/marshal_task_events.txt:225` and `:1851` and
# in `common/scripted_effects/00_education_effects.txt:3016` with
# `scope:educator` on the right; the warfare lifestyle writes it with `prev` at
# `events/lifestyles/warfare_lifestyle/learn_commander_trait_events.txt:567`,
# `:578`, `:610` and `:635`, and `events/relations_events/vassal_events.txt`
# line 2640 writes `root = { character_has_commander_trait_scope_does_not =
# prev }`. AGOT ships its own copies of all five files. In every one of them
# the man on the right is the man who knows the thing.
#
# TWO MEN LOOKING THE OTHER WAY FROM EACH OTHER. The first is read in the
# commander's own scope and puts the far commander on the right, so it asks
# whether the man opposite was taught something he was not. The second walks to
# this side's own commander and puts the scoped knight on the right, so it asks
# whether the man in the line was taught something the man giving the orders
# was not. Neither implies the other, they concern different pairs of men, and
# the two pools roll separately in any case.
#
# THE SECOND USES `root` ON PURPOSE, LIKE bc_side_has_kin_knight_trigger. Once
# the walk has moved the scope to the commander, the knight the template is
# rolling for is only reachable as `root` - `prev` and `this` would both be
# wrong here, and vanilla's own `vassal_events.txt:2640` writes exactly this
# shape.
#
# GUARDS. Naming a link as the TARGET of a trigger is a read. The first names
# the far side's commander and carries both `exists` lines as siblings and
# again inside the `trigger_if` limit; the second names this side's commander
# and carries its one.
#
# RARITY. The list is short and most men never earn a word from it, so the
# first needs a far commander who has been taught something at all and then
# needs this man not to have been taught the same thing, and the second needs a
# knight who has been taught something his own commander has not - which is the
# rarer of the two, because the man handed the army is usually the man who has
# been at this longest.
bc_the_man_opposite_has_learned_something_he_has_not_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		character_has_commander_trait_scope_does_not = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_he_has_learned_something_the_man_leading_him_has_not_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			character_has_commander_trait_scope_does_not = root
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 195 - THE NAME OTHER PEOPLE ALREADY USE FOR HIM.
#
# `has_any_nickname` and `has_bad_nickname` both returned zero across the whole
# mod. This is the strangest hole yet found. `bc_reward_effects.txt` hands out
# forty-odd nicknames at `:166` onwards - the warrior, greysteel, fireball,
# breakspear, shieldbreaker, widowmaker - so the mod WRITES names onto men for
# what they do in these battles and has never once READ one back. A chronicle
# that gives a man a name and then never notices he has it is only half a
# chronicle.
#
# BOTH KEYS ARE LIVE IN BOTH TREES. Vanilla writes `has_bad_nickname = yes` at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_nickname_effects.txt:5` and `:10`, at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_decisions_effects.txt:1223`, at
# `00_major_decisions_scripted_effects.txt:3581` and at
# `common/casus_belli_types/00_event_war.txt:3288`, and `has_any_nickname` at
# `00_nickname_effects.txt:41`, `00_prison_effects.txt:916` and
# `00_event_war.txt:3289`. AGOT ships its own live copies of the first at
# `00_nickname_effects.txt:5` and `:10`, at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_religious_interaction_effects.txt:1525`, at
# `events/agot_events/agot_targaryen_invasion_events.txt:1478` and `:1491` and
# at `events/activities/tour_activity/tour_travel_events.txt:257`, and of the
# second at `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_game_start.txt:258` and
# `:264` and at six places in `00_agot_character_data_effects.txt`.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE DENIAL IS LOAD-BEARING. A bad nickname is a
# nickname, so `has_any_nickname = yes` on its own would be a strict SUPERSET
# of the first gate and the pair would be nested rather than paired. The second
# therefore denies the first outright, which makes the two impossible to
# satisfy at once on any character and turns them into what they are meant to
# be: a man people have named for something ugly, and a man people have named
# for something else.
#
# NOT A COUNT AND NOT A REPUTATION NUMBER.
# `bc_the_name_opposite_outweighs_the_name_here` reads dynasty renown, which is
# what a house is worth. A nickname is what one man is called, it attaches to
# nobody else alive, and it survives the house losing everything. Neither gate
# here reads renown and the renown gate reads no name.
#
# THE FEEDBACK IS DELIBERATE. The forty names in `bc_reward_effects.txt` are
# all AGOT's approving ones, so the mod's own rewards can only ever make the
# SECOND gate true. A man this chronicle named ten battles ago can now be
# gated on carrying that name, and nothing the mod does will ever push a man
# into the first gate - that one is the game's own verdict, arrived at without
# us.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are bare `yes/no` reads on the scoped character, which is the
# one shape in this file that has never needed one.
#
# RARITY. Most men in this setting die unnamed. Of the minority who are called
# anything at all, the ugly names are a small slice, so the first is rare on
# any field and the second wants a man who has already done something somebody
# thought worth a word.
bc_he_rides_under_an_ugly_name_trigger = {
	has_bad_nickname = yes
}

bc_he_has_a_name_men_say_kindly_trigger = {
	has_any_nickname = yes
	NOT = { has_bad_nickname = yes }
}

# BATCH 196 - THE WORD TWO FAITHS BOTH USE ABOUT THE SAME THING.
#
# `has_same_sinful_trait` and `has_same_virtue_trait` both returned zero across
# the whole mod. What the mod has is `num_sinful_traits >= 2` and
# `num_virtuous_traits >= 2`, one man counted against his own faith, shipped
# with the header at `:1648`. That is a verdict on one man by one doctrine.
# These are something the engine will do that no count can: take TWO men, find
# a word they both carry, and ask whether the two doctrines that judge them -
# which need not be the same doctrine - happen to agree about that word.
#
# BOTH ARE LIVE IN BOTH TREES. Vanilla writes `has_same_sinful_trait = root`
# and `has_same_virtue_trait = root` side by side at
# `events/activities/tournaments/james_tournament_events.txt:170-171` and
# `:231-232`, and the sinful form at eight places in
# `events/yearly_events/bp1_yearly_james.txt` from `:805` onwards. AGOT carries
# its own live copy of the yearly file with the same lines at `:805`, `:813`,
# `:820`, `:841`, `:853`, `:867`, `:877` and `:889`. The dump gives the shape
# as `scope:character_1 = { has_same_sinful_trait = scope:character_2 }`.
#
# NOT THE SHIPPED COUNTS. A man with two sins by his own faith may share none
# of them with the man opposite, and two men may share one sinful word while
# neither of them carries a second, so neither of these gates can be a subset
# or a superset of `bc_his_own_faith_counts_against_him_trigger` or of
# `bc_his_own_faith_counts_for_him_trigger`.
#
# NOT BATCH 177 EITHER. That batch counts three personality traits in common
# without caring what anybody thinks of them. These need ONE trait in common
# and then need two separate doctrines to have independently condemned or
# praised it. Three shared harmless words pass 177 and fail these; one shared
# sinful word passes these and fails 177.
#
# NOT A SHARED-FAITH GATE. `bc_foe_is_heathen` asks whether the two men pray to
# different things. These do not care: the point of "by both of their
# respective faiths" is that two different doctrines can arrive at the same
# verdict about the same man, and often do, because the list of things a
# religion is prepared to call a fault is short and human.
#
# TWO MEN, TWO WORDS, NO NESTING. The first reads the man across the field and
# asks what they are damned for together. The second reads the man giving the
# orders on this side and asks what they are praised for together. Both may be
# true of one afternoon without either implying the other - a knight can share
# a virtue with his own commander while his commander shares a vice with the
# far one - and the pools roll separately in any case.
#
# GUARDS. Naming a link as the TARGET of a trigger is a read, so the first
# carries both far-side `exists` lines as siblings and again inside the
# `trigger_if` limit, and the second carries its one.
#
# RARITY. Two men of three or four words each must share one, and the two
# doctrines judging them must both have an opinion about that word and the same
# opinion. Most pairs of men on most fields share nothing that either religion
# has bothered to write down.
bc_the_same_word_damns_them_both_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		has_same_sinful_trait = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_the_same_word_speaks_well_of_them_both_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		has_same_virtue_trait = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
}

# BATCH 197 - THE LIST OF MEN HE HAS ALREADY PUT IN THE GROUND.
#
# `any_killed_character` RETURNED ZERO ACROSS THE WHOLE MOD. Two gates here
# are about killing and both of them read a trait rather than a list:
# `bc_he_killed_his_own_blood_trigger` at `:1950` is the three `kinslayer`
# rungs, and `bc_it_is_common_knowledge_that_he_killed_somebody_trigger` at
# `:6937` is `has_trait = murderer`. Neither is a count. A trait is what the
# world decided to call a man afterwards; this key is the list itself, which
# the game keeps whether or not anybody ever named him for it.
#
# NEITHER SHIPPED GATE NESTS WITH THESE IN EITHER DIRECTION. `murderer` is
# one secret that came out, so a man may carry it with a single name on his
# list and fail a count of five; a lord who has held five executions has five
# names and no `murderer` trait at all, because an execution is not a murder.
# `kinslayer` is narrower again - it asks who the dead man was and never how
# many there were.
#
# LIVE IN BOTH TREES, AND AGOT PICKS THE THRESHOLD ITSELF. Vanilla writes the
# bare count form at `common/modifiers/00_yearly_event_modifiers.txt:275`, and
# AGOT ships its own copy of that file with three bands - `count >= 15` at
# `:279`, `count >= 10` at `:285` and `count >= 5` at `:291` - so five is
# AGOT's own lowest rung and not a figure invented here. AGOT writes the
# iterator with an inner test in files of its own making as well: three times
# in `common/casus_belli_types/00_agot_rv_rescue_wars.txt` from `:648`, once
# at `00_agot_rv_revenge_wars.txt:362`, once at
# `zz_agot_rv_war_interactions.txt:300` and once at
# `common/scripted_effects/00_nickname_effects.txt:401`.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. The first needs five names on the
# scoped man. The second needs none at all on him and puts the five on the man
# giving him orders instead. The `NOT` in the second gate is load-bearing and
# is doing the whole work of keeping the pair apart, in the way batch 195's
# `NOT = { has_bad_nickname = yes }` does.
#
# TWO DIFFERENT FACTS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT MEN, as in batch 191. The second
# gate is a knight-side row by nature: batch 141's lesson is that a gate
# comparing the scoped man to his own side's commander can never fire in the
# commander pool, because there the scoped man IS that commander. It is
# written into the knight file only.
#
# GUARDS. Naming the own-side commander as the subject of a block is a read,
# so the second gate carries one `exists` as a sibling and repeats it inside
# the `trigger_if` limit. The count on the scoped man himself is a bare
# character-scope read and takes no guard, and `scope:combat_side` never takes
# one.
#
# RARITY. Five dead men is a career rather than an incident. Most lords on
# most fields have killed nobody the game is counting, which is exactly why
# the second gate's `NOT` costs almost nothing while the half above it costs
# a great deal.
bc_there_are_five_names_on_him_already_trigger = {
	any_killed_character = { count >= 5 }
}

bc_he_follows_a_man_with_five_names_on_him_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	NOT = { any_killed_character = { count >= 1 } }
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		scope:combat_side.side_commander = {
			any_killed_character = { count >= 5 }
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 198 - THE BOYS WHO CARRY HIS SHIELD.
#
# `num_of_relation_agot_squire` RETURNED ZERO ACROSS THE WHOLE MOD, AND SO DID
# `has_relation_agot_squire`. The mod has asked the squire question exactly
# once and from the other end: `bc_knight_the_squires_best_day` in the knight
# file gates on `has_trait = squire`, which is a fact about a boy who has not
# been knighted yet. These two gates are about the man who HAS one, and in
# AGOT that is a different person by definition - the trait sits on the pupil
# and the relation is counted on the master.
#
# NOR IS THIS THE WARD AND GUARDIAN PAIR AT `:2494`. That batch asks whether
# the man across the field raised the scoped man or was raised by him: one
# named pair of people, and nothing else. These ask how many boys of his own
# are trailing the scoped man about today, and never care who any of them is.
#
# AGOT OWNS THE RELATION AND AGOT SETS THE CEILING. `agot_squire` and its
# corresponding `agot_knight` are defined at
# `common/scripted_relations/00_agot_scripted_relations_knighting.txt:1`, and
# `agot_can_take_squire_trigger` refuses a man a new one at
# `num_of_relation_agot_squire < 2`
# (`common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_knighting_triggers.txt:115-124`). Two is
# therefore AGOT's own limit rather than a figure invented here. AGOT reads
# the same count again as `num_of_relation_agot_squire > 1` in a tournament
# event's weight at `events/agot_events/agot_knighthood_events.txt:1228`, and
# `has_relation_agot_squire` is live six times over in AGOT's own interaction
# files, from `common/character_interactions/00_agot_knight_interactions.txt`
# at `:431`.
#
# THE TWO ARE DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION. Two or more against exactly one, on
# the same count on the same man. No negation is needed and none is written.
#
# NO GUARDS. Both are bare character-scope counts on the scoped man. Neither
# names an optional link as a subject or as a target, so there is nothing here
# that needs one.
#
# RARITY. A man must be a knight before AGOT will hand him a squire at all,
# and the engine will not hand him a third. The first gate is that ceiling
# itself. The second is the single boy, which is the commoner of the two and
# still nothing like most men standing on a field.
bc_there_are_two_boys_of_his_out_here_trigger = {
	num_of_relation_agot_squire >= 2
}

bc_he_has_one_boy_of_his_own_trigger = {
	num_of_relation_agot_squire = 1
}

# BATCH 199 - THE OTHER WAR HE COULD HAVE HAD WITH THIS MAN.
#
# `has_any_cb_on` AND `has_cb_on` RETURNED ZERO ACROSS THE WHOLE MOD. Every
# mention of a casus belli in this repository is a comment citing some file in
# `common/casus_belli_types/` for an unrelated reason. The trigger itself has
# never been read here.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE CLAIM ROWS AT `:4849`. Those iterate the scoped man's own
# list and ask what is written in his name - one pressed, or three unpressed -
# without reference to anybody else on the field. `has_any_cb_on` is a question
# about two men: whether this one could walk out tomorrow and lawfully make war
# on that one. Neither nests inside the other in either direction. A man may
# hold nine claims, none of them on anything the man opposite holds, and fail
# these; a man with no claim whatever may still have a de jure or a faith-given
# cause against him and pass. Nor is it the war rows, which ask about the war
# he is already standing in - who declared it, how long it has run, whose it
# is. This asks about a war nobody has declared at all.
#
# LIVE IN BOTH TREES AND IN EXACTLY THIS FORM. Vanilla writes
# `has_any_cb_on = scope:recipient` and `has_any_cb_on = scope:actor` through
# `common/character_interactions/05_bp2_interactions.txt` from `:1069`, and
# negated at `common/character_interactions/03_fp2_interactions.txt:2632`, for
# thirty-five live rows in all. AGOT ships its own copies of both of those
# files and reads the key in a file of its own making besides, at
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_esr_threat_triggers.txt`.
#
# TWO MEN EACH, AND NOT THE SAME TWO. The first asks it of the man across the
# field. The second asks it of the man giving the orders on this side, which is
# the more uncomfortable of the two questions and the reason the pair is worth
# writing. Both may be true of one afternoon without either implying the other,
# and the pools roll separately in any case, as in batch 149's arrangement.
#
# GUARDS. Naming a link as the TARGET of a trigger is a read, so the first
# carries both far-side `exists` lines as siblings and repeats them inside the
# `trigger_if` limit, and the second carries its one.
#
# RARITY. A cause against one named man is a narrow thing - it is not enough to
# be owed something somewhere, the debt has to point at him - and the second
# gate additionally needs a knight who holds enough for the engine to give him
# a cause at all, which most men standing in a line do not.
bc_he_could_have_made_this_war_himself_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		has_any_cb_on = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_he_could_lawfully_fight_his_own_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		has_any_cb_on = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
}

# BATCH 200 - THE BOY WHO USED TO KNOCK HIM DOWN IN THE YARD.
#
# `has_relation_bully` AND `has_relation_victim` RETURNED ZERO ACROSS THE WHOLE
# MOD. The mod has read what these men are to each other from nearly every
# angle it can - blood, marriage, fostering, oath, wages, hooks, dread,
# opinion, alliance, homage - and never once read the oldest and least
# dignified of them, which is which of the two of them used to get hit.
#
# LIVE IN BOTH TREES AND IN THE CHARACTER-TARGET FORM. Vanilla writes both keys
# against `$CHARACTER$` through
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_relation_triggers.txt` from `:125`, negates
# `has_relation_victim = root` at
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_childhood_triggers.txt:78`, and reads
# `has_relation_bully = root` in
# `common/customizable_localization/00_character_descriptions.txt:877`, for
# eighteen vanilla files carrying `bully` and seventeen carrying `victim`. AGOT
# ships those files and writes the key in files of its own making besides, at
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_army_events.txt:386` and `:2376`,
# `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_feast_events.txt:257` and
# `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_ub_on_actions.txt:45`.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE FOSTERING ROWS AT `:2490`. A fostering is an arrangement two
# grown men made about a boy, and it is the same fact read from both ends. This
# is what happened inside somebody's arrangement while nobody was writing it
# down, between two boys who were not consulted, and it points at whichever of
# them was smaller that year rather than at whichever of them was owed
# something.
#
# NOR IS IT THE RIVAL, NEMESIS OR GRUDGE ROWS, AND THE ENGINE SAYS SO RATHER
# THAN THIS COMMENT. `common/scripted_relations/00_scripted_relations.txt:142`
# gives `bully` the line `corresponding = victim` and lists `rival`, `nemesis`,
# `grudge`, `friend` and `best_friend` among its `opposites`, so the man who is
# the scoped man's bully is by definition not also his rival, his nemesis or
# the man he holds a grudge against. A rivalry is a thing two adults arrived at
# together. This one was arrived at by one of them.
#
# EACH CARRIES THE NEGATION OF THE OTHER, in the fostering pair's arrangement
# rather than by argument. Ordinary play cannot make two men each other's
# bully, but the engine would answer both if some future content set both
# relations, and a gate that can be true beside its own opposite is not a gate.
#
# GUARDS. Naming a link as the TARGET of a trigger is a read, so both gates
# carry both far-side `exists` lines as siblings and repeat them inside the
# `trigger_if` limit.
#
# RARITY. The relation is handed out by childhood events between two boys of
# close age in one hall, most men never acquire it in either direction, and
# then both of them have to live thirty years, end up on opposite sides of one
# war, and one of them has to be giving the orders.
bc_the_boy_who_used_to_beat_him_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		has_relation_bully = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		NOT = {
			has_relation_victim = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

bc_the_boy_he_used_to_beat_commands_the_far_side_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		has_relation_victim = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		NOT = {
			has_relation_bully = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
	}
}

# BATCH 201 - THE OATH THAT HAD NO LORD IN IT.
#
# `has_relation_blood_brother` RETURNED ZERO ACROSS THE WHOLE MOD. The two
# hits on the word are a note in
# `common/character_memory_types/bc_memories.txt` at `:7` recording where a
# memory's shape was copied from, and a line at
# `:7055` in the relation-count batch's header observing that `blood_brother`
# carries `relation_aliases = { friend }`. The relation itself has never been
# read here.
#
# LIVE IN BOTH TREES, AND ONE OF THE LIVE ROWS IS IN THIS MOD'S OWN GENRE.
# Vanilla writes `has_relation_blood_brother = liege` inside a knight phase
# event, at `common/combat_phase_events/00_knight_phase_events.txt:1359`, as
# one arm of the test for which knights the severed-head content may pick -
# beside `is_acclaimed`, `is_close_family_of` and `is_powerful_vassal_of`,
# which is the company the engine itself thinks this relation keeps. It also
# writes it at `common/character_interactions/00_alliance.txt:399` and four
# times in `09_mpo_interactions.txt` from `:3043`, and reads it through
# `common/scripted_triggers/00_relation_triggers.txt`. AGOT ships twenty-two
# files carrying the key.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE KIN ROWS AT `:221`. `is_close_family_of` is blood and
# marriage. Blood-brotherhood is the arrangement men make precisely because
# they are not kin and cannot become kin, and the engine keeps it in an
# entirely separate list for that reason. A brother is an accident. This was
# a decision, taken once, by two people, with nobody's permission.
#
# NOR IS IT THE RIVAL, NEMESIS OR GRUDGE ROWS, NOR BATCH 200'S PAIR, AND THE
# ENGINE SAYS SO RATHER THAN THIS COMMENT.
# `common/scripted_relations/00_scripted_relations.txt:310` gives
# `blood_brother` the line `corresponding = blood_brother` and lists `rival`,
# `nemesis`, `grudge`, `bully`, `victim`, `potential_friend` and
# `potential_rival` among its `opposites`. Two men cannot be both.
#
# NOR IS IT THE RELATION COUNTS AT `:7086`, DESPITE THE ALIAS. `blood_brother`
# carries `relation_aliases = { friend }` at `:323` of the same file, so a
# sworn brother is one of the men `num_of_relation_friend >= 3` counts. That
# gate asks how many standing bonds a man holds anywhere in the world; this
# asks which man one particular bond points at, which is precisely the
# distinction that batch's own header draws at `:7045` in the other direction.
# Neither nests: a man may have sworn with exactly one man and fail the count,
# and a man with five friends may have opened a vein with nobody.
#
# SYMMETRIC, WHICH IS WHY THE PAIR IS TWO MEN AND NOT TWO DIRECTIONS.
# `corresponding = blood_brother` means there is no far end to read
# differently, so the fostering pair's construction is simply unavailable
# here. The pair asks one question of two different men instead - the man
# across the field, and the man giving the orders on this side - which is
# batch 149's arrangement, and the pools roll separately in any case.
#
# THE SECOND IS KNIGHT-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION, WHICH IS BATCH 141'S LESSON. A
# commander scoped into his own template IS `scope:combat_side.side_commander`,
# and no man is his own blood brother, so the gate would be a silently
# never-firing row in the commander pool. It is written into the knight file
# only.
#
# GUARDS. Naming a link as the TARGET of a trigger is a read, so the first
# carries both far-side `exists` lines as siblings and repeats them inside the
# `trigger_if` limit, and the second carries its one.
#
# RARITY. The relation is granted by a single interaction between two grown
# men who already like each other enough to bleed on it, most men never swear
# it at all, and then the oath has to outlive whatever put the two of them on
# a field together.
bc_the_far_side_is_commanded_by_his_blood_brother_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		has_relation_blood_brother = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	}
}

bc_he_swore_blood_with_the_man_giving_the_orders_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.side_commander }
		has_relation_blood_brother = scope:combat_side.side_commander
	}
}


# BATCH 202 - THE WAR THAT IS ALL ON ONE MAN'S LEDGER.
#
# CENSUS. `war_contribution` returns zero across the whole mod. The word
# "contribution" appears exactly once and it is prose, in the toast desc of
# `bc_what_the_paid_man_left_in_their_hands`, so nothing anywhere reads how
# much of a war a given man has actually paid for. The mod asks plenty about
# the war behind the combat already: how long it has run (`war_days >= 730` at
# :201), whether it is a civil war (:968), who leads it (`is_war_leader = root`
# at :995), which side of it this man is on (:2569 and :2577) and whether he
# has a second war at home (:8484). None of those read the war's own ledger of
# who has done the work.
#
# PRECEDENT. `war_contribution` is a war-scope trigger taking a target and a
# value: triggers.log gives the form as `war_contribution = { target = some
# character value > 5 }` with Supported Scopes: war. AGOT uses it in a file of
# its own authorship - `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_war_on_actions
# .txt` at :700, :721 and :744 - to pick out the three attackers with the most
# invested in a war that is about to be settled over their heads, and its bar
# for "this man has actually put something in" is `value >= 10`. Vanilla uses
# the same trigger at `common/scripted_effects/00_war_effects.txt:2532` with
# `value > 0`, at `common/scripted_effects/03_dlc_fp2_scripted_effects.txt
# :1259` against a stored debt, and at `common/scripted_effects/
# 07_dlc_ep3_scripted_effects.txt:11358`. Both gates below use `value >= 100`,
# which is ten times AGOT's own bar for a man who has put something in.
#
# THE WALK IS THE MOD'S ESTABLISHED ONE. `scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war`
# is how every war-scope gate in this file reaches the war, first at :198 and
# most recently at :8485, and `combat_war` is documented as Input Scopes:
# combat, Output Scopes: war.
#
# DISTINCTNESS. Nothing on this axis is shipped, so the only question is
# between the two gates. They are DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, which is the
# fostering pair's arrangement at :2490-2510: the first asks that root has
# carried this war and the far commander has not, the second asks that the far
# commander has carried it and root has not. No war can satisfy both. The
# subjects also differ, so this is batch 149's two-different-men arrangement as
# well, and the pools roll separately in any case.
#
# THE SECOND GATE IS WRITTEN FOR THE KNIGHT POOL and its own-man clause is
# nearly always true there, because a knight is not usually the man a war's
# ledger is written against. That clause is carried for disjointness rather
# than for rarity and it costs nothing to keep.
#
# GUARDS. `combat_war` is optional and so is `enemy_side.side_commander`, and
# naming a man as the TARGET of the contribution test is a read of him, so both
# gates carry `exists` for the war, for `enemy_side` and for
# `enemy_side.side_commander` as siblings and repeat all three in the
# `trigger_if` limit. A missing scope then fails the gate instead of passing it
# vacuously.
#
# RARITY. War contribution accrues to the men who win the battles and take the
# holdings, and in most wars that is one or two people out of a dozen
# participants. A hundred of it is a man who has fought the war more or less by
# himself. The first wants that man to be losing a battle, which is the shape
# of a war turning over on the one person holding it up; the second wants it
# true of the far commander while this side is winning, which is the same
# picture seen from the good end of the field.
bc_this_whole_war_is_on_his_own_ledger_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war = {
			war_contribution = {
				target = root
				value >= 100
			}
			NOT = {
				war_contribution = {
					target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
					value >= 100
				}
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_the_far_lord_has_carried_this_whole_war_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
	exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
	trigger_if = {
		limit = {
			exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side
			exists = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
		}
		scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war = {
			war_contribution = {
				target = scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander
				value >= 100
			}
			NOT = {
				war_contribution = {
					target = root
					value >= 100
				}
			}
		}
	}
}


# BATCH 203 - HOW MANY NAMES ARE ACTUALLY IN THIS WAR.
#
# CENSUS. `any_war_participant`, `every_war_participant`, `any_war_attacker`
# and `any_war_defender` all return zero across the whole mod. Batch 202 at
# :9264 opened the war's ledger of who has done the work; nothing anywhere
# counts how many people are on it. The war-scope gates this file already has
# ask how long the war has run (:201), whether it is a civil war (:968), who
# leads it (:995) and which side of it this man is on (:2569 and :2577), and
# every one of those is a question about one man or one war-wide flag rather
# than about the size of the guest list.
#
# PRECEDENT. `any_war_participant` is a war-scope list builder and AGOT uses it
# in files of its own authorship in at least six places, including
# `common/on_action/agot_on_actions/agot_war_on_actions.txt:345` and `:353`,
# `common/scripted_effects/00_agot_mega_wars_effects.txt:1304`, and
# `events/agot_events/agot_scenario_roberts_rebellion_minor_events.txt:1001`.
# The `count` form is live in vanilla on both the participant list and the
# attacker list: `events/activities/hold_court_activity/
# hold_court_events_general.txt:8223` writes `any_war_participant = { count = 0
# ... }` inside `vassal_war_conflict_trigger`, and
# `common/scripted_effects/09_dlc_mpo_scripted_effects.txt:4053` writes
# `any_war_attacker = { count >= 2 ... }`.
#
# THIS IS NOT THE BATTLE-SIZE AXIS AND IS NOT A SUBSET OF IT. `:185` asks
# `num_total_troops >= 15000` on the combat and the size bucket at :615 asks
# the same trigger at two thresholds; both count MEN STANDING ON THIS FIELD. A
# war participant is a name with a stake in the war, and the two numbers move
# independently in both directions: one great lord can put fifteen thousand men
# on a field by himself with nobody else named in the war at all, and eight
# petty lords can be named in a war and between them field a fraction of that.
# Neither gate below can be a subset or a superset of the size gates.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, which is the fostering pair's arrangement at
# :2490-2510. The first wants at least eight names in the war and the second
# wants at most two, so no war can satisfy both. Two is also the floor - a war
# has an attacker and a defender or it is not a war - so the second gate is
# exactly the war that nobody else would join.
#
# GUARDS. `combat_war` is optional, so both gates carry `exists` as a sibling
# and repeat it in the `trigger_if` limit, and a missing war fails the gate
# instead of passing it vacuously. The walk itself is the mod's established
# `scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war`, first used at :198.
#
# RARITY. Most wars are fought by a war leader, the man he is fighting, and the
# handful of allies and called vassals either of them can raise, which lands
# well short of eight names. Eight is a war that has stopped being a quarrel
# and started being everybody's business. At most two is the opposite and is
# rarer still, because a lord with allies calls them and a lord being attacked
# calls his: it means one man declared on another and not one other person in
# the world took a side. The first wants the crowded case while this side is
# losing, the second wants the empty case while this side is winning.
bc_this_war_has_a_crowd_of_names_in_it_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war }
		scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war = {
			any_war_participant = {
				count >= 8
			}
		}
	}
}

bc_this_war_has_only_two_names_in_it_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war }
		scope:combat_side.combat.combat_war = {
			any_war_participant = {
				count <= 2
			}
		}
	}
}


# BATCH 204 - THE GROUND WAS ALREADY SOMEBODY'S PROBLEM.
#
# CENSUS. `is_occupied` and `is_raided` both return zero across the whole mod,
# and so do `is_county_capital`, `has_ongoing_construction`, `num_buildings`
# and `available_loot`. The mod reads this province from several angles
# already - the six terrain bands from :117, `fort_level` at :5646 and :5656,
# `has_holding_type` at :5874 and :5884, `has_special_building` at :6021 and
# :6031, and through the county its development at :1362 and its holder at
# :1091 and :2154 - and every one of those asks what the ground IS or WHO
# HOLDS it. Not one of them asks what has been HAPPENING to it.
#
# IT DOES NOT BELONG IN bc_ground_triggers.txt. That file holds the six terrain
# bands and nothing else, and validate counts every `agot_is_*_terrain` mention
# in it against the 23 base helpers; anything added there breaks the partition
# check. Neither gate below mentions a terrain, and both live here with the
# rest of the province wrappers.
#
# PRECEDENT, AND AGOT PAIRS THESE TWO ITSELF. `common/decisions/agot_decisions/
# 00_agot_colonization_decisions.txt:30` and `:31` are consecutive lines inside
# one `root.location = { }` block reading `is_occupied = no` and `is_raided =
# no` together, in a file of AGOT's own making, to decide whether a man can go
# and look at his own settlement. AGOT reads `is_occupied = yes` again on a
# saved province scope at `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_heir_events.txt
# :7488`, `:8608` and `:8942`. Vanilla has both as well: `is_occupied = no` at
# `common/decisions/dlc_decisions/ep3_decisions.txt:322` and across
# `common/scripted_effects/tgp_mandala_scripted_effects.txt` from `:3002`, and
# `is_raided = yes` throughout `events/dlc/mpo/mpo_migration_events.txt` from
# `:1490`. triggers.log gives both as yes/no with Supported Scopes: province.
#
# DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION, the fostering pair's arrangement at :2490-2510: a
# province can be occupied and raided at the same time, so each gate carries
# the negation of the other and the overlap belongs to neither. What is left is
# two different kinds of trouble rather than two readings of one.
#
# NOT THE WAR-SCALE ROWS EITHER. Batch 203 at :9366 counts the names in the
# war and batch 202 at :9264 asks who has paid for it; both are properties of
# the war. This is a property of one province, and a huge war can be fought
# across untouched ground while a two-name war burns a county to the roots.
#
# GUARDS. `combat.location` does not always resolve - bc_context_effects.txt
# records the `BC: ground missing` case - so both gates carry `exists =
# scope:combat_side.combat.location` as a sibling and repeat it in the
# `trigger_if` limit, exactly as the six band wrappers do from :117.
#
# RARITY. Occupation needs somebody to have taken the holding and still be
# sitting on it, which is a minority of provinces even in wartime and a small
# minority of the ones a field battle happens in, since armies that have just
# taken a place tend to move on. An active raid is rarer still and does not
# last long. The first wants the ground already lost while this side is losing
# the battle for it; the second wants the countryside being stripped in the
# same province while this side is winning, which is a victory with nothing
# underneath it.
bc_this_ground_is_already_in_other_hands_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
	trigger_if = {
		limit = { exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location }
		scope:combat_side.combat.location = {
			is_occupied = yes
			is_raided = no
		}
	}
}

bc_this_ground_is_being_stripped_while_they_fight_trigger = {
	exists = scope:combat_side.combat.location
